\displaystyle\left|{\omega}^{c}_{t}(z)-\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1-\sqrt{1-2s_{0}}}{s_{0}}\left\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\Phi\right\|_{\infty}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

where we used the conclusion in the Newton-Kantorvich theorem that
x_{*}\in B[x_{0},t_{*}]
.
Finally, using Lemma
3.5
, we note that
\Im\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)=\Im\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)-\Im{\omega}^{c}_{t}(z)+\Im{\omega}^{c}_{t}(z)\geq\Im z
with high probability, when
\Im z
is sufficiently large. It further follows that

\Gamma_{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{k}}(\hat{\omega}_{1}(z),\cdots,\hat{\omega}_{k}(z))\neq 0
for all
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
when
\Im z
is sufficiently large. Thus
\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)
is analytic when
\Im z
is sufficiently large, since
F_{\mu_{t}}(\hat{\omega}(z))
is. By the definition of
F_{\mu_{t}}
, we have

(63)

\displaystyle F_{\mu_{t}}(\hat{\omega}_{t}(z))=\frac{-1}{m_{\mu_{t}}(\hat{\omega}_{t}(z))}=\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)-y_{t}+\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}{1-y_{t}-\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)},\quad t\in[\![k]\!].

Plugging (
63
) into (
61
) we have

((k-1)\mathrm{I}-\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime})\cdot\hat{x}=\hat{b},

where

\displaystyle\hat{x}:=\bigg(\frac{\hat{\omega}_{1}(z)}{z},\cdots,\frac{\hat{\omega}_{k}(z)}{z}\bigg)^{\prime},

\displaystyle\hat{b}:=\bigg(-1+\frac{(k-1)y_{1}}{z}-\frac{(k-1)y_{1}(1-y_{1})}{(1-y_{1}-\hat{\omega}_{1}(z))z},\cdots,-1+\frac{(k-1)y_{k}}{z}-\frac{(k-1)y_{k}(1-y_{k})}{(1-y_{k}-\hat{\omega}_{k}(z))z}\bigg)^{\prime}.

Solving the above linear system by inverting
((k-1)\mathrm{I}-\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime})
, we have

\hat{x}=(k-1)^{-1}(\mathrm{I}-\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime})\cdot\hat{b},

which gives

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)}{z}=

\displaystyle 1+\frac{y_{t}}{z}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{y_{i}}{z}-\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}{(1-y_{t}-\hat{\omega}_{t}(z))z}-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}{(1-y_{t}-\hat{\omega}_{t}(z))z}=1+O(|\Im z|^{-1}),

as
\Im z\to\infty
. Therefore, we can obtain

(64)

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\to\infty}\frac{\hat{\omega}_{t}(\mathrm{i}\eta)}{\mathrm{i}\eta}=1.

Thus by the uniqueness claim in Proposition
1.5
,
\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)
agrees with
\omega_{t}(z)
when
\Im z
is sufficiently large. Therefore, (
62
) implies that

(65)

\displaystyle\left|{\omega}^{c}_{t}(z)-{\omega}_{t}(z)\right|\prec\frac{1}{N},

for all
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
with
\Im z
being sufficiently large. Subtracting (
25
) from (
11
), we have

(66)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z)-\omega_{t}(z))=-\frac{k-1}{m_{N}(z)}+\frac{k-1}{m_{\boxplus}(z)}=(k-1)\frac{m_{N}(z)-m_{\boxplus}(z)}{m_{N}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}.

As a result,

(67)

\displaystyle\left|m_{N}(z)-m_{\boxplus}(z)\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}|\omega_{t}^{c}(z)-\omega_{t}(z)|\right)=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),

for all
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
with
\Im z
being sufficiently large.

Next, taking (
67
) as an input, we can use the continuity argument to obtain the bound for each fixed
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
.

\bullet

Any fixed
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
.

Let
z_{0}\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
has sufficiently large imaginary part so that (
65
) and (
67
) hold at
z_{0}
. Then, let
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
be any fixed point. We can find a sequence of number
z_{i}\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+},i=1,\ldots,N^{2}
, so that
z=z_{N^{2}}
and
|z_{i}-z_{i-1}|=O(N^{-2})
for all
i=1,\ldots,N^{2}
.
Our strategy is to extend the estimates in (
65
) and (
67
) from
z_{0}
to
z
via the intermediate points
z_{i}
’s step by step, using a continuity argument. In the sequence, we will only show the detailed argument for the first step from
z_{0}
to
z_{1}
. The remaining steps are the same.

We first consider the bound of
\omega^{\prime}_{t}(z)
. Similar as we did in (
59
), with
\omega_{t}^{c}
replaced by
\omega_{t}
, together with Lemma
4.5
, we can get

(68)

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\|_{(\infty,\infty)}\lesssim 1.

Differentiating the equation (
35
) w.r.t.
z
, we get

(69)

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}\Phi\cdot\omega^{\prime}(z)=\mathds{1},

where
\omega^{\prime}(z):=(\omega_{1}^{\prime}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}^{\prime}(z))
. Together with (
68
), we get

(70)

\displaystyle|\omega_{t}^{\prime}(z)|\lesssim 1,t\in[\![k]\!]

by inverting (
69
).
Recall the definition in (
24
), we have

\displaystyle(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))^{\prime}=\frac{{\rm tr}G(z){\rm tr}P_{t}G^{2}(z)-({\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)-1){\rm tr}G^{2}(z)}{({\rm tr}G(z))^{2}}.

By Lemma
4.2
, we can obtain

|(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))^{\prime}|\lesssim 1

with high probability.

Therefore, from (
65
) for
z_{0}
and the continuity of
\omega_{t}^{c}
and
\omega_{t}
, we have

(71)

\displaystyle\left|{\omega}^{c}_{t}(z_{1})-{\omega}_{t}(z_{1})\right|\prec\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}.

More precisely, by the definition of the stochastic domination, (
71
) implies that for any large
D>0
and small
\epsilon>0
, there exists an high probability event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\equiv\mathcal{E}(z_{1},\epsilon,D)
, satisfying
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c})<N^{-D}
, such that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we have

(72)

\displaystyle|\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{1})|\leq N^{\epsilon}(N^{-1}+N^{-2})\leq 2N^{-1+\epsilon},

for any small
\epsilon>0
.

From (
44
) we recall the perturbed system at
z_{1}

\displaystyle(k-1)F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))-\omega_{1}^{c}(z_{1})-\cdots-\omega_{k}^{c}(z_{1})+z_{1}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\gamma}_{t1}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{t2}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{t3}(z_{1})+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{\tilde{\gamma}^{2}_{t1}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}^{2}_{t2}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}^{2}_{t3}(z_{1})}{(k-1)F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))}\right),

with the definitions in (
45
).
Using (
71
), Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
4.5
, one can easily check that

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\gamma}^{2}_{t1}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}^{2}_{t2}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}^{2}_{t3}(z_{1})}{(k-1)F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))}\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N^{2}}.

Therefore, let
\Omega_{t}(z_{1}):=\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{1})
, and apply expansion
F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))
around
\omega_{t}(z_{1})
with (
25
). We get

\displaystyle(k-1)F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))\Omega_{1}(z_{1})-\Omega_{1}(z_{1})-\cdots-\Omega_{k}(z_{1})

(73)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\gamma}_{t1}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{t2}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{t3}(z_{1})-(k-1)\sum_{n\geq 2}\frac{1}{n!}F_{\mu_{t}}^{(n)}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))\Omega_{t}^{n}(z_{1})+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{2}}\right).

Denoted by

\tilde{\gamma}_{a}(z_{1}):=(\tilde{\gamma}_{1a}(z_{1}),\cdots,\tilde{\gamma}_{ka}(z_{1}))^{\prime},
for
a=1,2,3
.
Writing the system (
73
) (in
t\in[\![k]\!]
) in terms of
\mathrm{D}\Phi
in (
4
) and inverting
\mathrm{D}\Phi
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Omega(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\left(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{2}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{3}(z_{1})\right)\|_{\infty}

(74)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n\geq 2}\left\|\frac{k-1}{n!}(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1})\right\|_{\infty}+O_{\prec}\left(\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\|_{(\infty,\infty)}\cdot\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right),

where

\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1}):=\left(F_{\mu_{1}}^{(n)}(\omega_{1}(z_{1}))\Omega_{1}^{n}(z_{1}),\cdots,F_{\mu_{k}}^{(n)}(\omega_{k}(z_{1}))\Omega_{k}^{n}(z_{1})\right)^{\prime}.

Using (
68
), (
74
) can be written as

\displaystyle\|\Omega(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\left(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{2}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{3}(z_{1})\right)\|_{\infty}

(75)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n\geq 2}\left\|\frac{k-1}{n!}(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1})\right\|_{\infty}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right)

First, we consider the high order terms in (
75
). Recall (
51
) and let
\mathcal{D}(\omega):=\mathcal{D}(\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{k})
, We have

\displaystyle\frac{k-1}{n!}(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1})=\frac{k-1}{n!}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1})+\frac{k-1}{n!}\frac{\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)}{1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}}\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1}).

Taking
\ell^{\infty}
-norm on both sides, by triangular inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{k-1}{n!}(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1})\right\|_{\infty}\leq\left\|\frac{k-1}{n!}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1})\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\frac{k-1}{n!}\frac{\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)}{1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}}\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1})\right\|_{\infty}.

Notice that for
n\geq 2

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{k-1}{n!}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1})\right\|_{\infty}=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n!}\left(\frac{F_{\mu_{1}}^{(n)}(\omega_{1}(z_{1}))}{F_{\mu_{1}}^{\prime}(\omega_{1}(z_{1}))}\Omega_{1}^{n}(z_{1}),\cdots,\frac{F_{\mu_{k}}^{(n)}(\omega_{k}(z_{1}))}{F_{\mu_{k}}^{\prime}(\omega_{k}(z_{1}))}\Omega_{k}^{n}(z_{1})\right)^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}

(76)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq t\leq k}\frac{y_{t}}{|F_{\mu_{t}}^{\prime}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))|}|\Omega_{t}(z_{1})|^{n}\lesssim\max_{1\leq t\leq k}y_{t}|\Omega_{t}(z_{1})|^{n},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{k-1}{n!}\frac{\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)}{1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}}\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}(z_{1})\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{k-1}{n!(1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1})}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{F_{\mu_{t}}^{(n)}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))\Omega_{t}^{n}(z_{1})}{\mathcal{D}_{1}(\omega_{1})\mathcal{D}_{t}(\omega_{i})},\cdots,\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{F_{\mu_{t}}^{(n)}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))\Omega_{t}^{n}(z_{1})}{\mathcal{D}_{k}(\omega_{k})\mathcal{D}_{t}(\omega_{t})}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

(77)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(k-1)(1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1})}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{y_{t}|\Omega_{t}(z_{1})|^{n}}{|F_{\mu_{t}}^{\prime}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))|}\cdot\max_{1\leq s\leq k}\frac{1}{|F_{\mu_{s}}^{\prime}(\omega_{s}(z_{1}))|}\lesssim\max_{1\leq t\leq k}|\Omega_{t}(z_{1})|^{n},

where we used Lemma
4.5
.
Plugging (
76
) and (
77
) into (
75
), we have

\displaystyle\|\Omega(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\left(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{2}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{3}(z_{1})\right)\|_{\infty}+\sum_{n\geq 2}\|\Omega(z_{1})\|^{n}_{\infty}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right).

Which means that there exists an high probability event
\mathcal{E}_{2}=\mathcal{E}_{2}(z_{1},\epsilon,D)
, satisfying
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{c})<N^{-D}
, such that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\|\Omega(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\left(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{2}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{3}(z_{1})\right)\|_{\infty}+\sum_{n\geq 2}\|\Omega(z_{1})\|^{n}_{\infty}+O\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{2-\epsilon}}\right).

for any small
\epsilon>0
. Since
\|\Omega(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\leq 2N^{-1+\epsilon}
on
\mathcal{E}_{1}
in light of (
72
), we can absorb the quadratic term into the LHS, which gives

\displaystyle\|\Omega(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\left(\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{2}(z_{1})+\tilde{\gamma}_{3}(z_{1})\right)\|_{\infty}+O\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{2-\epsilon}}\right)

(78)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})\|_{\infty}+\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{2}(z_{1})\|_{\infty}+\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{3}(z_{1})\|_{\infty}+O\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{2-\epsilon}}\right),

on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
.
For the first term in the RHS, we have

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})\|_{\infty}+\left\|\frac{\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)}{1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})\right\|_{\infty}

(79)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{s}\Big|\mathcal{D}_{i}^{-1}(\omega_{s})\tilde{\gamma}_{s1}\Big|+\max_{s}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{D}_{s}^{-1}(\omega_{s})}{1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{D}_{t}^{-1}(\omega_{t})\tilde{\gamma}_{t1}(z_{1})\Big|.

For each
t\in[\![k]\!]
,

(80)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{t}^{-1}(\omega_{t})\tilde{\gamma}_{t1}(z_{1})=\frac{F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))}{F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))}\cdot\frac{1-y_{t}}{\theta_{t}}\gamma_{t1}(z_{1}).

By the continuity of function
F_{\mu_{t}}
, we have on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}

(81)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))}{F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))}\cdot\frac{1-y_{t}}{\theta_{t}}\Big|\lesssim\Big|\frac{F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))}{\theta_{t}}\Big|=\Big|\frac{F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})\omega_{t}(z_{1})(1-\omega_{t}(z_{1}))}\Big|+O\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-\epsilon}}\right),

where the last equality comes from (
71
).
Further note

(82)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})\omega_{t}(z_{1})(1-\omega_{t}(z_{1}))}\Big|\lesssim 1.

Combining (
80
), (
81
), (
82
), (
40
)
with Lemma
4.4
, we can obtain

\left|\mathcal{D}_{t}^{-1}\tilde{\gamma}_{t1}\right|\prec\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}.

This completes the estimate for the first term in the RHS of (
79
). For the second term in (
79
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\mathcal{D}_{s}^{-1}(\omega_{s})}{1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega)\mathds{1}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{D}_{t}^{-1}(\omega_{t})\tilde{\gamma}_{t1}(z_{1})\bigg|\lesssim\bigg|\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))}{F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))}\cdot\frac{1-y_{t}}{\theta_{t}}\gamma_{t1}(z_{1})\bigg|\prec\frac{1}{N},

where in the first step we used Lemma
4.5
, and in the last step we used Lemma
4.4
.

As a result,

\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{1}(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\prec N^{-1}.

Similarly, we can obtain,

\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{2}(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\prec N^{-1}
,
\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{3}(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\prec N^{-1}.

Again by the definition of the stochastic domination, we have there exists an high probability event
\mathcal{E}_{3}\equiv\mathcal{E}(z_{1},\epsilon,D)
, satisfying
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{3}^{c})<N^{-D}
, such that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}
,

(83)

\displaystyle\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{a}(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\leq N^{1-\epsilon},\quad a=1,2,3,

for any small
\epsilon>0
.
Therefore, plugging (
83
) into (
78
), we have event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}

(84)

\displaystyle\max_{t}|\omega_{t}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1})|=\|\Omega(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\leq CN^{1-\epsilon},

Repeating the above procedure for
O(N^{2})
steps, we will generate a series of events, i.e.,
\mathcal{E}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{E}_{CN^{2}}
. Therefore, we have on the event
\bigcap_{i=1}^{O(N^{2})}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, (
84
) still holds. Since all these events are high probability events, we obtain

(85)

\displaystyle\max_{t}|\omega_{t}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1})|\prec{\frac{1}{N}}.

Using (
67
), we can again have

(86)

\displaystyle|m_{N}(z_{1})-m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})|\prec\frac{1}{N}.

Repeating the above procedure we can prove (
29
) and (
30
) for all
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
.

∎

5.
Proof of Theorem
1.11

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.11
. To present the results, we define the following two point function. For any fixed
z_{1},z_{2}\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\text{supp}(\mu_{\boxplus})
, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{1},z_{2}):=\left(1+\frac{(\omega_{t}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{2})m_{\boxplus}(z_{2}))^{2}}{(z_{1}-z_{2})(m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-m_{\boxplus}(z_{2}))}+\frac{\omega_{t}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{2})}{\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})}}\right).

Further, if
z_{1}=z_{2}
, we denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{1},z_{1}):=\frac{(1+\omega_{t}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1}))(m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-1-\omega_{t}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{1})(\omega_{t}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1}))^{\prime})}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-1-2m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})\omega_{t}(z_{1})}.

Based on Proposition
4.3
, we have the following approximations for tracial quantities and the entries of matrices involving Green functions. The proof is given in Section
C
of Appendix.

Proposition 5.1
.

Under Assumptions
1.6
and
1.7
, if
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any
t\in[\![k]\!]
and for any fixed
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(87)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z)-\left(1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)\right)\right|,\;\left|{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z)-\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}\right|\prec\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}},

(88)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z){P}_{t}{G}(z)-\mathcal{L}_{t}(z,z)\right|,\;\left|{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z){P}_{t}{G}(z)-\frac{\mathcal{L}_{t}(z,z)}{1-y_{t}}\right|\prec\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}.

The same bounds hold for
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

In addition, for any
t\in[\![k]\!]
and for any fixed
z_{1}\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}
and
z_{2}\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(89)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{1},z_{2})\right|,\;\left|{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-\frac{\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{1},z_{2})}{1-y_{t}}\right|\prec\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}.

The same bounds hold for
z_{1}\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}
and
z_{2}\in(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

In addition to the concentration results for the tracial quantities, we also have the following concentration results for the diagonal entries of the involved random matrices.

Proposition 5.2
.

Under Assumptions
1.6
and
1.7
, if
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any fixed
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(90)

\displaystyle\left|[G(z)]_{ii}-m_{\boxplus}(z)\right|\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},

and for any
t\in[\![k]\!]
,

(91)

\displaystyle\left|[{P}_{t}{G}(z)]_{ii}-\left(1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)\right)\right|,\;\left|[{P}_{t}{G}(z){P}_{t}]_{ii}-\left(1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)\right)\right|\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},

(92)

\displaystyle\left|[(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{jj}-\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}\right|,\;\left|[{W}_{t}{G}(z){W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}-\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}\right|\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}.

The same bounds hold for
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

The proof of Proposition
5.2
is also given in Section
C
of Appendix.

The above bounds can be easily extended to the bound for the derivatives w.r.t.
z
using Cauchy integral. For instance, choosing sufficiently small (but of constant order) contour
\gamma
centred at
z
, we have

(93)

\displaystyle|\partial_{z}{\rm tr}G(z)-\partial_{z}m_{\boxplus}(z)|=\left|\frac{1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\oint_{\gamma}\frac{{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})-m_{\boxplus}(\tilde{z})}{(\tilde{z}-z)^{2}}{\rm d}\tilde{z}\right|\prec\frac{1}{N}.

Here for the last step in (
93
) we need a bound of
{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})-m_{\boxplus}(\tilde{z})
for all
\tilde{z}\in\gamma
. A desired bound for those
\tilde{z}
with
|\Im\tilde{z}|\geq N^{-K}
can be easily obtained from Propsition
4.3
by slightly modifying the contours
\gamma_{a}^{0}
’s to its neighborhood, and for those
\tilde{z}
with
|\Im\tilde{z}|<N^{-K}
one can use Lemma
4.2
and the fact
m_{\boxplus}(\tilde{z})\sim 1
directly.
Similar arguments can be applied to the derivatives of all quantities in (
87
)-(
92
), and the same bounds hold for any fixed order derivatives. Hence, in the sequel, we will often apply the error bounds obtained in the estimates of the Green function functionals to their derivatives directly without further explanation.

Based on Proposition
4.3
,
5.1
, and
5.2
, we can now prove Theorem
1.11
. Our proof basically consists of two parts. In the first part, based on the method of characteristic function, we prove the asymptotic normality of the LSS, and the corresponding mean and variance can be expressed in terms of the expectation of the Green function and its functionals. In the second part, we show that, the expectation of the Green function and its functionals can be well approximated by functionals of
m_{{\boxplus}}
and
\omega_{t},t\in[\![k]\!]
.
The conclusion for the first part can be summarized as the following theorem. Recall
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}
defined in (
22
).

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
and f be analytic inside
\gamma_{1}^{0}
and
\gamma_{2}^{0}
.
Denote by

(94)

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}(z)={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z)],\qquad\beta_{t}(z)=\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}G(z)-{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)],

for all
t\in[\![k]\!]
. Under Assumptions
1.6
and
1.7
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm Tr}f(H)-\oint_{\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}G(z)]f(z){\rm d}z}{\sigma_{f}}\Rightarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1)

with

(95)

\displaystyle\sigma_{f}^{2}=-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{2}^{0}}\mathcal{K}(z_{1},z_{2})f(z_{1})f(z_{2}){\rm d}z_{2}{\rm d}z_{1},

and

(96)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(z_{1},z_{2})=

\displaystyle\left(z_{1}-\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{\alpha_{s}(z_{1})}{1+\alpha_{s}(z_{1})+\beta_{s}(z_{1})}\right)^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Bigg[\partial_{z_{2}}\frac{{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){G}(z_{2})}{1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})}\Bigg].

The same result holds if
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
with
\gamma_{1}^{0}
and
\gamma_{2}^{0}
replaced by
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
, respectively.

Proof of Theorem
5.3
.

We only prove the case when
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
with contours
\gamma^{0}_{1}
and
\gamma^{0}_{2}
, and the proof of the case
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
with contours
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
is similar.
First, from the proof of Lemma
4.1
in Appendix, we can actually conclude that the nonzero eigenvalues of
H
are bounded below and above by positive constants with high probability. Then by Cauchy’s formula, by choosing our contour
\gamma_{a}^{0}
properly to enclose all eigenvalues (with high probability), we have

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}f(H)=\frac{-1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\oint_{\gamma^{0}_{a}}{\rm Tr}G(z)f(z){\rm d}z=\frac{-1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{a}}{\rm Tr}G(z)f(z){\rm d}z+O_{\prec}(N^{-K+1}),\quad a=1,2.

Recall the definition in (
23
), we have

(97)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}f(H)=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{a}}{\rm Tr}G(z)f(z){\rm d}z\cdot\Xi+O_{\prec}(N^{-K+1})=:L_{N}^{a}(f)+O_{\prec}(N^{-K+1}),\quad a=1,2

for some large (but fixed)
K
. Therefore, to prove the asymptotic normality of
{\rm Tr}f(H)
, it suffices to prove the asymptotic normality of
L_{N}^{1}(f)
. An advantage of
L_{N}^{1}(f)
over
{\rm Tr}f(H)
is that the Green function
G(z)
in the integrand of
L_{N}^{1}(f)
and the derivatives of various functionals of
G(z)
w.r.t. matrix entries always possess deterministic crude bound. It is crucial for us to transform high probability estimates into the expectation estimates in the following calculations, in the spirit of Lemma
3.3
(ii). Let

\phi_{f}^{N}(x)={\mathbb{E}}\big[{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big]

be the characteristic function of
\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle
. Taking derivative w.r.t.
x
, we have

(98)

\displaystyle(\phi_{f}^{N}(x))^{\prime}=\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]=\frac{-1}{2\pi}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm Tr}G(z_{1})\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]f(z_{1}){\rm d}z_{1},

Our aim is to derive an approximate ODE for
\phi_{f}^{N}(x)
, from which we can solve
\phi_{f}^{N}(x)
. Then our task boils down to calculating the term,

{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\big[\big\langle{\rm Tr}G(z_{1})\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big]
,
z_{1}\in\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}.

By definition, we have the trivial identity

\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\rm Tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})={\rm Tr}HG(z_{1})=N+z_{1}{\rm Tr}G(z_{1}).

Then we can write

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm Tr}G(z_{1})\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]=\frac{1}{z_{1}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm Tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big].

By the cumulant expansion (c.f., Lemma
A.1
in Section
A
of Appendix),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}{X}_{t,ij}[{W}_{t}{G}(z_{1})]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]=\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}(\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}(z_{1})]_{ji})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}xL_{N}^{1}(f)}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[{W}_{t}{G}(z_{1})]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\sum_{a=0}^{2}{2\choose a}\frac{\kappa_{3}^{t,j}}{2{N}^{3/2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[(\partial^{a}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}(z_{1})]_{ji})\partial^{2-a}_{t,ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\sum_{a=0}^{3}{3\choose a}\frac{\kappa^{t,j}_{4}}{6{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[(\partial^{a}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}(z_{1})]_{ji})\partial^{3-a}_{t,ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\geq 1}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}+s_{1}=s\\
s_{1}\geq 1\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{0}}\left\{[W_{t}G(z_{1})]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\right\}\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{1}}\Xi\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{s\geq 4}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{ij}^{(t)}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\partial^{s}_{t,ji}\left\{[W_{t}G(z_{1})]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\right\}\Big]+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathcal{R}_{l+1}^{t,ji}\Big]

(99)

\displaystyle\quad=:\mathsf{I}_{t1}+\mathsf{I}_{t2}+\mathsf{I}_{t3}+\mathsf{I}_{t4}+\mathsf{E}_{1}+\mathsf{E}_{2}+\mathsf{E}_{3},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l+1}^{t,ji}=

\displaystyle O(1)\cdot\sup_{X_{t,ji}\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G(z_{1})]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Xi\right\}\right|\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{t,ji}|^{l+2}\mathbf{1}_{|X_{t,ji}|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}\Big]

(100)

\displaystyle+O(1)\cdot\mathbb{E}\big[|X_{t,ji}|^{l+2}\big]\cdot\sup_{|X_{t,ji}|\leqslant N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Xi\right\}\right|.

Here we also used
\kappa_{a}^{t,j},a=3,4
to denote the
a
-th cumulant of
X_{t,ji}
’s. Note that since
i
is the index of i.i.d. samples, according to our assumption the cumulant depends on
j
only but not
i
. The following lemma gives the estimates of each term in (
99
).

Lemma 5.4
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t1}=\alpha_{t}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]-(\alpha_{t}+\beta_{t}){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t2}=\frac{x}{\pi}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\partial_{z_{2}}\Big[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){G}(z_{2})\Big]f(z_{2}){\rm d}z_{2}{\mathbb{E}}\big[{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big]+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t3}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right),\;\mathsf{I}_{t4}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right),\;\mathsf{E}_{1}+\mathsf{E}_{2}+\mathsf{E}_{3}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

The proof of Lemma
5.4
is given in Section
C
of Appendix.

First plugging the estimates of
\mathsf{I}_{t1}
,
\mathsf{I}_{t3}
and
\mathsf{I}_{t4}
in (
99
), we arrive at

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[{\rm Tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\bigg]=\alpha_{t}(z_{1}){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad-(\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]+\mathsf{I}_{t2}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

Moving the second term in the RHS to the LHS, and then dividing both sides by
(1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1}))
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{t}(z_{1}){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]}{1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})}+\frac{\mathsf{I}_{t2}}{1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

Here we actually used the fact that
|1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})|
is bounded below (implied by Lemma
5.5
below).
Summing over
t
and using the trivial identity
\sum_{t}P_{t}G=zG+I
, we arrive at an equation for
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\big[{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\big]
. Solving the equation gives

(101)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]=\left(z_{1}-\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{\alpha_{s}(z_{1})}{1+\alpha_{s}(z_{1})+\beta_{s}(z_{1})}\right)^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\mathsf{I}_{t2}}{1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right).

Substituting this result together with the estimate of
\mathsf{I}_{t2}
back to (
98
), we have

\displaystyle(\phi_{f}^{N}(x))^{\prime}=-x\sigma^{2}_{f}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)=-x\sigma^{2}_{f}\phi_{f}^{N}(x)+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right),

where
\sigma_{f}
is given in (
95
).
This proves the asymptotic normality of
\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle
, which further implies the normality of
\text{Tr}f(H)
.
∎

From Theorem
5.3
to Theorem
1.11
, one needs to approximate the tracial quantities by functions of
m_{\boxplus}
and
\omega_{t}
’s. To this end, we first provide the following lemma, whose proof is postponed to Section
C
in Appendix.

Lemma 5.5
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.11
, if
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any fixed
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(102)

\displaystyle 1+\alpha_{t}(z)+\beta_{t}(z)=\frac{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),

and

(103)

\displaystyle\left|m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)\right|>0.

The same bounds hold for
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

Now, we are ready to prove Theorem
1.11
.

Proof of Theorem
1.11
.

By the estimates in Propositions
4.3
,
5.1
, and Lemma
5.5
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{t}(z)}{1+\alpha_{t}(z)+\beta_{t}(z)}=\frac{y_{t}-1-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{2}}\right).

Here we also used the fact that
\alpha_{t}(z)
is of order
O_{\prec}\left(p_{t}/N\right)
.
This implies

\displaystyle\left(z_{1}-\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{\alpha_{s}(z_{1})}{1+\alpha_{s}(z_{1})+\beta_{s}(z_{1})}\right)^{-1}=\left(z_{1}-\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{y_{s}-1-\omega_{s}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-1-2\omega_{s}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}\right)^{-1}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

After some elementary algebraic operation (c.f., Section
F.1
in Appendix) , by using (
10
), (
11
) and (
12
), we can get

(104)

\displaystyle\left(z_{1}-\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{y_{s}-1-\omega_{s}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-1-2\omega_{s}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}\right)^{-1}=-\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}

which is bounded. This gives the leading factor of
\mathcal{K}(z_{1},z_{2})
. Applying Proposition
5.1
and Lemma
5.5
, we conclude

(105)

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[\partial_{z_{2}}\left({\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})\right)]}{1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})}=\frac{\partial_{z_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{1},z_{2})}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-1-2\omega_{t}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Here we used the fact that
\{m_{\boxplus}(z):z\in\gamma_{1}^{0}\}
and
\{m_{\boxplus}(z):z\in\gamma_{2}^{0}\}
are well separated (c.f., Section
J
in Appendix).

Similarly, we have by Proposition
5.1
with
G(z_{1})G(z_{2})=(G(z_{1})-G(z_{2}))/(z_{1}-z_{2})

(106)

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[\partial_{z_{2}}\left({\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})G(z_{2})\right)]}{1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})}=\frac{\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\frac{\omega_{t}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{2})m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-1-2\omega_{t}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Plugging (
104
), (
105
) and (
106
) into (
96
), we can get

(107)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(z_{1},z_{2})=

\displaystyle-\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(\frac{\partial_{z_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{t}(z_{1},z_{2})-\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\frac{\omega_{t}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{2})m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)}{m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-1-2\omega_{t}(z_{1})m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})}\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

Further simplification (c.f., Section
F.1
in Appendix) leads to

(108)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(z_{1},z_{2})=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega^{\prime}_{t}(z_{1})\omega^{\prime}_{t}(z_{2})}{(\omega_{t}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{2}))^{2}}-\frac{(k-1)m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}(z_{1})m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}(z_{2})}{(m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-m_{\boxplus}(z_{2}))^{2}}-\frac{1}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

Adding the parts that
|\Im z|\leq N^{-K}
of the contours back to the integral of the main term in the RHS of (
108
) (with negligible error) gives the variance in Theorem
1.11
.

For the expectation in Theorem
1.11
, we can estimate it as follows. Let

(109)

\displaystyle E_{t}(z):={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}G(z)]-N(1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)),\quad E(z):={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}G(z)]-Nm_{\boxplus}(z).

By Propositions
4.3
and
5.1
, we immediately obtain
E(z)=O_{\prec}\left(1\right)
and

E_{t}(z)=O_{\prec}(\sqrt{p_{t}/N})
.
Our task is to give an explicit expression for
E(z)
up to the first order. In the following estimation, since only one parameter
z
is involved, we use the shorthand notation
G:=G(z)
for brevity.

We start from the following lemma which is essentially the cumulant expansion of
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}G]
with some error estimates.

Lemma 5.6
.

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}G]=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}]{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}{G}-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}]

(110)

\displaystyle+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}^{2}\left([{I}-{P}_{t}]_{ii}[({P}_{t}-{I}){G}]_{ii}-[({I}-{P}_{t}){G}]_{ii}^{2}\right)\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

The proof of Lemma
5.6
is given in Section
C
of Appendix. Next, if we start from
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}]
and
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}]
, write them as
\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\mathbb{E}^{\chi}X_{t,ji}(\cdots)_{ij}
and then apply cumulant expansion w.r.t.
X_{t,ji}
’s, we can get the quantities
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]
and
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}{Q}_{t}{G}]
after the expansion. More specifically, by performing cumulant expansion up to
\kappa_{4}^{t,j}
term on
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}]
and
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}]
, and then using Propositions
4.3
,
5.1
and
5.2
to replace the random quantities by deterministic estimates, we can actually get

(111)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}]}{1-y_{t}}+\frac{y_{t}}{(1-y_{t})^{2}}+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{N^{2}(1-y_{t})}+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right)},

(112)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}{Q}_{t}{G}]=\frac{{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}]}}{1-y_{t}-\frac{1}{N}}-\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}(m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z))}{N^{2}(1-y_{t})\left(1-y_{t}-\frac{1}{N}\right)}+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right)}.

The derivation of the above two equations is similar to (
C.5
), and thus the details are omitted. In addition, we observe that
\text{Tr}P_{t}=p_{t}
and
\text{Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}=\text{Tr}P_{t}G
.

Further using Proposition
5.2
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}^{2}\left([{I}-{P}_{t}]_{ii}[({P}_{t}-{I}){G}]_{ii}-[({I}-{P}_{t}){G}]_{ii}^{2}\right)\Big]

(113)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)-m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}-\left(\frac{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}\right)^{2}+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)}.

Plugging (
109
), (
111
), (
112
) and (
5
) into (
C.5
), after some basic algebra, it turns out that the
\kappa_{4}^{t,j}
terms are cancelled out, and we can get

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}E_{t}(z)=

\displaystyle\frac{y_{t}-1-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}E(z)-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}]+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right)}.

Dividing the coefficient of
E_{t}(z)
on both sides, and then summing over
t
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}E_{t}(z)=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{y_{t}-1-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}E(z)

\displaystyle-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[(1-y_{t}){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[(1-y_{t}){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}]}{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)}.

Notice that by the resolvent identity, we have

\sum_{t=1}^{k}E_{t}(z)=zE(z).

Hence,

\displaystyle E(z)=

\displaystyle\left(z-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{y_{t}-1-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[(1-y_{t}){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[(1-y_{t}){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}]}{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)}

(114)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}(z)}{m_{\boxplus}(z)}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[(1-y_{t}){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[(1-y_{t}){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}]}{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)}

Using Proposition
5.1
, and then performing some simplification (c.f., Section
F.1
in Appendix), we can obtain

(115)

\displaystyle E(z)=\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega^{\prime\prime}_{t}(z)}{\omega_{t}^{\prime}(z)}+(k-1)\left(\frac{2m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}(z)}{m_{\boxplus}(z)}-\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime\prime}(z)}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}(z)}\right)\bigg]+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)}.

Therefore, by the definition of
E(z)
in (
109
),

(116)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}G]=Nm_{\boxplus}(z)+\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega^{\prime\prime}_{t}(z)}{\omega_{t}^{\prime}(z)}+(k-1)\left(\frac{2m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}(z)}{m_{\boxplus}(z)}-\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime\prime}(z)}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}(z)}\right)\bigg]+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)}.

Adding the part that
|\Im z|\leq N^{-K}
of the contour back to the integral of the main term in the RHS of (
116
) (with negligible error) gives the expectation in Theorem
1.11
.
∎

Appendix A
Additional preliminary and proofs of Lemmas in Section
3

The following cumulant expansion formula plays a central role in our computation, whose proof can be found in
[
32
]
; also see for instance
[
45
]
and
[
38
]
for earlier versions of this formula.

Lemma A.1

(Cumulant expansion)
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
be a smooth function, and denote by
f^{(k)}
its
kth
derivative. Then for every fixed
l\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(117)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\xi f(\xi)]=\sum_{k=0}^{l}\frac{\kappa_{k+1}(\xi)}{k!}{\mathbb{E}}[f^{(k)}(\xi)]+\mathcal{R}_{l+1},

assuming that all expectations in (
117
) exist, where
\mathcal{R}_{l+1}
is the remainder term (depending on
f
and
\xi
), such that for any
t>0
,

(118)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\ell+1}=

\displaystyle O(1)\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[|\xi|^{\ell+2}\mathbf{1}_{|\xi|>t}\right]\cdot\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\left|f^{(\ell+1)}(x)\right|+O(1)\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[|\xi|^{\ell+2}\right]\cdot\sup_{|x|\leqslant t}\left|f^{(\ell+1)}(x)\right|.

We also need the following result on the spectrum of the sample covariance matrix.

Lemma A.2
.

Under Assumptions
1.6
and
1.7
, we have for all
t\in[\![k]\!]
,

(119)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}\right)^{2}\leq\lambda_{\min}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})<\lambda_{\max}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})\leq 2\left(1+\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}\right)^{2}

with high probability.

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

In the regime that
p_{t}\geq N^{\epsilon}
for some fixed small
\epsilon>0
, (
119
) can be directly obtained from
[
13
]
.
In the regime that
p_{t}\leq N^{\epsilon}
for sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, we write

\displaystyle X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime}=\mathrm{I}+R,

where
R
is a
p_{t}\times p_{t}
matrix defined entrywise by
R_{ij}=x_{ti}x_{tj}^{\prime}-{\mathbb{E}}x_{ti}x_{tj}^{\prime}
and
x_{ti}
represents the
i
-th row of
X_{t}
. One can easily see that
|R_{ij}|\prec N^{-1}
and
\|R\|\leq\|R\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\prec p_{t}^{2}/N\prec N^{-1+2\epsilon}
. Therefore, by the Weyl’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{l}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})-1\big|\prec N^{-1+2\epsilon},\quad l\in[\![p_{t}]\!],

which implies (
119
).
∎

Based on Lemma
A.2
, we have some preliminary high probability bounds on the spectral norm of some random matrices involved in the following discussions.

Lemma A.3
.

Under Assumptions
1.6
and
1.7
, we have for all
t\in[\![k]\!]
,

(120)

\displaystyle\|X_{t}\|\prec 1,\quad\|(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\|\prec 1.

Further, for any
z\in\mathbb{C}
such that
\|G\|\prec 1
, we have

(121)

\displaystyle|{\rm tr}G|\prec 1,\quad|{\rm tr}P_{t}G|\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N},\quad|{\rm tr}Q_{t}G|\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N},\quad|{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}|\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N}.

Proof of Lemma
A.3
.

By Lemma
A.2
, we can obtain (
120
) directly. If
\|G\|=O_{\prec}(1)
, (
121
) can be bounded as the following,

\displaystyle|{\rm tr}G|\leq\|G\|\prec 1,\quad|{\rm tr}P_{t}G|\leq\frac{p_{t}}{N}\|P_{t}G\|\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N},

\displaystyle|{\rm tr}Q_{t}G|\leq\frac{p_{t}}{N}\|Q_{t}G\|\leq\frac{p_{t}}{N}\|X_{t}\|^{2}\|\|(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\|^{2}\|G\|\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N},

\displaystyle{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\leq\frac{p_{t}}{N}\|(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\|\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N}.

Here we used the inequality
|{\rm Tr}(A)|\leq r\|A\|
for any matrix
A
with rank
r
.
∎

We have the following properties of the truncation function
\Xi
in (
23
) which is a direct consequence of the definition and Lemma
A.2
.

Lemma A.4
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.11
, we have
\Xi=1
with high probability, and for any fixed
s
,
\partial_{t,ji}^{s}\Xi=0
with high probability.

From the definition of
\Xi
, we also have the following lemma

Lemma A.5
.

Let

\mathsf{X}=(\mathsf{X}^{(N)}(u):N\in\mathbb{N},\ u\in\mathsf{U}^{(N)})

be family of random variables, where
\mathsf{U}^{(N)}
is a possibly
N
-dependent parameter set.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that
|\mathsf{X}^{(N)}(u)|\leq N^{C}
a.s. uniformly in
u
for all sufficiently large
N
. Then for any fixed
n\geq 0
, we have
{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{X}]={\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{X}\cdot\Xi^{n}]+O(N^{-D})
for any fixed
D>0
.

Proof.

Notice that
{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{X}]={\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{X}\cdot\Xi^{n}]+{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{X}\cdot(1-\Xi^{n})].
By Cauchy-Schwarz, we have

\displaystyle|{\mathbb{E}}[\mathsf{X}\cdot(1-\Xi^{n})]|\leq N^{C}\mathbb{P}(\Xi\neq 1)=O(N^{-D})

for any large
D>0
. Where in the last step, we used Lemma
A.4
.

∎

We further notice that on the event
\{\Xi\neq 0\}
, by the definition of
\Xi
the crude bounds hold

\displaystyle\|(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\|\leq 2N^{K+1},\qquad\|X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime}\|\leq 2N^{K+1},\qquad t\in[\![k]\!].

Next, we give the proofs of Lemmas
3.3
and
3.5
.

Proof of Lemma
3.3
.

Part (i) is obvious from Definition
3.1
. For any fixed
\varepsilon>0
, we have

(122)

\displaystyle|{\mathbb{E}}\mathsf{X}_{1}|\leq{\mathbb{E}}|\mathsf{X}_{1}{\bf 1}(|\mathsf{X}_{1}|\leq N^{\varepsilon}\Psi)|+{\mathbb{E}}|\mathsf{X}_{1}{\bf 1}(|\mathsf{X}_{1}|\geq N^{\varepsilon}\Psi)|\leq N^{\varepsilon}\Psi+N^{C}\Psi\mathbb{P}(|\mathsf{X}_{1}|\geq N^{\varepsilon}\Psi)=O(N^{\varepsilon}\Psi)

for sufficiently large
N\geq N_{0}(\epsilon)
. This proves part (ii).
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.5
.

First, we have the following high probability bound on the spectral norm of
H
.

\displaystyle\|H\|=\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}P_{t}\Big\|\prec\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}X_{t}^{\prime}X_{t}\Big\|\prec 1,

where in the second step, we used Lemma
A.2
to get that
\sum_{t=1}^{k}X_{t}^{\prime}((X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-CI)X_{t}
is semi-negative definite for some large
C>0
. In the last step, we used the fact that the spectral norm of a
p\times N
sample covariance matrix is bounded with high probability.
Therefore, by the boundedness of the operator norm of
H
,
G(z)
can be expanded as

\displaystyle G(z)=-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}z^{-l}H^{l-1},

as
\Im z\to\infty
. Therefore, from the definition of
\omega_{t}^{c}(z)
, we have

\displaystyle\omega_{t}^{c}(z)=z-\frac{\sum_{s\neq t}{\rm tr}P_{s}G(z)}{{\rm tr}G(z)}=z-\frac{\sum_{s\neq t}\left({\rm tr}P_{s}z^{-1}+{\rm tr}P_{s}Hz^{-2}\right)}{z^{-1}}+O(z^{-2}),

as
\Im z\to\infty
. Taking imaginary part on both sides, we have

\displaystyle\Im\omega_{t}^{c}(z)-\Im z=\frac{\Im z\sum_{s\neq t}{\rm tr}P_{s}H}{|z|^{2}}+O\left(|z|^{-2}\right),

as
\Im z\to\infty
.
Since

\sum_{s\neq t}{\rm tr}P_{s}H\geq\sum_{s\neq t}{\rm tr}P_{s}=\sum_{s\neq t}p_{s}/N,

we can get

\Im\omega_{t}^{c}(z)-\Im z\geq\frac{\Im z}{2|z|^{2}}\sum_{s\neq t}p_{s}/N,

as
\Im z\to\infty
. Hence, when
\Im z
is sufficiently large, we have

\Im\omega_{t}^{c}(z)-\Im z\geq c

for some small constants
c>0
by Assumption
1.7
. This concludes the proof.
∎

We further recall the classical Newton-Kantorvich theorem
[
29
]
.

Theorem A.6

(Newton-Kantorvich)
.

Let
X,Y
be Banach spaces,
\mathcal{S}\subset X
, and
g:\mathcal{S}\mapsto Y
a continuous function, continuously differentiable on
\mathrm{int}(\mathcal{S})
. Take
x_{0}\in\mathrm{int}(\mathcal{S})
,
L,b>0
and suppose that,

1.

g^{\prime}(x_{0})
is non-singular,

2.

\|g^{\prime}\left(x_{0}\right)^{-1}\left[g^{\prime}(y)-g^{\prime}(x)\right]\|\leq L\|x-y\|\quad\text{ for any }x,y\in\mathcal{S}
,

3.

\|g^{\prime}\left(x_{0}\right)^{-1}g\left(x_{0}\right)\|\leq b
,

4.

2bL<1
.

Define

\displaystyle t_{*}:=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-2bL}}{L},\quad t_{**}:=\frac{1+\sqrt{1-2bL}}{L}

If
B[x_{0},t_{*}]\subset\mathcal{S}
, then sequences
\{x_{n}\}
generated by Newton’s Method for solving
g(x)=0
with starting point
x_{0}
,

\displaystyle x_{n+1}=x_{n}-g^{\prime}(x_{n})^{-1}g(x_{n}),\quad n=1,2,\cdots

is well defined, is contained in
B(x_{0},t_{*})
, converges to a point
x_{*}\in B[x_{0},t_{*}]
which is the unique zero of
g
in
B[x_{0},t_{*}]
and

\displaystyle\left\|x_{*}-x_{n+1}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\left\|x_{*}-x_{n}\right\|,\quad n=0,1,\cdots

Appendix B
Proofs of results in Section
4

B.1.
Proof of Lemma
4.1

For any
t\in[\![k]\!]
, define
\tilde{X}_{t}
be an
\tilde{p}_{t}\times\tilde{N}
Gaussian matrix with
\tilde{p}_{t}=y_{t}\tilde{N}
, where we choose
\tilde{N}=\ell N
so that
\tilde{p_{t}}=\ell p_{t}
is an integer. In this part, we regard
N
and also
k=k(N)
as fixed, but send
\ell
to infinity independently of
N
. Using the large
\ell
(and thus large
\tilde{N}
) limit behaviour of the ESD of the matrix
\tilde{H}
defined below in (
123
), we can get some basic property of
\mu_{\boxplus}
. Performing the singular value decomposition, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{t}=\tilde{U}^{\prime}_{t}\big(\tilde{\Lambda}_{t},0\big)\tilde{V}_{t},\quad t\in[\![k]\!],

where
\tilde{U}_{t}
and
\tilde{V}_{t}
are
\tilde{p}_{t}
dimensional and
\tilde{N}
dimensional orthogonal matrices respectively. Since
\tilde{X}_{t}
has i.i.d normal entries,
\tilde{U}_{t}
and
\tilde{V}_{t}
are independent and Haar distributed. Let

(123)

\displaystyle\tilde{H}:=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\tilde{X}_{t}^{\prime}(\tilde{X}_{t}\tilde{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\tilde{X}_{t}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\tilde{V}_{t}^{\prime}\left(I_{\tilde{p}_{t}}\oplus 0_{\tilde{N}-\tilde{p}_{t}}\right)\tilde{V}_{t}.

Notice that the ESD of
\tilde{V}_{t}^{\prime}\big(I_{\tilde{p}_{t}}\oplus 0_{\tilde{N}-\tilde{p}_{t}}\big)\tilde{V}_{t}
goes to
\mu_{t}
when
\tilde{N}\to\infty
via
\ell\to\infty
, and it is well known that the ESD of
\tilde{H}
converges weakly in probability to
\mu_{\boxplus}
; see
[
19
]
for instance. Therefore, in order to show (
27
), it suffices to show that

(124)

\displaystyle\text{Spec}(\tilde{H})\setminus\{0\}\subset[a,b]

with high probability (in
\tilde{N}
) for some strictly positive constants
a
and
b
. Since the non-zero eigenvalues of
\tilde{H}
are the same as the following matrix

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{B}}={\rm diag}((\tilde{X}_{t}\tilde{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}})_{t=1}^{k}\cdot\tilde{X}\tilde{X}^{\prime}\cdot{\rm diag}((\tilde{X}_{t}\tilde{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}})_{t=1}^{k},

where
\tilde{Y}
is defined analogously to
Y
in the definition of block correlation matrix. We have by the bounds of the smallest and largest eigenvalues of sample covariance matrix (c.f. Lemma
A.2
),

(125)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}\|\leq C\left(1+\sqrt{y}\right)^{2}\max_{t}\left(1-\sqrt{y_{t}}\right)^{-1}\leq C^{\prime}\max_{t}\left(1-\sqrt{y_{t}}\right)^{-1}

and similarly,

(126)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}\|\leq C\left(1-\sqrt{y}\right)^{-2}\max_{t}\left(1+\sqrt{y_{t}}\right)\leq C^{\prime}\left(1-\sqrt{y}\right)^{2}

with high probability (in
\tilde{N}
).
Here we used the assumption that
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
.
Note the above bounds hold almost surely. Since
y_{t}
and
y
are away from
1
, we have (
124
). Combining (
125
) with the fact that
\tilde{H}
is semi-positive definite, we have (
28
). Hence, we complete the proof.

B.2.
Proof of Lemma
4.2

If
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, similar to (
125
) and (
126
), we have

(127)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{B}}\|\leq C\left(1+\sqrt{y}\right)^{2}\max_{t}\left(1-\sqrt{y_{t}}\right)^{-1}\leq C^{\prime}\max_{t}\left(1-\sqrt{y_{t}}\right)^{-1},

(128)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}\|\leq C\left(1-\sqrt{y}\right)^{-2}\max_{t}\left(1+\sqrt{y_{t}}\right)\leq C^{\prime}\left(1-\sqrt{y}\right)^{2}

with high probability. Since
y_{t}
and
y
are away from
1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Spec}(H)\subset\{0\}\cup[a,b]

with high probability (in
N
) for some strictly positive constants
a
and
b
. Therefore, by the construction of contours in (
18
), we have
\|G(z)\|=\max_{i}|\lambda_{i}(H)-z|\sim 1
with high probability. The upper bound of
|{\rm tr}G(z)|
can be obtained directly by the inequality
|{\rm tr}G(z)|\leq\|G(z)\|
. For the lower bound, the discussion is divided into three cases.

(i) When
z\in\cup_{a=2}^{5}\mathcal{C}_{a}
, we know that
\Im z>0
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle|{\rm tr}G(z)|\geq\Im{\rm tr}G(z)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\Im z}{|\lambda_{i}-z|^{2}}\gtrsim 1

with high probability. Here in the last step we used the fact that
\mathrm{Spec}(H)
is bounded with high probability.

(ii) When
z\in\mathcal{C}_{6}
, with sufficiently large
\Re z
, we have

\displaystyle|{\rm tr}G(z)|\geq|\Re{\rm tr}G(z)|=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\Re z-\lambda_{i}}{|\lambda_{i}-z|^{2}}\gtrsim 1

(iii)When
z\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle|{\rm tr}G(z)|\geq\left(1-\frac{p}{N}\right)\frac{1}{|z|}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}-|z|}\gtrsim 1

with high probability. Here in the last step we used the smallness of
|z|
and the fact that
\lambda_{p}\geq a
with high probability.

If
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
, combining (
127
) with the fact that
H
is semi-positive definite, we have
\mathrm{Spec}(H)\subset[0,C]
with high probability (in
N
) for some strictly positive constant
C
. Therefore, if
z\in\gamma_{1}\cup\gamma_{2}
(sufficiently large contours, c.f. (
18
)), we still have
\|G(z)\|=\max_{i}|\lambda_{i}(H)-z|\sim 1
with high probability. For the estimate of
|{\rm tr}G(z)|
, we only need to consider the case when
z\in\mathcal{C}_{7}
. This case is similar to case (ii) above. We have

\displaystyle|{\rm tr}G(z)|\geq|\Re{\rm tr}G(z)|=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\lambda_{i}-\Re z}{|\lambda_{i}-z|^{2}}\gtrsim 1

with high probability. Here in the last step we used the fact that
\mathrm{Spec}(H)
is supported in the positive real line with high probability.

B.3.
Proof of Lemma
4.4

We will show the estimates of
\gamma_{t1}
and
\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\gamma_{t1}
in detail, and the estimates of other terms will be sketchy since it is simpler.

We first consider
\gamma_{t1}
. For simplicity, we set

\displaystyle Z={\rm Tr}P_{t}G-\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)

and further set

\displaystyle\mathsf{Z}^{p,q}:=(Z\cdot\Xi)^{p}(\bar{Z}\cdot\Xi)^{q},

where
\Xi
is defined in (
23
).
By direct calculation, one can show that

(129)

\displaystyle\partial_{t,ji}Z=[\mathcal{A}]_{ij}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=

\displaystyle 2W_{t}G(I-P_{t})-2W_{t}GP_{t}G(I-P_{t})

\displaystyle+2\left({\rm tr}G-{\rm tr}P_{t}G\right)\left((X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-2}X_{t}-W_{t}GQ_{t}+W_{t}GQ_{t}G(I-P_{t})+(I-P_{t})GX_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}W_{t}^{\prime})^{-2}\right)

(130)

\displaystyle+2\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)\left(W_{t}G(I-P_{t})+W_{t}GG(I-P_{t})+W_{t}GP_{t}G(I-P_{t})\right),

and it is easy to show that
\|\mathcal{A}\|
is bounded with high probability by Lemma
A.3
.

Taking derivatives inductively and using Lemma
A.3
, one can easily check the following bound for any fixed order partial derivatives

\displaystyle|\partial_{t,ji}^{s}Z|=O_{\prec}(1),\quad s\in\mathbb{N}.

For any fixed positive integer
n
, by the cumulant expansion, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm Tr}P_{t}G-\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)\left({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G\right)\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}X^{\prime}_{t,ij}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}-\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)\Big)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}Z\Big)\Xi\mathsf{Z}^{n-2,n}\bigg]+\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}\bar{Z}\Big)\Xi\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{s}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\geq 1}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}+s_{1}=s\\
s_{1}\geq 1\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{0}}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\}\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{1}}\Xi\Big]+{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\mathcal{R}^{ij}_{l+1}\bigg]

(131)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{1}+\mathsf{T}_{2}+\mathsf{T}_{3}+\mathsf{T}_{4}+\mathsf{E}_{1}+\mathsf{E}_{2}.

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{ij}_{l+1}=

\displaystyle O(1)\cdot\sup_{X_{t,ji}\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\Xi\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\right\}\right|\cdot\mathbb{E}[|X_{t,ji}|^{l+2}\mathbf{1}_{|X_{t,ji}|>x}]

(132)

\displaystyle+O(1)\cdot\mathbb{E}[|X_{t,ji}|^{l+2}]\cdot\sup_{|X_{t,ji}|\leqslant x}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\Xi\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\right\}\right|.

We first consider the error terms
\mathsf{E}_{i},i=1,2
. For
\mathsf{E}_{1}
, by the definition of
\Xi
in (
23
), we have
\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{1}}\Xi=0
with high probability. Together with the fact that
\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{0}}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\}\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{1}}\Xi
has deterministic bound when
\Im z>0
, we have

(133)

\displaystyle|\mathsf{E}_{1}|\prec N^{-D}

for any large
D
. For
\mathsf{E}_{2}
. Choosing
x=N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}
with
\epsilon
being any small positive constant, we have

(134)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{t,ji}|^{l+2}\mathbf{1}_{|X_{t,ji}|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}\Big]\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[|X_{t,ji}|^{2l+4}\Big]}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}(|X_{t,ji}|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon})}\lesssim N^{-D}

for any large
D>0
. Due to the existence of
\Xi
and
\Im z>0
, we have

(135)

\displaystyle\sup_{X_{t,ji}\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G(z_{1})]_{ji}\Xi\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\right\}\right|\lesssim N^{\tilde{K}}

for some fixed
\tilde{K}>0
. Combining (
134
) and (
135
), we have the first term in (
132
) can be bounded by
N^{-D}
for any large
D>0
. For the second term in (
132
), by Lemma
C.5
and the crude bound
|\bar{Z}|,|Z|\prec p_{t}
(c.f. Lemmas
A.3
and
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{|X_{t,ji}|\leqslant N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\Xi\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\right\}\right|\prec p_{t}^{2n-1}

Therefore, together with Assumption
1.6
, we have

(136)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|X_{t,ji}|^{l+2}]\cdot\sup_{|X_{t,ji}|\leqslant x}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\Xi\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\right\}\right|\prec p_{t}^{2n-1}N^{-\frac{l+2}{2}}.

Plugging (
134
), (
135
) and (
136
) into (
132
), we can get

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l+1}^{t,ji}\prec p_{t}^{2n-1}N^{-\frac{l+2}{2}}.

Choosing
l>4n+1
, we have

(137)

\displaystyle|\mathsf{E}_{2}|\prec\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{p_{t}^{2n-1}}{N^{2n+2}}\prec\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)^{n}.

We then turn to the main terms (
\mathsf{T}_{i},i=1,2,3,4
) in the RHS of (
131
). First,
notice that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}=

\displaystyle\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)\left({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G\right)-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G-{\rm tr}Q_{t}GG+{\rm tr}Q_{t}GP_{t}G

(138)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)\left({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right),

which together with (
129
) gives

(139)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}Z=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathcal{A}_{ij}={\rm tr}\mathcal{A}W_{t}G=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right),

where we used (
130
) from which it is easy to check
|{\rm Tr}\mathcal{A}W_{t}G|\leq p_{t}\|\mathcal{A}W_{t}G\|=O_{\prec}(p_{t})
.
Plugging (
138
) and (
139
) (also its complex conjugate counterpart) into (
131
), we have

(140)

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{1}+\mathsf{T}_{2}+\mathsf{T}_{3}={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-2,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n-1}\bigg].

Next, we estimate
\mathsf{T}_{4}
in (
131
).
First, similarly to the proof for Lemma
A.5
, we can easily show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{s}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\}\bigg]={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{s}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}Z^{n-1}\bar{Z}^{n}\}\bigg]+O_{\prec}(N^{-D})

for any fixed
D>0
. Further, since the complex conjugate takes no effect in the remaining estimations, we drop the complex conjugate for simplicity.
Therefore, we will work with
Z^{2n-1}
instead of
\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}
in the remaining derivation, for notational brevity. For any
s\geq 2
, we first write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{s}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}Z^{2n-1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{r=0}^{s}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\geq 0,s_{1},\cdots,s_{r}>0\\
s_{0}+s_{1}+\cdots+s_{r}=s\end{subarray}}\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{0}}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{1}}Z\cdots\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{r}}ZZ^{2n-1-r}

\displaystyle=:\sum_{r=0}^{s}\mathcal{C}_{r,s}Z^{(2n-1-r)\vee 0}

When
r=s
, i.e.,
s_{0}=s_{1}-1=\cdots=s_{r}-1=0
, with (
129
) and the fact that
\|\mathcal{A}\|\prec 1
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{r,s}|\prec

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\left([\mathcal{A}]_{ji}\right)^{r}\bigg|\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|[W_{t}G]_{ji}||[\mathcal{A}]_{ji}|

(141)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sqrt{\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|[W_{t}G]_{ji}|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|[\mathcal{A}]_{ji}|^{2}}=\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sqrt{{\rm Tr}W_{t}GG^{\ast}W_{t}^{\prime}}\sqrt{{\rm Tr}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{A}^{*}}\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}.

When
s-r=1
, i.e.,
s_{0}-1=s_{1}-1=\cdots=s_{r}-1=0
or there exists an
i
such that
s_{0}=s_{1}-1=\cdots=s_{i}-2=\cdots=s_{r}-1=0
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{r,s}|\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+2}{2}}}\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\left([\mathcal{A}]_{ji}\right)^{r}\bigg|+\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+2}{2}}}\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}[\mathcal{A}]_{ji}\left([\mathcal{A}]_{ji}\right)^{r-1}\bigg|

\displaystyle\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+2}{2}}}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\left(|[\mathcal{A}]_{ji}|+|[W_{t}G]_{ji}|\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\left(\sqrt{p_{t}{\rm Tr}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{A}^{*}}+\sqrt{p_{t}{\rm Tr}W_{t}GG^{*}W_{t}^{\prime}}\right)\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}},

where in the third step we used Cauchy-Schwarz.
When
s-r\geq 2
, we have two cases,
r=0
and
r>0
. For
r>0
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{r,s}|\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+3}{2}}}\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\geq 0,s_{1},\cdots,s_{r}>0\\
s_{0}+s_{1}+\cdots+s_{r}=s\end{subarray}}\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{0}}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{1}}Z\cdots\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{r}}Z\bigg|\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}},

and for
r=0

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{0,s}|\prec

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{2}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\bigg|+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{3}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\bigg|+\frac{1}{N^{\frac{5}{2}}}\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{4}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\bigg|+\cdots

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{2}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\bigg|+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right).

Notice that all terms in
\partial_{t,ji}^{2}[W_{t}G]_{ji}
contain at least one off-diagonal entry, which can be denoted by
\mathcal{M}_{ij}
in general, say. Hence, the bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|[\mathcal{M}]_{ij}|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sqrt{\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|[\mathcal{M}]_{ij}|^{2}Np_{t}}=\sqrt{p_{t}{\rm Tr}\mathcal{M}\mathcal{M}^{*}}\prec p_{t}

implies

|\mathcal{C}_{0,s}|\prec p_{t}/N.

Combining the above estimates, we can get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-2,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{r=1}^{2n-1}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\right)|Z|^{2n-r-1}\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)^{n}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-2,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{r=1}^{2n-1}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\right)|Z\cdot\Xi|^{2n-r-1}\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)^{n}\right)

where in the last step, we substitute
Z\cdot\Xi
for
Z
, by a similar argument as the proof of Lemma
A.5
, up to an
O_{\prec}(N^{-D})
error which can be absorbed by
O_{\prec}\left(\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)^{n}\right)
.

Applying Young’s inequality for each term in the RHS of the above equation, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)^{2n}(\log N)^{2n}\bigg]+\frac{2n-1}{2n\left(\log N\right)^{\frac{2n}{2n-1}}}{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{r=1}^{2n-1}\left(\frac{r+1}{2n}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\right)^{\frac{2n}{r+1}}(\log N)^{\frac{2n}{r+1}}\bigg]+\frac{2n-r-1}{2n\left(\log N\right)^{\frac{2n}{2n-r-1}}}{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]\right)

(142)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\prec}\left(\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)^{n}\right)+\sum_{r=0}^{2n-1}\frac{2n-r-1}{2n\left(\log N\right)^{\frac{2n}{2n-r-1}}}{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big],

where in the last step we absorbed the logarithmic factor into
O_{\prec}(\cdot)
by definition, and we also noticed that the quantities which give us the
O_{\prec}(\cdot)
terms in the first two lines all have crude deterministic bound due to the existence of the smooth cutoff function
\chi
, and thus the stochastic bound can also be applied when one take expectation.
Absorbing the term with
\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}
in the RHS into LHS in the above equation, we get

(143)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\Big]\prec\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)^{n}.

Using Markov inequality, we obtain (
220
) from (
143
), due to the arbitrariness of the fixed
n
.

If we replace the definition of
Z
by

\displaystyle Z={\rm Tr}P_{t}-(1-y_{t}){\rm Tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1},

write
{\rm Tr}P_{t}=\sum_{ij}^{(t)}X_{t,ji}[W_{t}]_{ji}
and then apply cumulant expansion w.r.t
X_{t,ji}
’s
, similarly, we can obtain the estimate of
\gamma_{t2}
. Here at the end we used the trivial fact that
{\rm Tr}P_{t}=p_{t}
. The details are omitted.

Again, if we replace the definition of
Z
by

\displaystyle Z={\rm Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}-(1-y_{t}){\rm Tr}Q_{t}G,

write
{\rm Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}=\sum_{ij}^{(t)}X_{t,ji}[W_{t}GP_{t}]_{ji}
and then apply cumulant expansion w.r.t
X_{t,ji}
’s
, similarly, we can obtain the estimate of
\gamma_{t3}
. Here at the end we used the fact that
{\rm Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}={\rm Tr}P_{t}^{2}G={\rm Tr}P_{t}G
. The details are also omitted.

Next, we consider the estimate of
\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\gamma_{t1}
.
By Lemma
A.2
, we have,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}((X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1})\leq C\left(1-\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}\right)^{-2},

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}(X_{t}^{\prime}X_{t}),\quad\lambda_{\max}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})\leq C\left(1+\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}\right)^{2}\leq 4C,

with high probability (c.f. Definition
3.2
), where
A
is any Hermitian matrix with bounded spectral norm
C
. Then with high probability we have that
\sum_{t=1}^{k}X_{t}^{\prime}[(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-2}-aI]X_{t}
is a negative definite matrix for sufficiently large
a>0
. In addition, because
Q_{t}
and
X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime}
are positive semi-definite matrices, we have

(144)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}Q_{t}\Big\|\leq C\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime}\Big\|=O_{\prec}(1).

Define

\displaystyle Z_{k}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}{\rm Tr}P_{t}G-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)

and further set

\displaystyle\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{q,p}:=(Z_{k}\cdot\Xi)^{p}(\bar{Z}_{k}\cdot\Xi)^{q}.

Then for any fixed
n
, applying the cumulant expansion formula in Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n,n}\big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\alpha_{t}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}X^{\prime}_{t,ij}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\alpha_{t}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t,ji}\{\alpha_{t}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\}\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\alpha_{t}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

(145)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{s}\{\alpha_{t}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\}\bigg]+O_{\prec}(1).

where the error
O_{\prec}(1)
can be verified similarly to (
131
) by choosing
l
sufficiently large.
By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}=

\displaystyle\sum_{d=1}^{k}{\rm tr}P_{d}G\Big((\partial_{x}\mathcal{F})(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}P_{d}G)+(\partial_{y}\mathcal{F})(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}G)\Big)+\sum_{d=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{d}(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}P_{d}G)

\displaystyle-\sum_{d=1}^{k}\Big((\partial_{x}\mathcal{F})(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}P_{d}G)+(\partial_{y}\mathcal{F})(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}G)\Big)\left({\rm tr}(X_{d}X_{d}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{d}G\right)\left({\rm tr}G-{\rm tr}P_{d}G\right)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{d=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{d}\left({\rm tr}(X_{d}X_{d}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{d}G\right)[W_{t}G(I-P_{d})G(I-P_{t})]_{ji}

\displaystyle-2\sum_{d=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{d}[W_{t}GQ_{d}G(I-P_{t})]_{ji}\left({\rm tr}G-{\rm tr}P_{d}G\right)+2\mathcal{W}_{t}(W_{t}^{\prime}([X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{ij}+[W_{t}GQ_{t}]_{ji}

\displaystyle-[(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-2}X_{t}G(I-P_{t})]_{ji})\left({\rm tr}G-{\rm tr}P_{t}G\right)-2\mathcal{W}_{t}[W_{t}G(I-P_{t})]_{ji}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)

Similarly to (
129
), one can check that, there exists a matrix
\mathcal{A}_{k}\in\mathbb{C}^{N\times p_{t}}
with
\|\mathcal{A}_{k}\|=O_{\prec}(1)
, s.t.

(146)

\displaystyle\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}=[\mathcal{A}_{k}]_{ij}.

With the boundedness of
\mathcal{W}_{t}
and its partial derivatives, the boundedness of
\|\mathcal{A}_{k}\|
is benefited from either the spectral norm of the summation of
Q_{d}
is bounded or
\sum_{s=1}^{k}{\rm tr}M=O_{\prec}(1)
when
M
has
O_{\prec}(1)
operator norm and of rank
p_{t}
.
For example, for the fourth term of
\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{d=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{d}({\rm tr}(X_{d}X_{d}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{d}G)W_{t}G(I-P_{d})G(I-P_{t})\Big\|\prec\sum_{d=1}^{k}\left|{\rm tr}(X_{d}X_{d}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{d}G\right|\prec\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{p_{t}}{N}=O_{\prec}(1).

And for the fifth term of
\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{d=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{d}W_{t}GQ_{d}G(I-P_{t})\left({\rm tr}G-{\rm tr}P_{d}G\right)\Big\|

\displaystyle\prec\Big\|\sum_{d=1}^{k}W_{t}GQ_{d}G(I-P_{t})\Big\|+\Big\|\sum_{s=1}^{k}W_{t}GQ_{d}G(I-P_{t}){\rm tr}P_{d}G\Big\|

\displaystyle\prec\Big\|\sum_{d=1}^{k}Q_{d}\Big\|+\Big|\sum_{d=1}^{k}{\rm tr}P_{d}G\Big|=O_{\prec}(1).

Here we used (
144
) to bound the spectral norm of the summation of
Q_{d}
. The other terms in the formula of
\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}
can be checked similarly.

Therefore, we have the technical estimates

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\rm tr}W_{t}G\mathcal{A}_{k}=O_{\prec}\left(1\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}\leq\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sqrt{\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|[\mathcal{A}_{k}]_{ij}|^{2}Np_{t}}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\sqrt{p_{t}{\rm Tr}\mathcal{A}_{k}\mathcal{A}_{k}^{*}}}{N}=O_{\prec}\left(1\right),

and the same bounds hold when
Z_{k}
is replaced by its complex conjugate
\bar{Z}_{k}
.

Applying the above two estimates, together with (
138
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t,ji}\{\mathcal{W}_{t}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\}\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\mathcal{W}_{t}}{N}\partial_{t,ji}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\}\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)({\rm Tr}G-{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{1}{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}(\partial_{x}\mathcal{F})(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{1}{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}(\partial_{y}\mathcal{F})(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}G)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\mathcal{W}_{t}}{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}\right)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-2,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\mathcal{W}_{t}}{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}\bar{Z}_{k}\right)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n-1}\bigg]+O_{\prec}(N^{-D})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\left({\rm tr}Q_{t}G-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(I-P_{t})G\right)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{1}{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}(\partial_{x}\mathcal{F})(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{1}{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}(\partial_{y}\mathcal{F})(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}G)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}(1)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-2,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}(1)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n-1}\bigg]+O_{\prec}(N^{-D})

(147)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}(1)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}(1)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-2,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}(1)\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n-1}\bigg],

where in the first step we replaced
Z_{k}\cdot\Xi
by
Z_{k}
(the error is negligible by Lemma
A.5
) and absorbed the terms containing the derivatives of
\Xi
into the error (c.f. Lemma
A.4
). In the last step we also used (
121
), the boundedness of the partial derivatives of
\mathcal{F}(x,y)
, and

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}P_{t}G)=O_{\prec}(p_{t}),\quad\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}G)=O_{\prec}(p_{t}).

Hence, (
147
) gives the estimate for the first two terms in the RHS of (
145
).
Then we turn to the third term in the RHS of (
145
). Since the complex conjugate takes no effect in the remaining estimations, we drop the complex conjugate for simplicity. Specifically, we will work with
Z_{k}^{2n-1}
instead of
\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n-1,n}
in the case
s\geq 2
, for notational brevity. Here we also drop the function
\Xi
first and put it back at the last step, up to negligible error.

For
s>2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{s}\{\mathcal{W}_{t}[W_{t}G]_{ji}Z_{k}^{2n-1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{r=0}^{2n-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\geq 0,s_{1},\cdots,s_{r}>0\\
s_{0}+s_{1}+\cdots+s_{r}=s\end{subarray}}(\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{0}}\mathcal{W}_{t}[W_{t}G]_{ji})\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{1}}Z_{k}\cdots\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{r}}Z_{k}Z_{k}^{2n-1-r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{2n-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}}\right)Z_{k}^{2n-1-r}=\sum_{r=0}^{2n-1}O_{\prec}(1)Z_{k}^{2n-1-r},

where the Green function entries such as
G_{ab}
and
(W_{t}G)_{ab}
are all bounded by
O_{\prec}(1)
crudely.
For
s=2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\partial_{t,ji}^{2}\{\mathcal{W}_{t}[W_{t}G]_{ji}Z_{k}^{2n-1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\Big(\mathcal{W}_{t}(\partial_{t,ji}^{2}[W_{t}G]_{ji})+2\left(\partial_{t,ji}\mathcal{W}_{t}\right)\left(\partial_{t,ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\right)+\left(\partial^{2}_{t,ji}\mathcal{W}_{t}\right)[W_{t}G]_{ji}\Big)Z_{k}^{2n-1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\Big(\mathcal{W}_{t}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}^{2}Z_{k}\right)+2\mathcal{W}_{t}\left(\partial_{t,ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\right)\left(\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}\right)+2\left(\partial_{t,ji}\mathcal{W}_{t}\right)[W_{t}G]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}\right)\Big)Z_{k}^{2n-2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\mathcal{W}_{t}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}\right)^{2}Z_{k}^{2n-3}=O_{\prec}(1)Z_{k}^{2n-1}+O_{\prec}(1)Z_{k}^{2n-2}+O_{\prec}(1)Z_{k}^{2n-3},

where we used the fact that
\partial_{t,ji}\mathcal{W}_{t},\partial_{t,ji}^{2}[W_{t}G]_{ji}
and
\partial_{t,ji}Z_{k}
all generate at least one off-diagonal entry, and then we can use the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to bound the summation over
i,j
of these off-diagonal entries by tracial quantities.

Combining the above cases, and substituting
Z_{k}\cdot\Xi
for
Z_{k}
, we can get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n,n}\big]\leq\sum_{r=0}^{2n-1}{\mathbb{E}}\big[O_{\prec}(1)|Z_{k}\cdot\Xi|^{2n-1-r}\big]+O_{\prec}(N^{-D}).

for any large
D>0
.
Applying Young’s inequality as we did in the estimate of
\gamma_{t1}
, we can get

(148)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}_{k}^{n,n}\big]=O_{\prec}\left(1\right).

Using Markov’s inequality, we obtain the estimate of
\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\gamma_{t1}
from (
148
).

If we replace the definition of
Z_{k}
by

\displaystyle Z_{k}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\rm tr}P_{t}-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1},

and apply the cumulant expansion again, similarly, we can obtain the estimate of
