\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\gamma_{t2}
.
Also, if we replace the definition of
Z_{k}
by

\displaystyle Z_{k}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\rm tr}P_{t}GP_{t}-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}{\rm tr}Q_{t}G,

and apply the cumulant expansion again, similarly, we can obtain the estimate of
\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\gamma_{t3}
. Here we used the fact that
{\rm Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}={\rm Tr}P_{t}^{2}G={\rm Tr}P_{t}G
. The details are omitted. This completes the proof of Lemma 5.4.

B.4.
Proof of Lemma
4.5

Recall

\displaystyle F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})=\omega_{t}-y_{t}+\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}{1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}}

and also the contour defined in (
17
).
Taking derivatives with respect to
\omega_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})=1+\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}{(1-y_{t}-\omega_{t})^{2}},\qquad F^{(n)}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})=\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})n!}{(1-y_{t}-\omega_{t})^{n+1}},\quad n\geq 2.

For
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2}^{0})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, since
\mu_{\boxplus}
has point mass at the origin, we know that when
|z|\to 0
, by the definition of the Stieltjes transform,
|m_{\boxplus}(z)|\to\infty
. Recall that

(149)

\displaystyle m_{\boxplus}(z)=\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}(z)}-\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}(z)},\quad t\in[\![k]\!],

we have
|\omega_{t}(z)|\to 0
as
|z|\to 0
, which can be checked starting from
z=-\infty
and applying monotonicity. More specifically, when
z\to-\infty
along the real line, we have
m_{\boxplus}(z)\to 0+
as well. By (
149
), in this case, we have either
\omega_{t}(z)\to-\infty
or
\omega_{t}(z)\to 1-y_{i}
. We first show that the latter is impossible. This can be checked by taking
z=-E+\mathrm{i}\eta
with increasing
\eta
and sufficiently large
E
. When
E
is sufficiently large,
m_{\boxplus}(z)
is sufficiently small. The smallness of
m_{\boxplus}(z)
will not change when one increase
\eta
. However, if
\omega_{t}(z)
is close to
1-y_{t}
when
z=-E
for large
E
, by increasing
\eta
, it is impossible for the RHS of (
149
) keep being small due to
\Im\omega_{t}(z)\gtrsim\eta
. This shows
\omega_{t}(z)\to-\infty
if
z\to-\infty
. Then starting from
-\infty
, we increase
z
to
0-
. Due to monotonicity,
\omega_{t}(z)\to 0-
when
z\to 0-
by (
149
) and the fact that
\mu_{\boxplus}
has a point mass at
0
. Then we shall further argue that when
z\to 0+
,
\omega_{t}(z)\to 0+
as well. This can be done by showing that when
z
goes from
-\varepsilon
to
\varepsilon
via the arc
|z|=\varepsilon
clockwise, it is impossible for
\omega_{t}(z)
to go from
-\tilde{\varepsilon}
to somewhere close to
1
. Since on the arc
|z|=\varepsilon
,
m_{\boxplus}(z)
will always be large in magnitude. But it is possible for the RHS of (
149
) keep being large when
\omega_{t}(z)
go from
-\tilde{\varepsilon}
to somewhere close to
1
. Therefore, for
z\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21})\cup\mathcal{C}_{1}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22})
, by choosing
\epsilon_{11}
and
\epsilon_{12}
sufficiently small, and then performing Taylor expansion around
\omega_{t}=0
, we have

\displaystyle|\omega_{t}|\sim 1,\qquad F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})=\frac{\omega_{t}}{1-y_{t}}+O(|\omega_{t}|^{2})\sim 1,

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})=1+\frac{y_{t}}{1-y_{t}}+O(|\omega_{t}|)=\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}+O(|\omega_{t}|)\sim 1,

\displaystyle F^{(n)}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})=\frac{y_{t}n!}{(1-y_{t})^{n-1}}+O(\left|y_{t}\omega_{t}\right|)\sim y_{t},\quad n\geq 2,

and

\displaystyle 1-\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})}=1-\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(0)+O\left(|F^{\prime\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(0)\omega_{t}|\right)}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(0)}+\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}O\left(y_{t}|\omega_{t}|\right)=\frac{y-1+O\left(|\omega_{t}|\right)}{k-1}\sim\frac{1}{k}.

For
z\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21},M_{11})
with
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, since
\mu_{\boxplus}
is supported on the nonnegative half of real line and
z
lies outside the support of
\mu_{\boxplus}
(c.f. Lemma
4.1
), we have
\Im\omega_{t}=0
when
\Im z=0
. By the continuity of
\omega_{t}
, if we choose
\epsilon_{21}
to be sufficiently close to
\epsilon_{11}
so that
\Im z=\sqrt{\epsilon_{11}^{2}-\epsilon_{21}^{2}}
is sufficiently small on
\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21},M_{11})
, we also have that
\Im\omega_{t}
is sufficiently small. According to previous discussion, if we choose
z=x+\mathrm{i}0+
for
x\in(-\infty,0)
,
m_{\boxplus}(z)=m_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z))
is monotonic in
x
, and thus
\omega_{t}(z)=\Re\omega_{t}(z)+\mathrm{i}0+
and
\Re\omega_{t}(z)
goes from
-\infty
to
0-
when
x
goes from
-\infty
to
0-
. Hence
|1-y_{t}-\Re\omega_{t}(x+\mathrm{i}0+)|\geq 1-y_{t}
. Then, by continuity (c.f. (
70
)), if we choose
\epsilon_{21}
to be sufficiently close to
\epsilon_{11}
so that
\Im z=\sqrt{\epsilon_{11}^{2}-\epsilon_{21}^{2}}
is sufficiently small, we also have
|1-y_{t}\Re\omega_{t}(z)|\geq\frac{1}{2}(1-y_{t})
when
z\in\mathcal{C}_{2}
.

Since
\Im z>0
and
|z|
is bounded, by the continuity of
\omega_{t}(z)
and definition of Stieltjes transform, we have

\displaystyle|\omega_{t}(z)|\sim 1,\qquad|F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})|=-\frac{1}{|m_{\boxplus}(z)|}\sim 1.

Then we can perform Taylor expansion around
\Im\omega_{t}
,

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})=1+\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}{(1-y_{t}-\Re\omega_{t})^{2}}+O(|\Im\omega_{t}|)\sim 1,

\displaystyle F^{(n)}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})=\frac{y_{t}n!}{(1-y_{t}-\Re\omega_{t})^{n-1}}+O(\left|\Im\omega_{t}\right|)\sim y_{t},\quad n\geq 2.

Further, we lower bound the last quantity in Lemma
4.5
by its imaginary part, which reads as

\displaystyle\bigg|1-\frac{1}{(k-1)}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})}\bigg|\geq\bigg|\frac{1}{(k-1)}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\Im F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})}{|F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})|^{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\gtrsim\bigg|\frac{1}{(k-1)}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{2y_{t}(1-y_{t})(1-y_{t}-\Re(\omega_{t}))\Im(\omega_{t})}{|1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}|^{4}}\bigg|\gtrsim\frac{1}{k},

where we used the bound
|1-y_{t}-\Re\omega_{t}(z)|\geq\frac{1}{2}(1-y_{t})
when
z\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21},M_{11})
. The case of
z\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22},M_{12})
with
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
is similar.

For
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
, by choosing
M_{11},M_{21},M_{12}
and
M_{22}
sufficiently large, we have
|\omega_{t}|
sufficiently large. Therefore,

\displaystyle|\omega_{t}|\sim 1,\qquad|F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})|=|\omega_{t}-y_{t}+O(|\omega_{t}|^{-1})|\sim|\omega_{t}|\sim 1,

\displaystyle|F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})|=\left|1+O(y_{t}|\omega_{t}|^{-1})\right|\sim 1,\qquad|F^{(n)}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})|=O(y_{t}\left|\omega_{t}\right|^{-n})\sim y_{t},\quad n\geq 2.

\displaystyle\bigg|1-\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t})}\bigg|=\bigg|1+\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{1+O(y_{t}|\omega_{t}|^{-1})}\bigg|=\bigg|\frac{-1+O(|\omega_{t}|^{-1})}{k-1}\bigg|\gtrsim\frac{1}{k}.

We finish the proof by combining the above results.

Appendix C
Proofs of results in Section
5

C.1.
Proof of Proposition
5.1

For any
t\in[\![k]\!]
, by the definition of
\omega_{t}^{c}(z)
, we have

{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)=1+\omega_{t}^{c}(z)m_{N}(z)

which together with Proposition
4.3
gives the a priori bound

(150)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)-\left(1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{{\boxplus}}(z)\right)\right|\prec\left|\omega_{t}^{c}(z)-\omega_{t}(z)\right|+\left|m_{N}(z)-m_{{\boxplus}}(z)\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Notice that this error bound can be improved as follows

\displaystyle{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)=\left(\frac{y_{t}}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z)-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z)\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{y_{t}}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)\right)\left(m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),

where we used the estimates of
\gamma_{t2}
and
\gamma_{t3}
(c.f., Lemma
4.4
) in the first step, and Proposition
4.3
and (
150
) in the second step.
Solving the above equation for
{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)
, we can obtain,

\displaystyle{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)=1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{{\boxplus}}(z)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

This proves the first estimate of (
87
).
Starting from the estimate of
\gamma_{t3}
, for any
t\in[\![k]\!]
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right)=\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

For the estimates of two-points functions of Green functions, we first define

\displaystyle Z_{1}=

\displaystyle{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})\right)

\displaystyle+\left({\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})\right),

\displaystyle Z_{2}=

\displaystyle{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})P_{t}-\frac{1-y_{t}}{N}{\rm Tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2}),

and then follow the same argument as we did in the estimate of
\gamma_{t1}
, one can get

(151)

\displaystyle Z_{1},Z_{2}=O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

The details are omitted.
Therefore,

\displaystyle{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})=

\displaystyle\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})\right)

\displaystyle-\left({\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\left(\frac{{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})}{z_{1}-z_{2}}-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})\right)

(152)

\displaystyle-\left({\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Interchanging
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
in the above equation, we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{2}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})=

\displaystyle\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{2})\right)\left(\frac{{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})}{z_{2}-z_{1}}-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{2}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)

(153)

\displaystyle-\left({\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{2}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{2})\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Replacing
{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})
and
{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{2})P_{t}G(z_{1})
by
{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})
and
{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{2})P_{t}G(z_{1})
respectively, and then combining (
152
) and (
153
), we get

\displaystyle{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{({\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})-{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{2}))^{2}}{(z_{1}-z_{2})({\rm tr}G(z_{1})-{\rm tr}G(z_{2}))}

\displaystyle+\frac{{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{2}){\rm tr}G(z_{1})-{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1}){\rm tr}G(z_{2})}{{\rm tr}G(z_{1})-{\rm tr}G(z_{2})}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

By the choice of
\gamma^{0}_{1}
and
\gamma^{0}_{2}
, together with the estimates
{\rm tr}G(z_{1})-{\rm tr}G(z_{2})=m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})-m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})+O_{\prec}(N^{-1})
, we see that
{\rm tr}G(z_{1})\neq{\rm tr}G(z_{2})
with high probability. Further using the estimates of the tracial quantities we already obtained, we get the estimate of
{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})
.
Finally, using the estimate of
{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})
together with the estimation of
Z_{2}
, we can obtain the estimate of
{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})
. This completes the proof of (
89
).

Since for any function
F
which is independent of
z
, we have

{\rm tr}F{G}(z)^{2}=\partial_{z}\left({\rm tr}FG(z)\right),
together with (
151
), we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z){P}_{t}{G}(z)=

\displaystyle\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z)\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z){P}_{t}{G}(z)-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z){P}_{t}{G}(z)\right)

\displaystyle-\left({\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z){P}_{t}{G}(z)-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z)\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z)-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z)\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z)\right)\left(\partial_{z}({\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z))-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z){P}_{t}{G}(z)\right)

\displaystyle-\left({\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z){P}_{t}{G}(z)-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z)\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}(z)-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z)\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Using the estimation of
Z_{2}
with
z_{1}=z_{2}=z
, and then by the deterministic approximations (c.f. (
87
) and Proposition
4.3
), we can obtain (
88
).

C.2.
Proof of Proposition
5.2

We should first have some concentration results of the diagonal entries, which are stated in the following lemma.

Lemma C.1
.

For any
t\in[\![k]\!]
, if
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(154)

\displaystyle[P_{t}]_{ii}-y_{t}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),

(155)

\displaystyle[P_{t}G]_{ii}-\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)(G_{ii}-[P_{t}G]_{ii})=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),

(156)

\displaystyle[{P}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}]_{ii}-\big({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\big)\big([{G}{P}_{t}]_{ii}-[{P}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}]_{ii}\big)-\left(1-[{P}_{t}]_{ii}\right){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),

(157)

\displaystyle 1-(1-y_{t})[(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{jj}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),

(158)

\displaystyle(1-y_{t})[{W}_{t}{G}{W}_{t}^{\prime}]_{jj}-\left({\rm tr}{G}-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}\right)\left([\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}-[{W}_{t}{G}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}\right)=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

The same estimates hold when
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

However, if we directly apply the above lemma to obtain the concentration results of
[G]_{ii}
and
[PG]_{ii}
, the error term will blow up for large
k
. Therefore, we have to further explore the fluctuation averaging effect due to the summation over
k
. The resulting concentration behaviour can be described by the following lemma.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
\mathcal{W}_{t},t\in[\![k]\!]
be a collection of deterministic complex numbers. Suppose that
\sup_{t}|\mathcal{W}_{t}|<C
for some constant
C
. If
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(159)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}[P_{t}G]_{ii}-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)(G_{ii}-[P_{t}G]_{ii})=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

The same estimates hold when
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

The proof of Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
C.2
will be postponed to Section
D
.
With the aid of the two lemmas above, we can prove Proposition
5.2
. Firstly, (
157
) directly implies the first estimate of (
92
).
For any
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
or
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
, taking

\mathcal{W}_{t}=-(1-y_{t})/(\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z))

in (
159
) and rearranging terms, we have

(160)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}

\displaystyle\frac{1+{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G}{-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)(1-y_{t})^{-1}}[P_{t}G]_{ii}-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G}{-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)(1-y_{t})^{-1}}[G]_{ii}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

From (
155
), it is easy to check

(161)

\displaystyle|[PG]_{ii}|\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}.

For the first term in the LHS of (
160
), using (
161
), Lemma
4.4
and Proposition
5.1
, one can check

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1+{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G}{-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)(1-y_{t})^{-1}}[P_{t}G]_{ii}=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}[P_{t}G]_{ii}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)=1+z[G]_{ii}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

For the second term in the LHS of (
160
), similarly, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G}{-\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)(1-y_{t})^{-1}}[G]_{ii}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}[G]_{ii}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

Combining the above estimations, (
160
) boils down to

\displaystyle 1+z[G]_{ii}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}[G]_{ii}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),

which further gives

(162)

\displaystyle[G]_{ii}=\left(z-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}\right)^{-1}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)=m_{\boxplus}(z)+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

Therefore, by the deterministic approximation of the tracial quantities (c.f., Proposition
5.1
and the estimate of
\gamma_{t2}
in Lemma
4.4
) and (
155
), we have

\displaystyle[P_{t}G]_{ii}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G}{1+{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G}[G]_{ii}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)

(163)

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}m_{\boxplus}(z)+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)=1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

By applying similar arguments, we can also prove the second estimate of (
91
) and (
92
). The details are omitted. Hence, we complete the proof of Proposition
5.2
.

C.3.
Proofs of Lemma
5.4

Before we prove Lemma
5.4
, we give some preliminary lemmas, whose proofs will be postponed to Section
D
.

The following lemma provide some estimates on some
W_{t}
related quantities.

Lemma C.3
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.11
, if
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any fixed
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(164)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}]_{ji}\Big|\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}},

(165)

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G(z)]_{ji}\Big|\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}},\qquad\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G(z)P_{t}]_{ji}\Big|\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}.

The same bounds hold for
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

We remark here that similarly to (
93
), the estimates (
164
) and (
165
) still hold if we take derivatives w.r.t to
z
for the quantities in the LHS.

For brevity, in the sequel, for any random variable
\xi
, we use the notation

\displaystyle\langle\xi\rangle:=\xi-\mathbb{E}\xi.

Lemma C.4
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.11
, if
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any fixed
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(166)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\big\langle{\rm Tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}xL_{N}(f)}\bigg]=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right),

(167)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\big\langle{\rm Tr}Q_{t}G(z)\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}xL_{N}(f)}\bigg]=\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\big\langle{\rm Tr}P_{t}G(z)\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}xL_{N}(f)}\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

(168)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{2}{\rm Tr}{G}(z)\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

The same bounds hold for
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

Next, we present a lemma concerning the perturbations of the spectrum of
G,(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}
and
X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime}
, which will be useful for the estimation of the remainder term in (
118
).

Lemma C.5
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
1.11
, let
X_{t}^{(ij)}:=X_{t}^{(ij)}(x)
be the matrix with the single entry
X_{t,ij}
replaced by a generic argument
x
in
X_{t}
. We also define
P_{t}^{(ij)}:=P_{t}^{(ij)}(x)
,
H^{(t,ij)}:=H^{(t,ij)}(x)
and
G^{(t,ij)}:=G^{(t,ij)}(x)
as the analogue of
P_{t},H
and
G
where a single entry
X_{t,ij}
is replaced by
x
.
Then we have, for any fixed (but small)
\epsilon>0
, we have the following estimates uniformly in
|x|\leq N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}
,

(169)

\displaystyle\Big\|X_{t}^{(ij)}\big(X_{t}^{(ij)}\big)^{\prime}\Big\|\prec 1,\quad\Big\|\Big(X_{t}^{(ij)}\big(X_{t}^{(ij)}\big)^{\prime}\Big)^{-1}\Big\|\prec 1,\quad\big\|G^{(t,ij)}\big\|\prec 1.

With the help of the above Lemmas, we can prove Lemma 6.4 as follows.

Proof of Lemma
5.4
.

We start with the estimate of the error terms
\mathsf{E}_{1}
,
\mathsf{E}_{2}
, and
\mathsf{E}_{3}
.
For
\mathsf{E}_{1}
, using Lemma
A.4
and the fact that
\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{0}}\left\{[W_{t}G(z_{1})]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\right\}\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{1}}\Xi
has deterministic upper bound when
\Im z_{1}\geq N^{-K}
, we have
|\mathsf{E}_{1}|\prec N^{-D}
for any fixed large
D>0
. For
\mathsf{E}_{2}
, due to the existence of the truncation function
\Xi
and the fact
\Im z
is greater than
N^{-K}
, we have the deterministic upper bound for
\partial^{s}_{t,ji}\left\{[W_{t}G(z_{1})]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\right\}\cdot\Xi
. This together with the high probability bound
|\partial^{s}_{t,ji}\left\{[W_{t}G(z_{1})]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\right\}|\prec 1
leads to
|\mathsf{E}_{2}|\prec p_{t}/N^{3/2}
. Lastly, we estimate
\mathsf{E}_{3}
. First, for any fixed (but small)
\epsilon>0
, by Cauchy Schwarz, we have

(170)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{t,ji}|^{l+2}\mathbf{1}_{|X_{t,ji}|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}\Big]\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[|X_{t,ji}|^{2l+4}\Big]}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}\big(|X_{t,ji}|>N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\big)}\lesssim N^{-D}

for any large
D>0
, in light of Assumption
1.6
. Due to the existence of
\Xi
and
\Im z\geq N^{-K}
, we have

(171)

\displaystyle\sup_{X_{t,ji}\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G(z_{1})]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Xi\right\}\right|\lesssim N^{O(K)}.

Combining (
170
) and (
171
), we have the first term in (
100
) can be bounded by
N^{-D}
for any large
D>0
. Here our
D
may vary from line to line. For the second term in (
100
), by Lemma
C.5
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{|X_{t,ji}|\leqslant N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Xi\right\}\right|\prec 1

Therefore, together with Assumption
1.6
, we have

(172)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|X_{t,ji}|^{l+2}]\cdot\sup_{|X_{t,ji}|\leqslant N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{l+1}\left\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Xi\right\}\right|\prec N^{-\frac{l+2}{2}}.

Plugging (
170
), (
171
) and (
172
) into (
100
), we can get

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{l+1}^{t,ji}\prec N^{-\frac{l+2}{2}}.

Observe that, in addition to the above high probability bound, we also have the crude deterministic bound of
\mathcal{R}_{l+1}^{t,ji}
due to the existence of
\Xi
and the fact
\Im z\geq N^{-K}
. Hence,
choosing
l\geq 4
, we have
|\mathsf{E}_{3}|\prec p_{t}/N^{2}
.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{1}+\mathsf{E}_{2}+\mathsf{E}_{3}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

In the sequel, we estimate
\mathsf{I}_{ta}
’s one by one.
We start with
\mathsf{I}_{t1}
. By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t1}=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}^{\prime}_{t})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\left({\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}(z_{1})-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

(173)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}^{\prime}_{t})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\left({\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),

where in the last step, we used Proposition
5.1
.
Using the trivial identity
\mathbb{E}[\zeta\langle\xi\rangle]=\mathbb{E}[\langle\zeta\rangle\xi]
, the main term in
\mathsf{I}_{t1}
can be further expanded as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}^{\prime}_{t})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\left({\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}^{\prime}_{t})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\Big]{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\left({\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathbb{E}}\big[{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big]{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}^{\prime}_{t})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}^{\prime}_{t})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle\big\langle{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]+O_{\prec}(N^{-D})

(174)

\displaystyle=:\mathsf{I}_{t11}+\mathsf{I}_{t12}+\mathsf{I}_{t13}+O_{\prec}(N^{-D}),

for any fixed
D>0
.
With the notation in (
94
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t11}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\left({\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]+O_{\prec}(N^{-D}).

For
\mathsf{I}_{t12}
, using Lemma
C.4
, we can similarly write

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t12}=

\displaystyle-\beta_{t}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

For
\mathsf{I}_{t13}
, by Propositions
4.3
and
5.1
,

\displaystyle|\mathsf{I}_{t13}|=

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}^{\prime}_{t})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle\big\langle{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}xL_{N}^{1}(f)}\big\rangle\Big]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left|\big\langle{\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}^{\prime}_{t})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle\right|\left|\big\langle{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle\right|\Big]\prec\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}.

Therefore, plugging the above estimates together with (
174
) into (
173
) arrives at

(175)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t1}=

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]-(\alpha_{t}+\beta_{t}){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1})\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Next, we estimate
\mathsf{I}_{t2}
. From (
97
) we see that the difference
L_{N}^{1}(f)-L_{N}^{2}(f)
is
O_{\prec}(N^{-K+1})
. Further, we also have deterministic upper bound
N^{O(K)}
for
L_{N}^{1}(f)-L_{N}^{2}(f)
from the definition. It is easy to check that these bounds still apply if we take derivative for
L_{N}^{1}(f)-L_{N}^{2}(f)
w.r.t.
X_{t,ij}
’s. Hence, we can perform the replacement of
L_{N}^{1}(f)
by
L_{N}^{2}(f)
in any part of the integrand. The purpose to use such replacement is to avoid possible singularities of the integrand. Particularly,
\mathsf{I}_{t2}
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t2}=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{i}x}{N}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[{W}_{t}{G}(z_{1})]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}L_{N}^{1}(f)\right){\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{i}x}{N}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[{W}_{t}{G}(z_{1})]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}L_{N}^{2}(f)\right){\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]+O_{\prec}(N^{-\frac{K}{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{x}{2\pi}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[{W}_{t}{G}(z_{1})]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}{G}(z_{2})\right){\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\bigg]f(z_{2}){\rm d}z_{2}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\frac{K}{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{x}{\pi}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\Big(\partial_{z_{2}}\left({\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})\right)-\partial_{z_{2}}\left({\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){G}(z_{2})\right)\Big){\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\bigg]f(z_{2}){\rm d}z_{2}+O_{\prec}(N^{-\frac{K}{2}}),

where in the third equality, the term containing
\partial_{t,ji}\Xi
is absorbed into the error.

Moreover, by the concentration in Proposition
5.1
and the fact
G(z_{1})G(z_{2})=(G(z_{1})-G(z_{2}))/(z_{1}-z_{2})
, we have

(176)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t2}=\frac{x}{\pi}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\partial_{z_{2}}\Big[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){G}(z_{2})\Big]f(z_{2}){\rm d}z_{2}{\mathbb{E}}\big[{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big]+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Next, we estimate
\mathsf{I}_{t3}
. After taking derivatives, we notices that all terms in
\mathsf{I}_{t3}
can be expressed in terms of the following factors

\displaystyle[(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{jj},\quad[W_{t}{{G}}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj},\quad[I-{P}_{t}]_{ii},\quad[{{G}}]_{ii},\quad[{P}_{t}{{G}}]_{ii},\quad[{P}_{t}{{G}}{P}_{t}]_{ii}

(177)

\displaystyle[{W}_{t}]_{ji},\quad[{W}_{t}{{G}}]_{ji},\quad[{W}_{t}{{G}}{P}_{t}]_{ji},\quad[P_{t}G^{2}W_{t}^{\prime}]_{ij},\quad[G^{2}W_{t}^{\prime}]_{ij}.

We call a factor with
ii
or
jj
index as a
diagonal entry
and a factor with
ij
or
ji
index as an
off-diagonal entry
, in the sequel.
It is easy to check that all terms in
\mathsf{I}_{t3}
either contain one off-diagonal entry together with two diagonal entries or contain three off-diagonal entries. For simplicity, we denote by
A_{ij}B_{ij}C_{ij}
a generic term in the latter case and by
D_{ij}E_{ii}F_{jj}
a generic term in the former case, where
A,\ldots,D
-entries are chosen from (
177
). Then it suffices to bound

(178)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}A_{ij}B_{ij}C_{ij}\bigg],\qquad\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}D_{ij}E_{ii}F_{jj}\bigg].

For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}A_{ij}B_{ij}C_{ij}\bigg]\bigg|\prec

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|A_{ij}||B_{ij}|\bigg]\leq\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}(|A_{ij}|^{2}+|B_{ij}|^{2})\bigg]\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

For the second term in (
178
), we first replace the diagonal entries by their deterministic estimates, using Proposition
5.2
. Let
E
and
F
be the deterministic approximation of
E_{ii}
and
F_{jj}
, respectively. Then we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}D_{ij}E_{ii}F_{jj}\bigg]\bigg|\leq\bigg|\frac{E}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}D_{ij}F_{jj}\bigg]\bigg|+\frac{|\kappa_{3}^{t,j}|}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|D_{ij}||F_{jj}||E-E_{ii}|\bigg]

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{E}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}D_{ij}F_{jj}\bigg]\bigg|+\frac{1}{N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\left|D_{ij}\right|\bigg]=\bigg|\frac{E}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}D_{ij}F_{jj}\bigg]\bigg|+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

Further estimating
F_{jj}
by
F
, we can get

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}D_{ij}E_{ii}F_{jj}\bigg]\bigg|\prec

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{EF}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}D_{ij}\bigg]\bigg|+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)\prec\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}},

where we used Lemma
C.3
in the last step.

Combining the above estimates, we obtain

(179)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t3}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

Finally, we estimate
\mathsf{I}_{t4}
. For brevity, we further write

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t4}=:\mathsf{I}_{t41}+\mathsf{I}_{t42}+\mathsf{I}_{t43}+\mathsf{I}_{t44}.

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t4b}:={3\choose b-1}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{6{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\partial^{4-b}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}\partial^{b-1}_{t,ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big],\quad b=1,2,3,4.

We first consider the simplest one
\mathsf{I}_{t42}
. Again, due to Lemma
A.4
, we can neglect the terms with derivatives of
\Xi
. Notice that the other terms in
\partial^{2}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{{G}}]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}
contains at least two off-diagonal entries. Let
A_{ij}
and
B_{ij}
be two of them and bound the other two terms simply by
O_{\prec}(1)
, it suffices to have the following bound for
\mathsf{I}_{t42}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}|A_{ij}||B_{ij}|\bigg]\lesssim\frac{1}{N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|A_{ij}|^{2}+|B_{ij}|^{2}\bigg]=O_{\prec}\left({\frac{p_{t}}{N^{2}}}\right).

This gives

(180)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t42}=O_{\prec}\left({\frac{p_{t}}{N^{2}}}\right).

Similar arguments lead to

(181)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t44}=O_{\prec}\left({\frac{p_{t}}{N^{2}}}\right).

For
\mathsf{I}_{t41}
, we first rewrite

(182)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t41}=\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{6{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle\partial^{3}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]

After straightforward calculation, we notice that the terms either contain at least two off-diagonal entries as factors, or all four factors are diagonal entries. In the former case,
we can bound them similarly to
\mathsf{I}_{t42}
and
\mathsf{I}_{t44}
. In the latter case, 2 diagonal factors will be from the set
\mathcal{I}
and the other two are from
\mathcal{J}
defined below

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}=\{[{{G}}]_{ii},[{P}_{t}{{G}}]_{ii},[{P}_{t}{{G}}{P}_{t}]_{ii},[I-{P}_{t}]_{ii}\},\quad\mathcal{J}=\{[{X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}]_{jj},[{W}_{t}{{G}}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}\}.

In light of (
182
), for the contribution of the terms with four diagonal entries to
\mathsf{I}_{t41}
, it suffices to bound the following type of terms

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\Big\langle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}A_{ii}B_{ii}C_{jj}D_{jj}\Big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\bigg],

where
A_{ii},B_{ii}\in\mathcal{I}
and
C_{jj},D_{jj}\in\mathcal{J}
. From the concentration results for diagonal entries (Propposition
5.2
), we know that there exists deterministic estimate
A
, such that
|A_{ii}-A|\prec 1/\sqrt{N}
, and thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\Big\langle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}A_{ii}B_{ii}C_{jj}D_{jj}\Big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\bigg]=\frac{A}{{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\Big\langle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}B_{ii}C_{jj}D_{jj}\Big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

Similarly, by applying the concentration results of
B_{ii},C_{jj},D_{jj}
repeatedly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\Big\langle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}A_{ii}B_{ii}C_{jj}D_{jj}\Big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\bigg]=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right),

which gives

(183)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t41}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

Lastly, we consider
\mathsf{I}_{t43}
. Notice that

(184)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t43}=

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{2N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left(\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{{G}(z_{1})}]_{ji}\right)\left({\rm i}x\partial_{t,ji}^{2}L_{N}^{1}(f)+({\rm i}x\partial_{t,ji}L_{N}^{1}(f))^{2}\right){\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big].

For the term with
({\rm i}x\partial_{t,ji}L_{N}^{1}(f))^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left(\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{{G}(z_{1})}]_{ji}\right)\left(\partial_{t,ji}L_{N}^{1}(f)\right)^{2}\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{{G}(z_{1})}]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}{{G}(z_{2})}\right)\left(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}{{G}(z_{3})}\right)\Big]f(z_{2})f(z_{3}){\rm d}z_{2}{\rm d}z_{3}\bigg|+O_{\prec}(N^{-k})

(185)

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left|\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}{{G}(z_{2})}\right|\left|\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}{{G}(z_{3})}\right|\Big]{\rm d}z_{2}{\rm d}z_{3}

where in the last step we bounded the term
\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{{G}(z_{1})}]_{ji}
by
O_{\prec}(1)
which is easy to check after one computes the derivative. Further, notice that
\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}{{G}(z)}
is a linear combination of terms

\displaystyle[P_{t}{{G}^{2}(z)}{W}_{t}]_{ij},\quad[{{G}^{2}(z)}{W}_{t}]_{ij}

Let
\mathcal{E}_{ij}(z)
and
\mathcal{F}_{ij}(z)
be either of the above two. Then we can bound trvially

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|\mathcal{E}_{ij}(z_{2})||\mathcal{F}_{ij}(z_{3})|\leq\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|\mathcal{E}_{ij}(z_{2})|^{2}+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}|\mathcal{F}_{ij}(z_{3})|^{2}.

Notice that both of the two sums are tracial quantities which can be simply bounded by
O_{\prec}(p_{t})
. Hence, we conclude

(186)

\displaystyle\text{(\ref{061330})}\prec p_{t}.

Further, for the term with
{\rm i}x\partial_{t,ji}^{2}L_{N}^{1}(f)
in (
184
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left(\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{{G}(z_{1})}]_{ji}\right)(\partial^{2}_{t,ji}L_{N}^{1}(f)){\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{{G}(z_{1})}]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}^{2}{\rm Tr}{{G}(z_{2})}\right){\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]f(z_{2}){\rm d}z_{2}\bigg|

(187)

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{2}{\rm Tr}{{G}(z_{2})}\right|\Big]{\rm d}z_{2}\prec p_{t}N^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Here in the last step, we used (
168
). Plugging (
186
) and (
187
) into (
184
), we can conclude

(188)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t43}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

In summary, with (
180
),(
181
),(
183
) and (
188
) we have

(189)

\displaystyle\mathsf{I}_{t4}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

∎

C.4.
Proofs of Lemma
5.5

Recall the definitions of
\alpha_{t}
and
\beta_{t}
from (
94
).
Using Propositions
4.3
and
5.1
together with the estimate of
\gamma_{t2}
in Lemma
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}(z)=

\displaystyle\frac{y_{t}}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),

(190)

\displaystyle\beta_{t}(z)=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{1+\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle{1+\alpha_{t}(z)+\beta_{t}(z)}=\frac{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}{1-y_{t}}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Next, we prove (
103
) when
z
lies in our contours. For
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
, we have
|z|
sufficiently large and therefore
|m_{\boxplus}(z)|
sufficiently small. By the continuity of
\omega_{t}(z)
and (
13
), we see that
|\omega_{t}(z)|
is sufficiently large. In this case, we notice that

\displaystyle m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)=m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\left(\frac{\omega_{t}(z)y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}(z)}-(1-y_{t})\right)\to 1

as
|z|\to\infty
. By the continuity of
m_{\boxplus}(z)
and
\omega_{t}(z)
, we have

|m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)|>0

for sufficiently large
|z|
. This proves (
103
) for
z
on
\mathcal{C}
. For
z\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21})\cup\mathcal{C}_{1}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22})
with
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
(c.f. (
17
)), since we know that when
|z|\to 0
, due to the singularity at
0
,

\displaystyle|m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)|=\Big|m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\left(\frac{\omega_{t}(z)y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}(z)}-(1-y_{t})\right)\Big|\to\infty,

we can also get

|m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)|>0.

For
z\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21},M_{11})\cup\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22},M_{12})
with
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
(c.f. (
17
)), we first notice that

\displaystyle m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)=\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}(z)}{\omega_{t}^{\prime}(z)\omega_{t}(z)(1-\omega_{t}(z))}.

Then it suffices to lower bound
|m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}(z)|
for
z\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21},M_{11})\cup\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22},M_{12})
with
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
(c.f. (
17
)). Since

\displaystyle|m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}(z)|\geq\Im m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}(z)=\int\frac{2(\lambda-\Re z)\Im z}{|\lambda-z|^{4}}{\rm d}\mu_{\boxplus}>0,

we can get the desired result.

C.5.
Proofs of Lemma
5.6

We start from the cumulant expansion of
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}G(z)]
as follows

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}G(z)]=

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[\partial_{t,ji}[W_{t}G(z)]_{ji}]+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{3}^{t,j}}{2N^{3/2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[\partial^{2}_{t,ji}[W_{t}G(z)]_{ji}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{6N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[\partial^{3}_{t,ji}[W_{t}G(z)]_{ji}]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)=:J_{t1}+J_{t2}+J_{t3}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

Here the error term can be bounded similarly to the estimates of
\mathsf{E}_{i},i=1,2,3
in the proof of Lemma
5.4
, we omit the details.

For
J_{t1}
, by direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle J_{t1}={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[({\rm tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right)\left({\rm Tr}{G}-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}\right)]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[({\rm tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right)\left({\rm Tr}{G}-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}\right)\Xi]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}]+O_{\prec}(N^{-D})

(191)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}]{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}{G}-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}]+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Here in the last step we used the estimate of
\gamma_{t2}
in Lemma
4.4
and Propositions
4.3
and
5.1
.

For
J_{t2}
, similar to the arguments in the estimate of
\mathsf{I}_{t3}
(c.f. (
177
)-(
179
)), we can get

\displaystyle J_{t2}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

For
J_{t3}
, we only need to consider the terms consisting of four diagonal entries, and the other terms with at least two off-diagonal entries can be bounded similarly to (
180
). Specifically, we will have

\displaystyle J_{t3}=

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}^{2}\left([{I}-{P}_{t}]_{ii}[({P}_{t}-{I}){G}]_{ii}-[({I}-{P}_{t}){G}]_{ii}^{2}\right)\Big]+O_{\prec}\left({\frac{p_{t}}{N^{2}}}\right).

Combining the above estimates of
J_{t1}
,
J_{t2}
and
J_{t3}
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}G]={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}]{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}{G}-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}]

(192)

\displaystyle+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}^{2}\left([{I}-{P}_{t}]_{ii}[({P}_{t}-{I}){G}]_{ii}-[({I}-{P}_{t}){G}]_{ii}^{2}\right)\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

Appendix D
Proofs of Lemmas in Sections

-

D.1.
Proof of Lemma
C.1

With certain abuse of notation, we recycle the notation
Z
to denote

\displaystyle Z=[{P}_{t}{G}]_{ii}-\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right)\left({G}_{ii}-[{P}_{t}{G}]_{ii}\right),

in this proof.
Further, we set

\displaystyle\mathsf{Z}^{q,p}:=(Z\cdot\Xi)^{p}(\bar{Z}\cdot\Xi)^{q}.

Then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[{P}_{t}{G}]_{ii}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right)([{G}]_{ii}-[{P}_{t}{G}]_{ii})\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\left(\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right)([{G}]_{ii}-[{P}_{t}{G}]_{ii})\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\frac{1}{N}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}\left((n-1)\left(\partial_{t,ji}Z\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-2,n}+n\left(\partial_{t,ji}\bar{Z}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n-1}\right)\Big]

(193)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\partial^{s}_{t,ji}\left\{[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\right\}\Big]+O_{\prec}(\frac{1}{N}).

where the error
O_{\prec}(N^{-1})
can be verified similarly to (
131
) by choosing
l
sufficiently large.
By direct calculation,

(194)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}=\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right)([{G}]_{ii}-[{P}_{t}{G}]_{ii})+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Further, similarly to (
129
), via direct calculation, we can write
\partial_{t,ji}Z=[\mathcal{A}]_{ij}
for some matrix
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbb{C}^{N\times p_{t}}
with
\|\mathcal{A}\|\prec 1
. The details are omitted. Then it is easy to show

(195)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{N}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}Z=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

The same bound also holds when
Z
is replaced by
\bar{Z}
.
Hence, plugging (
194
) and (
195
) to (
193
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\Big]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-2,n}\Big]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n-1}\Big]

(196)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\partial^{s}_{t,ji}\left\{[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\right\}\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)

For the case
s\geq 2
, since the complex conjugate takes no effect in our estimations, we drop the complex conjugate for convenience. Here we also drop the function
\Xi
first and put it back at the last step, up to negligible error. Hence, for the fourth term of (
D.1
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\partial^{s}_{t,ji}\left\{[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}Z^{2n-1}\right\}\Big]=\sum_{r=0}^{2n-1}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[C_{r}Z^{2n-1-r}\Big]

where

\displaystyle C_{r}=\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0},\dots,s_{r}>0\\
s=s_{0}+\dots+s_{r}\end{subarray}}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}(\partial^{s_{0}}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji})\partial^{s_{1}}_{t,ji}Z\cdots\partial^{s_{r}}_{t,ji}Z

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0},\dots,s_{r}>0\\
s=s_{0}+\dots+s_{r}\end{subarray}}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s-r}{2}}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}(\partial^{s_{0}}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji})\partial^{s_{1}}_{t,ji}Z\cdots\partial^{s_{r}}_{t,ji}Z=:O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}\mathcal{C}_{r,s}

Case
1
: when
s-r=0
, we have
s_{0}=s_{1}-1=\cdots s_{r}-1=0
, and
\mathcal{C}_{r,s}
can be written as,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{r,s}=\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}Z\cdots\partial_{t,ji}Z.

Since the product above has at least two off-diagonal entries, we have

|\mathcal{C}_{r,s}|\prec 1.

Case
2
: when
s-r=1
, we have
s_{0}=s_{1}=\cdots=s_{r}=1
or
s_{0}=s_{1}-1=\cdots=s_{i}-2=\cdots s_{r}-1=0
for an
i
. Now
\mathcal{C}_{r,s}
becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{r,s}=O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}(\partial^{s_{0}}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji})\partial^{s_{1}}_{t,ji}Z\cdots\partial^{s_{r}}_{t,ji}Z.

We have at least one off-diagonal entry in both cases for the above product. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{r,s}\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}\sqrt{{p_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}|[\mathcal{M}]_{ij}|^{2}}}=\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}\sqrt{{p_{t}[\mathcal{M}\mathcal{M}^{*}]_{ii}}}\prec{\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}}.

Here
[\mathcal{M}]_{ij}
is the off-diagonal entry either from
{W}_{t}{G}
or the derivative of
Z
.

Case
3
: when
s-r\geq 2
, we can use the trivial bound

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{r,s}=O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s-r}{2}}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}(\partial^{s_{0}}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}{G}]_{ji})\partial^{s_{1}}_{t,ji}Z\cdots\partial^{s_{r}}_{t,ji}Z=O_{\prec}\left({\frac{p_{t}}{N}}\right).

Combining all these cases, we get

|C_{r}|\prec N^{-\frac{r+1}{2}}.

Substituting
Z\cdot\Xi
for
Z
and then applying Young’s inequality to (
D.1
), we can obtain,

(197)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]=O_{\prec}\left(N^{-n}\right).

By Markov inequality, we obtain (
155
) from (
197
).

For the proof of (
154
) and (
156
)-(
158
), we only give the constructions of
Z
.

Define

\displaystyle Z=[{P}_{t}]_{ii}-{\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}(1-[P_{t}]_{ii}),

Then following the same argument in the proof of (
155
), we have

\displaystyle[{P}_{t}]_{ii}-{\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}(1-[P_{t}]_{ii})=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

Plugging (
218
) into the above estimate and then solving for
[P_{t}]_{ii}
, we can obtain (
154
).

Define

\displaystyle Z=[{P}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}]_{ii}-\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right)([{G}{P}_{t}]_{ii}-[{P}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}]_{ii})-(1-[{P}_{t}]_{ii}){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G},

Then following the same argument in the proof of (
155
), we can obtain (
156
).

Define

\displaystyle Z=[X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{jj}-(1-y_{t})[(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{jj},

Then following similar argument with index
j
replaced by
i
in the proof of (
155
), one can show

\displaystyle|Z|\prec N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Using the fact that
[X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{jj}=1
, we can obtain (
157
).

Define

\displaystyle Z_{1}

\displaystyle=[{X}_{t}{G}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}-\left({\rm tr}{G}-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}\right)\left([\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}-[{W}_{t}{G}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}\right),

\displaystyle Z_{2}

\displaystyle=[{X}_{t}{P}_{t}{G}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}-(1-y_{t})[{W}_{t}{G}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}.

Then following similar argument with index
j
replaced by
i
in the proof of (
155
), one can show

\displaystyle|Z_{1}|\prec N^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad|Z_{2}|\prec N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Using the fact that

{X}_{t}{G}{W}^{\prime}_{t}={X}_{t}{P}_{t}{G}{W}^{\prime}_{t},

and then subtracting

from

, we can obtain

\displaystyle(1-y_{t})[{W}_{t}{G}{W}_{t}^{\prime}]_{jj}-\left({\rm tr}{G}-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}\right)\left([\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}-[{W}_{t}{G}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}\right)=O_{\prec}(N^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

D.2.
Proof of Lemma
C.2

We show the proof of (
159
) with
\mathcal{W}_{t}=1
. The proof can be easily generalised to the case with bounded
\mathcal{W}_{t}
. With certain abuse of notation, we recycle the notation
Z
to denote

Define

\displaystyle Z=\sum_{t=1}^{k}[P_{t}G]_{ii}-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)(G_{ii}-[P_{t}G]_{ii}),

and set

\displaystyle\mathsf{Z}^{p,q}=(Z\cdot\Xi)^{p}(\bar{Z}\cdot\Xi)^{q}.

Then by the cumulant expansion, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}X^{\prime}_{t,ij}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)(G_{ii}-[P_{t}G]_{ii})\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{N}\partial_{t,ji}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\}\bigg]-{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)(G_{ii}-[P_{t}G]_{ii})\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{(s+1)/2}}\right)\partial_{t,ji}^{s}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}\}\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)

(198)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle M_{1}-M_{2}+M_{3}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),

where the error
O_{\prec}(N^{-1})
can be verified similarly to (
131
) by choosing
l
sufficiently large.

Again, similarly to (
129
), via direct calculation, we can write
\partial_{t,ji}Z=[\mathcal{A}]_{ij}
for some matrix
\mathcal{A}\in\mathbb{C}^{N\times p_{t}}
with
\|\mathcal{A}\|\prec 1
, and further
\mathcal{A}
is a finite sum of terms of the form
a_{t}[\mathcal{M}_{t}]_{ij}
with
a_{t}=O_{\prec}(1)
and
\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{M}_{t}\mathcal{M}_{t}^{*}\|\prec 1
. For instance, one such
\mathcal{M}_{t}
in
\mathcal{A}
is
P_{t}G(\sum_{d=1}^{k}b_{d}Q_{d})GW_{t}^{\prime}
for some
|b_{d}|\prec 1
.

Since the complex conjugate takes no effect in the remaining estimations, we drop the complex conjugate for simplicity. Specifically, we will work with
Z^{2n-1}
instead of
\mathsf{Z}^{n-1,n}
in the remaining derivation, for notational brevity. Here again we drop the function
\Xi
first and put it back at the last step, up to negligible error.

\displaystyle M_{1}-M_{2}=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(-\frac{1}{N}[Q_{t}G]_{ii}-\frac{1}{N}[Q_{t}G^{2}]_{ii}\right)Z^{2n-1}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{N}[W_{t}GP_{t}]_{ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}Z^{2n-1}\bigg]

(199)

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{O(1)}{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}Z\right)Z^{2n-2}\bigg]+O_{\prec}(N^{-D}).

for any large
D>0
.
Firstly, we have

(200)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(-\frac{1}{N}[Q_{t}G]_{ii}-\frac{1}{N}[Q_{t}G^{2}]_{ii}\right)\leq\frac{1}{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}Q_{t}G\right\|+\frac{1}{N}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}Q_{t}G^{2}\right\|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),

and

(201)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{N}[W_{t}GP_{t}]_{ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{k}[P_{t}G^{\ast}Q_{t}GP_{t}]_{ii}}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{k}[G^{\ast}Q_{t}G]_{ii}}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Here we used (
144
) and

(202)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{t=1}^{k}[P_{t}G^{\ast}Q_{t}GP_{t}]_{ii}\Big|\leq\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}P_{t}G^{\ast}Q_{t}GP_{t}\Big\|\leq\Big\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}P_{t}\Big\|\max_{d}\|G^{\ast}Q_{d}G\|\prec\|H\|\prec 1.

As we mentioned, all the terms in
\partial_{t,ji}Z
(or
[\mathcal{A}]_{ji}
) is of the format
a_{t}[\mathcal{M}_{t}]_{ij}
with
a_{t}=O_{\prec}(1)
. Since

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{N}[W_{t}G]_{ij}[a_{t}\mathcal{M}_{t}]_{ij}\prec\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{k}[G^{*}Q_{t}G]_{ii}}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{k}[\mathcal{M}_{t}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{t}]_{ii}}.

For the mentioned example
\mathcal{M}_{t}=P_{t}G(\sum_{d=1}^{k}b_{d}Q_{d})GW_{t}^{\prime}=:P_{t}G\widetilde{Q}GW_{t}^{\prime}
, similarly to (
202
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}[\mathcal{M}_{t}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{t}]_{ii}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}[P_{t}GQ_{d}GQ_{t}G^{*}Q_{d}G^{*}P_{t}]_{ii}\leq\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{k}P_{t}G\widetilde{Q}GQ_{t}G^{*}\widetilde{Q}^{*}G^{*}P_{t}\right\|\prec\left\|H\right\|\prec 1.

The same bound applies to other terms in
\mathcal{A}
.
Hence, we have

(203)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}Z=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Therefore, plugging (
200
), (
201
) and (
203
) into (
199
) we have

\displaystyle M_{1}-M_{2}=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)Z^{2n-1}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)Z^{2n-2}\bigg].

Next, for
M_{3}
in the RHS of (
198
), i.e,
s\geq 2
terms, we rewrite

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\partial_{t,ji}^{s}\{[W_{t}G]_{ji}Z^{2n-1}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{r=1}^{s}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}\geq 0,s_{1},\cdots,s_{r}>0\\
s_{0}+s_{1}+\cdots+s_{r}=s\end{subarray}}(\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{0}}[W_{t}G]_{ji})\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{1}}Z\cdots\partial_{t,ji}^{s_{r}}ZZ^{(2n-1-r)\vee 0}

\displaystyle=:\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{r=1}^{s}\mathfrak{b}_{s,r}Z^{(2n-1-r)\vee 0}.

When
s=r
, i.e.,
s_{0}=s_{1}-1=\cdots=s_{r}-1=0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\mathfrak{b}_{s,r}=\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}Z\cdots\partial_{t,ji}Z\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}|[W_{t}G]_{ji}||[\mathcal{A}]_{ji}|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\right).

When
s=r+1
, i.e.,
s_{0}+s_{1}-1+\cdots+s_{r}-1=1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\mathfrak{b}_{s,r}=

\displaystyle\frac{O(1)}{N^{\frac{r+2}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\left((\partial_{t,ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji})\partial_{t,ji}Z\cdots\partial_{t,ji}Z+[W_{t}G]_{ji}\partial^{2}_{t,ji}Z\cdots\partial_{t,ji}Z\right)

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}|[W_{t}G]_{ji}|+\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}|[\mathcal{A}]_{ji}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{k}[G^{*}Q_{t}G]_{ii}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{p_{t}}{N}}+\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{k}[\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}]_{ii}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{p_{t}}{N}}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\right).

When
s-r\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\mathfrak{b}_{s,r}\prec\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{t}}\frac{1}{N}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{r+1}{2}}}\right).

In summary, we have the coefficient of
Z^{2n-1-r}
is of order
O_{\prec}(N^{-(r+1)/2})
. Substituting
Z\cdot\Xi
for
Z
and then applying Young’s inequality as we did in the proof of (
220
), we can get

(204)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}^{n,n}\big]=O_{\prec}\left(N^{-n}\right).

Using Markov’s inequality, we obtain (
159
) from (
204
).

D.3.
Proof of Lemma
C.3

We prove the first estimate in (
165
). To this end, we introduce

(205)

\displaystyle Z_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}[X_{t}G]_{ji}-{\rm tr}P_{t}G\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}+{\rm tr}G\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji},\quad Z_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}[X_{t}P_{t}G]_{ji}-(1-\frac{p_{t}}{N})\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}

and we further set

\displaystyle\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{p,q}=(Z_{1}\cdot\Xi)^{p}(\bar{Z}_{1}\cdot\Xi)^{q},\quad\mathsf{Z}_{2}^{p,q}=(Z_{2}\cdot\Xi)^{p}(\bar{Z}_{2}\cdot\Xi)^{q}.

Our aim is to estimate
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
, and via these estimates we can get an estimate of
\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[W_{t}G]_{ji}
at the end.
For any fixed
n
, and sufficiently large
l
, following similar remainder estimates as (
131
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n,n}\big]=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[X_{t}]_{jd}[G]_{di}\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-1,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[{\rm tr}(I-P_{t})G\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}(\partial_{t,jd}[G]_{di})\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-1,n}\bigg]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[{\rm tr}(I-P_{t})G\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-1,n}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{O(1)}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[G]_{di}\left(\partial_{t,jd}Z_{1}\right)\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-2,n}\bigg]+\frac{O(1)}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[G]_{di}\left(\partial_{t,jd}\bar{Z}_{1}\right)\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-1,n-1}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}\partial^{s}_{t,jd}\left\{[G]_{di}\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-1,n}\right\}\bigg]+O_{\prec}(1)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle T_{11}+T_{12}+T_{13}+T_{14}+T_{15}++O_{\prec}(1).

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}(\partial_{t,jd}[G]_{di})+\left({\rm tr}(I-P_{t})G\right)\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[GP_{t}GW^{\prime}_{t}-G^{2}W^{\prime}_{t}]_{ij}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}\left\|GP_{t}GW^{\prime}_{t}-G^{2}W^{\prime}_{t}\right\|=O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}\right).

This gives

\displaystyle T_{11}+T_{12}={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}\right)\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-1,n}\bigg].

Further, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t,jd}Z_{1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{u=1}^{N}1_{jj}G_{du}-\sum_{u=1}^{N}[X_{t}G(I-P_{t})]_{jd}[W_{t}G]_{ju}-\sum_{u=1}^{N}[X_{t}GW_{t}]_{jj}[(I-P_{t})G]_{du}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{N}([(I-P_{t})GW_{t}^{\prime}]_{dj}+[(I-P_{t})G(I-P_{t})GW_{t}^{\prime}]_{dj})\sum_{u=1}^{N}[W_{t}G]_{ju}

(206)

\displaystyle-\sum_{u=1}^{N}[W_{t}]_{jd}[W_{t}G]_{ju}-\sum_{u=1}^{N}[W_{t}G(I-P_{t})]_{jd}[W_{t}G]_{ju}-\sum_{u=1}^{N}[W_{t}GW_{t}]_{jj}[(I-P_{t})G]_{du}.

By applying Cauchy-Schwarz inequality on each term, and using the boundedness of
\|G\|,\|X_{t}\|
and
\|(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\|
, one can easily get

(207)

\displaystyle\partial_{t,jd}Z_{1}=O_{\prec}(\sqrt{N}).

By direct calculation, for
s>1
, we can also find that
\partial^{s}_{t,jd}Z_{1}
can be bounded by a finite sum of the terms with the following form,

(208)

\displaystyle\sum_{u=1}^{N}(|[A]_{ju}|+|[B]_{du}|)\cdot(|[D]_{jj}|+|[E]_{dj}|)+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{u=1}^{N}[F]_{uj}=O_{\prec}(\sqrt{N})

where
A
,
B
,
D
,
E
and
F
are some matrices bounded by
O_{\prec}(1)
in spectral norm. Here we used Cauchy-Schwarz for the
u
-sum and
v
-sum. This gives, for and fixed
s\geq 1
,

(209)

\displaystyle\partial^{s}_{t,jd}Z_{1}=O_{\prec}(\sqrt{N}).

In addition, by (
206
), it is easy to check that
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[G]_{di}\partial_{t,jd}Z_{1}
is a linear combination of the terms of the following forms

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[G]_{di}\sum_{u=1}^{N}A_{jj}B_{du},\qquad\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[G]_{di}\sum_{u=1}^{N}C_{jd}D_{ju},\qquad\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[G]_{di}E_{jd}\sum_{u=1}^{N}F_{ju}

for some matrices
A,B,C,D,E,F
with bounded operator norm with high probability. Further, we can bound each term as the following,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[G]_{di}\sum_{u=1}^{N}A_{jj}B_{du}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i,u=1}^{N}[B^{\prime}G]_{ui}A_{jj}=O_{\prec}(1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[G]_{di}\sum_{u=1}^{N}C_{jd}D_{ju}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[CG]_{ji}\sum_{u=1}^{N}D_{ju}=O_{\prec}(1)

(210)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}[G]_{di}E_{jd}\sum_{u=1}^{N}F_{uj}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[EG]_{ji}\sum_{u=1}^{N}F_{ju}=O_{\prec}(1),

where we used

\big|\sum_{iu}[B^{\prime}G]_{ui}\big|=\big|\mathds{1}^{\prime}B^{\prime}G\mathds{1}\big|\leq N\|B^{\prime}G\|,

and similar bounds apply to all the other sums in (
210
).
Therefore,

\displaystyle T_{13}={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\big[O_{\prec}\left(1\right)\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-2,n}\big],\quad T_{14}={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\big[O_{\prec}\left(1\right)\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-1,n-1}\big]

Next, we consider
T_{15}
. Since the complex conjugate takes no effect in the remaining estimations, we drop the complex conjugate for simplicity in this local part. Specifically, we will work with
Z_{1}^{2n-1}
instead of
\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n-1,n}
in the following derivation, for notational brevity. Here we also drop the
\Xi
-factors first and put it back at the last step.

For
s\geq 2
, we rewrite

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}\partial^{s}_{t,jd}\left\{[G]_{di}Z_{1}^{2n-1}\right\}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{r=0}^{s}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{0}+s_{1}+\cdots+s_{r}=s\\
s_{0}\geq 0,s_{1},s_{2},\cdots,s_{r}>0\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}\partial^{s_{0}}_{t,jd}[G]_{di}\partial^{s_{1}}_{t,jd}Z_{1}\cdots\partial^{s_{r}}_{t,jd}Z_{1}Z_{1}^{(2n-1-r)\vee 0}\bigg].

When
r=0
, the coefficient of
Z_{1}^{2n-1}
can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}\partial_{t,jd}^{s}[G]_{di}.

By direct calculation, for
s\geq 0
, we find that
\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}|\partial_{t,jd}^{s}[G]_{di}|
can be bounded by a finite sum of the terms with the following form,

(211)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}|[A]_{di}|+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}|[B]_{ji}|=O_{\prec}\left(N^{\frac{3}{2}}\right),

where
A
and
B
are some matrix bounded by
O_{\prec}(1)
in operator norm. Here we used Cauchy-Schwarz for the
d
-sum and
i
-sum for the first and second terms respectively. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}\partial_{t,jd}^{s}[G]_{di}=O_{\prec}(1).

When
r=1
, the coefficient of
Z_{1}^{2n-2}
can be bounded using (
207
) and (
211
). It reads

\displaystyle\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}O\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}\sum_{s_{0}+s_{1}=s}\partial_{t,jd}^{s_{0}}[G]_{di}\partial_{t,jd}^{s_{1}}Z_{1}\prec\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}\frac{\sqrt{N}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}\sum_{s_{0}=1}^{s}\left|\partial_{t,jd}^{s_{0}}[G]_{di}\right|\prec\sqrt{N},

When
r\geq 2
, the coefficient of
Z_{1}^{2n-1-r}
can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}\frac{1}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}\partial_{t,jd}^{s_{0}}[G]_{di}\partial_{t,jd}^{s_{1}}Z_{1}\cdots\partial_{t,jd}^{s_{r}}Z_{1}\prec\sum_{s\geq 2}^{l}\frac{N^{\frac{r}{2}}p_{t}^{r}}{N^{\frac{s+1}{2}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{d=1}^{N}\left|\partial_{t,jd}^{s_{0}}[G]_{di}\right|\prec N.

Therefore, substituting
Z_{1}\cdot\Xi
for
Z_{1}
(up to negligible error), then by Young’s inequality, we can obtain

{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{n,n}\big]=O_{\prec}\left(N^{\frac{2n}{3}}\right).

By similar arguments, we can also obtain

{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathsf{Z}_{2}^{n,n}\big]=O_{\prec}\left(N^{\frac{2n}{3}}\right).

Hence, by Markov inequality, we have

|Z_{1}|,|Z_{2}|\prec N^{\frac{1}{3}}.

Recall the definition in (
205
) and notice the fact
X_{t}G=X_{t}P_{t}G
. Hence, taking the difference between
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
leads to

\displaystyle\left({\rm tr}G+1-{\rm tr}P_{t}G-\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}=O_{\prec}(N^{\frac{1}{3}}).

The coefficient of the sum can be bounded below as follows. By Propositions
4.3
and
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}G+1-{\rm tr}P_{t}G-\frac{p_{t}}{N}=

\displaystyle m_{\boxplus}(z)(1-\omega_{t}(z))-\frac{p_{t}}{N}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)=\frac{\omega_{t}(z)-1}{\omega_{t}(z)}\left(1-\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Further, notice

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\omega_{t}(z)-1}{\omega_{t}(z)}\right|\left(1-\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)>c\left(1-\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right),

for some positive constants
c
when
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2}^{0})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
and
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
. Here we need Lemma
4.5
to get
|\omega_{t}(z)|,|m_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z))|\sim 1
, and then from the fact that
|m_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z))|\sim 1
we can obtain
|\omega_{t}(z)-1|\sim 1
since
m_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z))=y_{t}/(1-\omega_{t}(z))-(1-y_{t})/\omega_{t}(z)
. Therefore, we can obtain

(212)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}G]_{ji}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right).

Define

\displaystyle Z=\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}^{\prime}]_{ij}+{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}[W_{t}^{\prime}]_{ij}.

Then similar to the proof of the first estimate in (
165
), we can get
|Z|\prec N^{\frac{1}{3}}
. This details are omitted. Using the estimate of
\gamma_{t2}
in Lemma
4.4
, we can obtain (
164
).

For the second estimate in (
165
), we consider the two terms separately. For the first term, we can write
\sum_{i=1}^{N}[P_{t}GW_{t}^{\prime}]_{ij}
as
\sum_{i=1}^{N}[X_{t}^{\prime}W_{t}GW_{t}^{\prime}]_{ij}
and conduct the cumulant expansion w.r.t.
X_{t}^{\prime}
-entries, and the remaining estimate is similar to the first estimate in (
165
). For the second term
\sum_{i=1}^{N}({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G)[(I-P_{t})GW_{t}^{\prime}]_{ij}+{\rm tr}Q_{t}G[W_{t}^{\prime}]_{ij}
, we can use (
212
), (
164
), the estimate of
\gamma_{t2}
in Lemma
4.4
and Proposition
5.1
directly. The details are omitted. Therefore, we completed the proof of Lemma
C.3
.

D.4.
Proof of Lemma
C.4

We start from the following identity,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}xL_{N}^{1}(f)}\big\rangle\Big]=0

since
{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}=p_{t}
almost surely.
By the cumulant expansion, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}\big\langle{\rm e}^{ixL_{N}^{1}(f)}\big\rangle\Big]={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}{X}_{t,ij}[{W}_{t}]_{ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]=\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}(\partial_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}]_{ji})\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}xL_{n}(f)}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[{W}_{t}]_{ji}\partial_{t,ji}{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]+\frac{1}{{N}^{3/2}}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{3}^{t,j}\sum_{a=0}^{2}{2\choose a}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[(\partial^{a}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}]_{ji})\partial^{2-a}_{t,ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{{N}^{2}}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\kappa_{4}^{t,j}\sum_{a=0}^{3}{3\choose a}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[(\partial^{a}_{t,ji}[{W}_{t}]_{ji})\partial^{3-a}_{t,ji}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]+O_{\prec}\left({\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}}\right)

(213)

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{P}_{t1}+\mathcal{P}_{t2}+\mathcal{P}_{t3}+\mathcal{P}_{t4}+O_{\prec}\left({\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}}\right),

where the remainder terms are estimated similarly to the estimates of
\mathsf{E}_{a},a=1,2,3
in Lemma
5.4
. The estimation for
\mathcal{P}_{t3}
and
\mathcal{P}_{t4}
are also similar to
\mathsf{I}_{t3}
and
\mathsf{I}_{t4}
in (
179
) and (
189
). From which we can obtain,

(214)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}_{t3}+\mathcal{P}_{t4}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{7/6}}\right)}.

Next we estimate
\mathcal{P}_{t1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{t2}
. For
\mathcal{P}_{t1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{t1}=

\displaystyle\left(1-y_{t}-{\frac{1}{N}}\right){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]=\left(1-y_{t}\right){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

(215)

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\big\langle{\rm tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\big\rangle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\Big]=\left(1-y_{t}\right){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),

where in the last step we used (
218
). For
\mathcal{P}_{t2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{t2}=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{i}x}{N}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\sum_{ij}^{(t)}[{W}_{t}]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}L_{N}^{1}(f)\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-x}{2\pi N}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[{W}_{t}]_{ji}\left(\partial_{t,ji}{\rm Tr}{G}(\tilde{z})\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]f(\tilde{z}){\rm d}\tilde{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-x}{2\pi N}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[{W}_{t}]_{ji}\left(2[W_{t}G^{2}(\tilde{z})]_{ji}-2[W_{t}G^{2}(\tilde{z})P_{t}]_{ji}\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]f(\tilde{z}){\rm d}\tilde{z}

(216)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-x}{2\pi N}\oint_{\bar{\gamma}_{1}^{0}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[\left(2{\rm tr}G^{2}(\tilde{z})Q_{t}-2{\rm tr}P_{t}G^{2}(\tilde{z})Q_{t}\right)\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]f(\tilde{z}){\rm d}\tilde{z}=0.

Hence, by plugging (
215
), (
216
) and (
214
) into (
213
), we obtain

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]=(1-y_{t}){\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\big\langle{\rm e}^{\mathrm{i}x\langle L_{N}^{1}(f)\rangle}\big\rangle\Big]+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{7/6}}\right)}.

This verifies (
166
)
Next, we can start with another identity,

{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}\big\langle{\rm e}^{ixL_{n}(f)}\big\rangle\Big]={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}\big\langle{\rm e}^{ixL_{n}(f)}\big\rangle\Big].

By performing cumulant expansion at the LHS of the above identity, and then following the same argument as the estimations of
\mathcal{P}_{tb}
,
b=1,2,3,4
, we can obtain (
167
).

Lastly, we consider (
168
). By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\left|\partial_{t,ji}^{2}{\rm Tr}{G}(z)\right|=2\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\left|\partial_{z}\left\{\partial_{t,ji}\left([{W}_{t}{G}(z)(I-{P}_{t})]_{ji}\right)\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\left|\partial_{z}\left\{\left([({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{jj}-[{W}_{t}{{G}}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}\right)[(I-{P}_{t}){{G}}(I-{P}_{t})]_{ii}-[{W}_{t}{{G}}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}[I-{P}_{t}]_{ii}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle+\text{sum of off-diagonal entries}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\left|\partial_{z}\left\{\left([({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}]_{jj}-[{W}_{t}{{G}}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}\right)[(I-{P}_{t}){{G}}(I-{P}_{t})]_{ii}-[{W}_{t}{{G}}{W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}[I-{P}_{t}]_{ii}\right\}\right|+O_{\prec}\left(p_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\left|\partial_{z}\left\{\frac{N}{p_{t}}\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)({\rm tr}G-{\rm tr}P_{t}G)-\frac{N(1-y_{t})}{p_{t}}{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right\}\right|+O_{\prec}(\sqrt{N}p_{t})=O_{\prec}(\sqrt{N}p_{t}).

where we used the tracial quantities to replace the
ii
and
jj
entries (c.f. Propositions
4.3
,
5.1
and
5.2
) such that the error
O_{\prec}(\sqrt{N}p_{t})=\sum_{ij}^{(t)}O_{\prec}(1/\sqrt{N})
emerges here. In the last step, we used the estimate of
\gamma_{t1}
in Lemma
4.4
. Here we also omitted the expression of the sum of off-diagonal entries, whose estimate can be done similarly to (
141
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
C.4
.

D.5.
Proof of Lemma
C.5

Let
\Delta_{1}:=(x-X_{t,ij})E_{ij}
. Here
E_{ij}
represents the matrix with
ij
entry being
1
and the other entries are
0
. We have

\displaystyle X_{t}^{(ij)}\left(X_{t}^{(ij)}\right)^{\prime}=(X_{t}+\Delta_{1})(X_{t}^{\prime}+\Delta_{1}^{\prime})=:X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime}+\tilde{\Delta}_{1}.

The spectral norm of the matrix
\tilde{\Delta}_{1}
can be bounded as follows,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\Delta}_{1}\|\lesssim\|X_{t}\|\|\Delta_{1}\|+\|\Delta_{1}\|^{2}\prec|x|+\big|X_{t,ij}\big|

where we used Lemma
A.3
.
Therefore, when
|x|\leq N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}
, by Weyl’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\sigma_{l}\Big(X_{t}^{(ij)}\Big(X_{t}^{(ij)}\Big)^{\prime}\Big)-\sigma_{l}\Big(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime}\Big)\Big|\leq\|\tilde{\Delta}_{1}\|\prec N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon},\quad l=1,\cdots,p_{t},

which implies the first and second estimates in (
169
).
Similarly, let

\Delta_{2}:=\big(X_{t}^{(ij)}(X_{t}^{(ij)})^{\prime}\big)^{-1}-(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}.

We have

\displaystyle P^{(ij)}_{t}=

\displaystyle(X_{t}^{\prime}+\Delta_{1}^{\prime})\left((X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}+\Delta_{2}\right)(X_{t}+\Delta_{1})=:P_{t}+\tilde{\Delta}_{2}.

Similarly, when
|x|\leq N^{-1/2+\epsilon}
, it is easy to check

\|\tilde{\Delta}_{2}\|\prec N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}.

Therefore, by the Weyl’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big|\sigma_{l}(H-z\mathrm{I})-\sigma_{l}(H^{(t,ij)}-z\mathrm{I})\big|\leq\|\tilde{\Delta}_{2}\|\prec N^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon},\quad l=1,\cdots,N

when
|x|\leq N^{-1/2+\epsilon}
. This completes the proof of (
169
).

Appendix E
Proof of Theorem
1.9
(Cases 1
\&
2)

E.1.
Proof of case 1

Our goal is to show that, for each fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
,

(217)

\displaystyle\max_{t}|\omega_{t}^{c}(z)-\omega_{t}(z)|,\quad\left|m_{N}(z)-m_{\boxplus}(z)\right|\prec\frac{1}{N}.

To this end, we will first establish a perturbed system of (
35
) for
\omega_{t}^{c}(z)
’s, and then show that (
217
) holds for
z
with sufficiently large
\Im z
. Finally, by a continuity argument, we will show that (
217
) holds for each fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
. For notational simplicity, we write
G:=G(z)
, and
m_{N}:=m_{N}(z)
for short, whenever there is no confusion.

By Lemma
4.4
, we have
