(218)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}=\frac{y_{t}}{1-y_{t}}+O_{\prec}\left({\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}}\right).

(219)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}=(1-y_{t}){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}+O_{\prec}\left({\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}}\right),

(220)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}=\left({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right)\left({\rm tr}{G}-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}\right)+O_{\prec}\left({\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}}\right).

Plugging (
218
) and (
219
) into (
220
), and using (
24
),
we obtain, for any
t\in[\![k]\!]
,

\displaystyle 1+\omega_{t}^{c}(z)m_{N}-\left(\frac{y_{t}}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}(1+\omega_{t}^{c}(z)m_{N})\right)(m_{N}-1-\omega_{t}^{c}(z)m_{N})=O_{\prec}\left({\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}}\right),

where we used the trivial bound
|{\rm tr}{G}|,|{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}|\leq 1/\Im z
.
Rearranging the terms, we have

\displaystyle m_{N}+\frac{\omega_{t}^{c}(z)m_{N}(m_{N}-1-\omega_{t}^{c}(z)m_{N})}{1-y_{t}}=O_{\prec}\left({\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}}\right).

Multiplying both sides by
1-y_{t}
, and then dividing both sides by

(221)

\displaystyle\theta_{t}:=m_{N}\omega_{t}^{c}(z)(1-\omega_{t}^{c}(z)),

we have

\displaystyle m_{N}=\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}^{c}(z)}-\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}^{c}(z)}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\theta_{t}|}{\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}}\right)=-\frac{1}{F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{|\theta_{t}|}{\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}}\right).

Using (
25
), we can get

\displaystyle(k-1)F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))-\omega_{1}^{c}(z)-\omega_{2}^{c}(z)-\cdots-\omega_{k}^{c}(z)+z=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{|F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))|}{|\theta_{t}|}{\frac{k-1}{N}\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}}\right),\quad z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}.

Since
k
is fixed in case 1, the
k
-dependence of the RHS can be neglected, i.e.,

\displaystyle(k-1)F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))-\omega_{1}^{c}(z)-\omega_{2}^{c}(z)-\cdots-\omega_{k}^{c}(z)+z=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{|F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))|}{|\theta_{t}|}{\frac{1}{N}}\right),\quad z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}.

The above system of equations for all
t\in[\![k]\!]
forms a perturbed version of (
35
). Here we keep the factor
|F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c})|/|\theta_{t}|
whose bound is a priori unknown. It will be proved that this factor is of order
1
during the stability analysis of the perturbed system. Based on this perturbed system of (
35
), we can proceed to the next step.

\bullet
Sufficiently large
\Im z
.
We start with the regime when
\Im z>0
is sufficiently large.
Recall that by the definition of the reciprocal Stieltjes transform of
\mu_{t}
,
t\in[\![k]\!]
, we have for any
\omega\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
,

(222)

\displaystyle F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega)=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{m_{\mu_{t}}(\omega)}=\omega-y_{t}+\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}{1-y_{t}-\omega}.

Taking derivatives with respect to
\omega
gives

(223)

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega)=1+\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}{(1-y_{t}-\omega)^{2}},

(224)

\displaystyle F^{(n)}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega)=\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})n!}{(1-y_{t}-\omega)^{n+1}},\quad n\geq 2.

To prove (
217
) for
z
with sufficiently large
\Im z
, we first show the following stability result for the system (
4
) at
(\omega_{1}^{c}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}^{c}(z))
for sufficiently large
\Im z
.

Lemma E.1
.

There exists sufficiently large
\eta_{0}
, such that for any
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
with
\Im z\geq\eta_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{k}}(\omega^{c}_{1}(z),\cdots,\omega^{c}_{k}(z))\leq C.

where
C
is a strictly positive constant independent of
\Im z
and
N
.

Proof of Lemma
E.1
.

Recall the definition in (
4
) and (
51
), we have by resolvent identity,

(225)

\displaystyle(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}(\omega_{1}^{c}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}^{c}(z))=\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})+\frac{\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})}{1-\mathds{1}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\mathds{1}^{\prime}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{k}}(\omega^{c}_{1}(z),\cdots,\omega^{c}_{k}(z))\leq\|\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\|_{(\infty,\infty)}+\left\|\frac{D^{-1}(\omega^{c})\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})}{1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\mathds{1}}\right\|_{(\infty,\infty)}.

For the first term, by Lemma
3.5
, we have for sufficiently large
\Im z
,
\Im\omega_{t}^{c}
(
t\in[\![k]\!]
) are also large. As a result,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\|_{(\infty,\infty)}=

\displaystyle\max_{t}\frac{1}{|(k-1)F_{\mu_{t}}^{\prime}(\omega^{c}_{t}(z))|}=\max_{t}(k-1)^{-1}\left|1+\frac{y_{t}(1-y_{t})}{(1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}^{c}(z))^{2}}\right|^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{t}(k-1)^{-1}\left|1+O(|\Im\omega_{t}^{c}(z)|^{-2})\right|^{-1}\leq C

as
\Im z\to\infty
. For the second term, since

\left\|\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\right\|_{(\infty,\infty)}\leq k\left\|\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\right\|^{2}_{(\infty,\infty)}\leq C,

it suffices to show

\left|1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\mathds{1}\right|>c

for some strictly positive constant
c
which is independent of
\Im z
and
N
. We have indeed

\displaystyle\left|1-\mathds{1}^{\prime}\mathcal{D}^{-1}(\omega^{c})\mathds{1}\right|=

\displaystyle\Big|1-\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{F_{\mu_{t}}^{\prime}(\omega^{c}_{t}(z))}\Big|=\Big|1-\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(1+O(|\Im\omega_{t}^{c}(z)|^{-2})\right)^{-1}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{-1}{k-1}+O(\max_{t}|\Im\omega_{t}^{c}(z)|^{-2})\Big|>\frac{1}{2(k-1)}.

when
\Im z
is sufficiently large. This completes the proof of Lemma
E.1
.
∎

Next, we continue the proof of (
217
) in the regime when
\Im z>0
is sufficiently large. From (
224
), we also have for
\omega\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
with large
\Im\omega
,

(226)

\displaystyle|F_{\mu_{t}}^{(2)}(\omega)|=O\left(|\omega|^{-3}\right).

Hence the matrix of second derivatives of
\Phi
given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}^{2}\Phi\left(\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{k}\right):=\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi}{\partial\omega_{1}^{2}},\cdots,\frac{\partial^{2}\Phi}{\partial\omega_{k}^{2}}\right)={\rm diag}\Big((k-1)F_{\mu_{t}}^{(2)}\left(\omega_{t}\right)\Big)_{t=1}^{k}

satisfies
\|\mathrm{D}^{2}\Phi\left({\omega}^{c}_{1}(z),\cdots,{\omega}^{c}_{k}(z)\right)\|=O\left(|\Im z|^{-3}\right)
, as
\Im z\to\infty
. For any
t\in[\![k]\!]
, denote by

(227)

\displaystyle r_{t}(z):=(k-1)F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))-\omega_{1}^{c}(z)-\cdots-\omega_{k}^{c}(z)+z=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{|F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))|}{|\theta_{t}|}{\frac{1}{N}}\right),

and
r(z):=(r_{1}(z),\cdots,r_{k}(z))^{T}
. As
\Im z\to\infty
, by Lemma
3.5
, we have with high probability

(228)

\displaystyle\frac{|\theta_{t}|}{|F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))|}=\left|\frac{m_{N}(y_{t}-1+\omega_{t}^{c}(z))^{2}}{\omega_{t}^{c}(z)(1-\omega_{t}^{c}(z))}\right|=|m_{N}|\left|\frac{(y_{t}-1+\omega_{t}^{c}(z))^{2}}{\omega_{t}^{c}(z)(1-\omega_{t}^{c}(z))}\right|\geq\frac{1}{2}|m_{N}|\gtrsim\frac{1}{\Im z}.

Therefore, we can obtain

|r_{t}(z)|\prec|\Im z|/N

for all
t\in[\![k]\!]
.

Hence, choosing
\eta_{0}>0
sufficiently large, and using Lemma
E.1
with the above bound we can achieve that

\displaystyle s_{0}:=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{k}}(\omega_{1}^{c}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}^{c}(z))\|\mathrm{D}^{2}\Phi\left({\omega}^{c}_{1}(z),\cdots,{\omega}^{c}_{k}(z)\right)\|_{(\infty,\infty)}\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\Phi(\omega_{1}^{c}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}^{c}(z))\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\mathrm{D}^{2}\Phi\left({\omega}^{c}_{1}(z),\cdots,{\omega}^{c}_{k}(z)\right)\|_{(\infty,\infty)}\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\left({\omega}^{c}_{1}(z),\cdots,{\omega}^{c}_{k}(z)\right)\|_{(\infty,\infty)}\|r(z)\|_{\infty}\prec\frac{1}{N|\Im z|^{2}},

which implies that
s_{0}<\frac{1}{2}
with high probability on the domain
\{z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}:\Im z\geq\eta_{0}\}
. By the Newton-Kantorovich theorem (Theorem
A.6
) with

b\equiv\Gamma_{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{k}}(\omega_{1}^{c}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}^{c}(z))\|\mathrm{D}^{2}\Phi\left({\omega}^{c}_{1}(z),\cdots,{\omega}^{c}_{k}(z)\right)\|_{(\infty,\infty)},

and
L\equiv\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\Phi(\omega_{1}^{c}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}^{c}(z))\|_{\infty}
, we have that there are for every such
z
unique
\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)
’s satisfying

\Phi_{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{k}}(\hat{\omega}_{1}(z),\cdots,\hat{\omega}_{k}(z),z)=0,

with

\displaystyle\left|{\omega}^{c}_{t}(z)-\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1-\sqrt{1-2s_{0}}}{s_{0}}\|(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}(\omega_{1}^{c}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}^{c}(z))\|_{(\infty,\infty)}\|r(z)\|_{\infty}

(229)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|r(z)\|_{\infty}\prec{\frac{|\Im z|}{N}}.

Finally, using Lemma
3.5
, we note that
\Im\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)=\Im\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)-\Im{\omega}^{c}_{t}(z)+\Im{\omega}^{c}_{t}(z)\geq\Im z
for
\Im z\geq\eta_{0}
with high probability as
N\to\infty
. It further follows that

\Gamma_{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{k}}(\hat{\omega}_{1}(z),\cdots,\hat{\omega}_{k}(z))\neq 0
for all
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
with
\Im z\geq\eta_{0}
. Thus
\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)
is analytic on the domain
\{z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}:\Im z\geq\eta_{0}\}
since
F_{\mu_{t}}
is. Finally, using (
222
) with
\omega=\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)
, we see that

(230)

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\to\infty}\frac{\Im\hat{\omega}(\mathrm{i}\eta)}{\mathrm{i}\eta}=1.

Thus by the uniqueness claim in Proposition
1.5
,
\hat{\omega}_{t}(z)
agrees with
\omega_{t}(z)
on the domain
\{z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}:\Im z\geq\eta_{0}\}
. Therefore, (
E.1
) implies that

(231)

\displaystyle\left|{\omega}^{c}_{t}(z)-{\omega}_{t}(z)\right|\prec\frac{1}{N},

for all fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
with
\Im z\geq\eta_{0}
. Therefore, subtracting (
25
) from (
11
), we have

(232)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z)-\omega_{t}(z))=-\frac{k-1}{m_{N}(z)}+\frac{k-1}{m_{\boxplus}(z)}=(k-1)\frac{m_{\boxplus}(z)-m_{N}(z)}{m_{N}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}.

As a result,

(233)

\displaystyle\left|m_{N}(z)-m_{\boxplus}(z)\right|\prec\frac{1}{k-1}\sum_{t=1}^{k}|\omega_{t}^{c}(z)-\omega_{t}(z)|\prec\frac{1}{N},

for all fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
with
\Im z\geq\eta_{0}
.

Next, taking (
233
) as an input, we can use the continuity argument to obtain the bound for each fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
.

\bullet

Any fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
.

For any fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, we have the following stability of the system (
35
) at
(\omega_{1}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}(z))
, which is an extension of Lemma
E.1
.

Lemma E.2
.

For any fixed
k\in\mathbb{N}
, and any fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, let
\omega_{t}(z),t\in[\![k]\!]
be the subordination functions. Then there is a strictly positive constant
C
independent of
N
such that

(234)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{k}}(\omega_{1}(z),\omega_{2}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}(z))\leq C,

and

(235)

\displaystyle|\omega_{t}^{\prime}(z)|\leq C,\quad t\in[\![k]\!].

Here the constant
C
may depend on
z
.

Proof of Lemma
E.2
.

To prove (
234
), by Cramer’s rule, it suffices to show that

|\det(\mathrm{D}\Phi)|>c

for some constant
c>0
. By basic algebra, we have

\displaystyle|\det(\mathrm{D}\Phi)|=

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{r=0}^{k}(r-1)(k-1)^{k-r-1}\sum_{i_{1}\neq\ldots\neq i_{r}}f_{i_{1}}\cdots f_{i_{r}}\bigg|

(236)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(k-1)^{k-1}-\sum_{r=1}^{k}(r-1)(k-1)^{k-r-1}\sum_{i_{1}\neq\ldots\neq i_{r}}|f_{i_{1}}|\cdots|f_{i_{r}}|

where
f_{t}:=\left(k-1\right)\left(F_{\mu_{t}}^{\prime}(\omega_{t}(z))-1\right)
.

By (
10
) and (
11
), we have,

\displaystyle\left|f_{t}\right|=(k-1)\left|\frac{\Im F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z))-\Im\omega_{t}(z)}{\Im\omega_{t}(z)}\right|=\frac{\sum_{s\neq t}\Im\omega_{s}(z)-(k-2)\Im\omega_{t}(z)}{\Im\omega_{t}(z)}-\frac{\Im z}{\Im\omega_{t}(z)},\quad t\in[\![k]\!].

For any fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, we have by
\Im\omega_{t}(z)\geq\Im z>0
,

\displaystyle\left|f_{t}\right|<\frac{\sum_{s\neq t}\Im\omega_{s}(z)-(k-2)\Im\omega_{t}(z)}{\Im\omega_{t}(z)}=:a_{t},\quad t\in[\![k]\!].

This together with (
236
) implies

\displaystyle\left|\det\left(\mathrm{D}\Phi\right)\right|>

\displaystyle(k-1)^{k-1}-\sum_{r=1}^{k}(r-1)(k-1)^{k-r-1}\sum_{t_{1}\neq\ldots\neq t_{r}}a_{t_{1}}\cdots a_{t_{r}}=\det\Big({\rm diag}(a_{t}+k-1)_{t=1}^{k}-\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\prod_{t}^{k}\Im\omega_{t}(z)}\det\begin{bmatrix}\sum_{t\neq 1}\Im\omega_{t}(z)\ &-\Im\omega_{1}(z)&\dots&-\Im\omega_{1}(z)\\
-\Im\omega_{2}(z)&\sum_{t\neq 2}\Im\omega_{t}(z)&\dots&-\Im\omega_{2}(z)\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
-\Im\omega_{k}(z)&-\Im\omega_{k}(z)&\dots&\sum_{t\neq k}\Im\omega_{t}(z)\end{bmatrix}=0,

where the last step follows from the fact that the matrix has linearly dependent columns. Since here our
z
and
k
are fixed, it is easy to show the positiveness of
|\det({\rm D}\Phi)|
is effective, i.e.,
|\det({\rm D}\Phi)|>c
for some positive constant
c
which may depend on
k
and
z
. This completes the proof of (
234
).

The estimates in (
235
) follow by differentiating the equation (
35
) with respect to
z
, we get

(237)

\displaystyle\mathrm{D}\Phi\cdot\omega^{\prime}(z)=\mathds{1},

where
\omega^{\prime}(z):=(\omega_{1}^{\prime}(z),\cdots,\omega_{k}^{\prime}(z))
. Together with (
234
), we get (
235
) by inverting (
237
).

∎

To prove (
217
) for each fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, we start from
z_{0}=\Re z+\mathrm{i}\eta_{0}
with sufficiently large
\eta_{0}>\Im z
, and decrease the imaginary part step by step with a step size
N^{-2}
, so that
z_{0}
goes to
z
eventually after
O(N^{2})
steps. We aim to show that the bound (
217
) remains hold after each step, by using the continuity of the subordination functions and the Stieltjes transforms. In the sequel we show the details of this continuity argument for the first step. The remaining steps are the same. Let
z_{1}=\Re z+\mathrm{i}(\eta_{0}-N^{-2})
.

In the sequel, till Section
E.2
, all the omitted
z
-variables are
z_{1}
. Notice that for the perturbed system

\displaystyle(k-1)F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))-\omega_{1}^{c}(z_{1})-\cdots-\omega_{k}^{c}(z_{1})+z_{1}=r_{t}(z_{1}).

Let
\Omega_{t}:=\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{1})
, performing Taylor expansion for
F_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))
around
\omega_{t}(z_{1})
we get

\displaystyle(k-1)F^{\prime}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))\Omega_{t}-\Omega_{1}-\Omega_{2}-\cdots-\Omega_{k}=r_{t}(z_{1})-(k-1)\sum_{n\geq 2}\frac{1}{n!}F_{\mu_{t}}^{(n)}(\omega_{t}(z_{1}))\Omega_{t}^{n}.

Then with (
4
) we have

\displaystyle\Omega=(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot r(z_{1})-\sum_{n\geq 2}\frac{k-1}{n!}(\mathrm{D}\Phi)^{-1}\cdot\Omega_{F}^{n}

where
\Omega:=(\Omega_{1},\ldots,\Omega_{k})
and
\Omega_{F}^{n}:=\left(F_{\mu_{1}}^{(n)}(\omega_{1}(z_{1}))\Omega_{1}^{n},\cdots,F_{\mu_{k}}^{(n)}(\omega_{k}(z_{1}))\Omega_{k}^{n}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}
. Taking
\ell_{\infty}
norm on both sides, and using Lemma
E.2
and (
224
), we have

(238)

\displaystyle\|\Omega\|_{\infty}\leq C\|r(z_{1})\|_{\infty}+C\sum_{n\geq 2}\max_{t}\frac{\|\Omega\|_{\infty}^{n}}{|1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}(z_{1})|^{n}}.

Since
\Im\omega_{t}(z)\geq\Im z>0
, we have
|1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}(z_{1})|\geq|\Im\omega_{t}(z_{1})|>0
. Also, by the definition of
\omega_{t}^{c}(z)
(c.f., (
24
)), we have

\displaystyle|(\omega_{t}^{c}(z))|=\left|\frac{{\rm tr}G(z){\rm tr}P_{t}G^{2}(z)-({\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)-1){\rm tr}G^{2}(z)}{({\rm tr}G(z))^{2}}\right|\leq C

with high probability. Here we used the fact that
|{\rm tr}G(z)|\sim 1
when
\Im z>0
and
\|H\|
is bounded with high probability (c.f., Lemma
4.2
).
Therefore, together with (
231
) at
z_{0}
and the continuity of
\omega_{t}
(c.f. (
235
)), we have for
z_{1}
,

(239)

\displaystyle|\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{1})|\prec\frac{1}{N}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}.

More precisely, by the definition of the stochastic domination, (
239
) implies that for any large
D>0
and small
\epsilon>0
, there exists an high probability event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\equiv\mathcal{E}(z_{1},\epsilon,D)
, satisfying
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1}^{c})<N^{-D}
, such that on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle|\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1})-\omega_{t}(z_{1})|\leq N^{\epsilon}(N^{-1}+N^{-2})\leq 2N^{-1+\epsilon},

for any small
\epsilon>0
. Together with the fact that
\Im\omega_{t}(z_{1})>0
, (
238
) can be rewritten as

(240)

\displaystyle\|\Omega\|_{\infty}\leq C\|r(z_{1})\|_{\infty}+C\|\Omega\|_{\infty}^{2}\quad\text{on}\quad\mathcal{E}_{1}.

As
N\to\infty
, we can absorb the quadratic term into the left hand side, which gives

\displaystyle\|\Omega\|_{\infty}\leq C\|r(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\quad\text{on}\quad\mathcal{E}_{1}.

Recall the definition of
r(z)
in (
227
), we have there exists an high probability event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, such that

(241)

\displaystyle\|r(z_{1})\|_{\infty}\leq N^{\epsilon}\left(\frac{|F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))|}{|\theta_{t}|}{\frac{1}{N}}\right)

Further notice that, on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we have

(242)

\displaystyle\frac{|\theta_{t}(z_{1})|}{|F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))|}=|m_{N}(z_{1})|\left|\frac{(y_{t}-1+\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))^{2}}{\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1})(1-\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))}\right|=|m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})|\left|\frac{(y_{t}-1+\omega_{t}(z_{1}))^{2}}{\omega_{t}(z_{1})(1-\omega_{t}(z_{1}))}\right|+O\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-\epsilon}}\right).

Together with the facts that

|y_{t}-1+\omega_{t}(z_{1})|\geq\Im\omega_{t}(z_{1})>0
with
|m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})|\geq\Im m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})>0
,
we know

|\theta_{t}(z_{1})|/|F^{2}_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}^{c}(z_{1}))|>c>0,

on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
.

Therefore, combining (
240
), (
241
) and (
242
), we have on the event
\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\mathcal{E}_{2}
,

(243)

\displaystyle\|\Omega\|_{\infty}\leq CN^{1-\epsilon}.

Repeating the above procedure for
O(N^{2})
steps, we will generate a series of events, i.e.,
\mathcal{E}_{1},\cdots,\mathcal{E}_{CN^{2}}
. Therefore, we have on the event
\bigcap_{i=1}^{O(N^{2})}\mathcal{E}_{i}
, (
243
) still holds. Since all these events are high probability events, we obtain

(244)

\displaystyle\|\Omega\|_{\infty}\prec{\frac{1}{N}}.

Using (
232
), we can again have

(245)

\displaystyle|m_{N}(z_{1})-m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})|\prec\frac{1}{N}.

Now, further by
[
10
]
, we know that
\mu_{1}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus\mu_{k}\Rightarrow\mu_{1}^{\infty}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus\mu_{k}^{\infty}
when
k
is fixed. By Stieltjes continuity theorem, this implies
m_{\boxplus}(z)\to m_{\boxplus}^{\infty}(z)
for any fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, where
m_{\boxplus}^{\infty}(z)
is the Stieltjes transform of the limiting measure
\mu_{1}^{\infty}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus\mu_{k}^{\infty}
. This together with
|m_{N}(z)-m_{\boxplus}(z)|\prec\frac{1}{N}
implies the convergence of
m_{N}(z)
to
m_{\boxplus}^{\infty}(z)
with high probability. Using the Stieltjes continuity theorem in orther direction, we can conclude that
\mu_{N}
converges weakly in probability to
\mu_{1}^{\infty}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus\mu_{k}^{\infty}
.

E.2.
Proof of case 2

Recall the crude bounds
in Lemma
A.3
. Further, by setting
\mathcal{W}_{t}=1
in Lemma
4.4
, we have,

(246)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\Big({\rm tr}P_{t}G-\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right)\left({\rm tr}G-{\rm tr}P_{t}G\right)\Big)=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{{N}}\right),

(247)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\rm tr}P_{t}-{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\Big)=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{{N}}\right),

(248)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\Big(\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\rm tr}P_{t}GP_{t}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\Big)=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{{N}}\right).

We note here in (
248
),
{\rm tr}P_{t}GP_{t}
is actually
{\rm tr}P_{t}G
. However, we keep this form since only starting with
{\rm tr}P_{t}GP_{t}
, and then applying the cumulant expansion we can see an (almost) algebraic cancellation with
{\rm tr}Q_{t}G
.

Applying Lemma
A.3
to absorb the
O_{\prec}(p_{t}/N)
terms into error term in (
246
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\rm tr}P_{t}G-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right){\rm tr}G=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N}\right).

Plugging (
247
) and (
248
) into above equation with the trivial fact that
{\rm tr}P_{t}GP_{t}={\rm tr}P_{t}G
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\rm tr}P_{t}G-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(\frac{y_{t}}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\rm tr}P_{t}G\right){\rm tr}G=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N}\right).

In light of the smallness of
y_{t}
’s in case 2 of Theorem
1.9
, we can also rewrite the above as

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\rm tr}P_{t}G-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(y_{t}-{\rm tr}P_{t}G\right){\rm tr}G=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N}\right).

Further using the trivial identity

\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\rm tr}P_{t}G={\rm tr}HG=1+z{\rm tr}G,

we arrive at

\displaystyle 1+(z-y+1)m_{N}+zm_{N}^{2}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N}\right).

By the stability of the quadratic equations, we can conclude that

\displaystyle|m_{N}(z)-m_{y}(z)|\prec\frac{p_{\max}}{N}.

Here
m_{y}(z)
is the Stieljes transform of the
Machenko-Pastur law

\mu_{mp,y}
(c.f. Theorem
1.9
) which satisfying the following quadratic equation

(249)

\displaystyle 1+(z-y+1)m_{y}(z)+zm^{2}_{y}(z)=0,\quad z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}.

This immediately implies the weak convergence (in probability) of
\mu_{N}
to
\mu_{mp,\hat{y}}
, in light of the well known fact that
\mu_{mp,y}
converges to
\mu_{mp,\hat{y}}
weakly.

Appendix F
Proofs of Corollaries
1.14
and
1.15

F.1.
Simplification of variance and expectation: Proofs of (
108
) and (
115
)

We first show the derivation of (
104
). Recall Proposition
1.5
in the main text, we have

\displaystyle m_{\boxplus}(z)=m_{\mu_{t}}(\omega_{t}(z)),\quad t\in[\![k]\!],\quad\omega_{1}(z)+\omega_{2}(z)+\cdots+\omega_{k}(z)=z-\frac{k-1}{m_{\boxplus}(z)}.

For brevity, we omit
z
from the notation in the sequel. Taking derivative w.r.t
z
, we have

(250)

\displaystyle m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}=\frac{y_{t}\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{(1-\omega_{t})^{2}}+\frac{(1-y_{t})\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{\omega_{t}^{2}},\quad t\in[\![k]\!],

(251)

\displaystyle\omega_{1}^{\prime}+\omega_{2}^{\prime}+\cdots+\omega_{k}^{\prime}=1+\frac{(k-1)m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}.

Solving
\omega_{t}^{\prime}
in (
250
) and then plugging the results into (
251
), we have

(252)

\displaystyle m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}^{2}(1-\omega_{t})^{2}}{y_{t}\omega_{t}^{2}+(1-y_{t})(1-\omega_{t})^{2}}\right)=1+\frac{(k-1)m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}.

Notice that

\displaystyle y_{t}\omega_{t}^{2}+(1-y_{t})(1-\omega_{t})^{2}=

\displaystyle\omega_{t}^{2}+(1-y_{t})(1-2\omega_{t})=\omega_{t}^{2}+\omega_{t}(1-m_{\boxplus}(1-\omega_{t}))(1-2\omega_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{t}(1-\omega_{t})(1-m_{\boxplus}+2m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}),

then (
252
) becomes

(253)

\displaystyle m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}\left(-\mathfrak{u}+\mathfrak{n}\right)=1+\frac{(k-1)m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}},

where we denote

\displaystyle\mathfrak{u}:=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}}{m_{\boxplus}-1-2m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}},\quad\mathfrak{n}:=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}^{2}}{m_{\boxplus}-1-2m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}}.

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{u}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}}{m_{\boxplus}}-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(\frac{\omega_{t}}{m_{\boxplus}}-\frac{\omega_{t}}{m_{\boxplus}-1-2\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus}}\right)=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}}{m_{\boxplus}}+\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\mathfrak{u}+2\mathfrak{n},

which gives

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\right)\mathfrak{u}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}}{m_{\boxplus}}+2\mathfrak{n}=\frac{zm_{\boxplus}-(k-1)}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}+2\mathfrak{n}.

Combining the above equation with (
253
), we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}-\frac{k-1}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}=\mathfrak{u}-\mathfrak{n}=\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\mathfrak{u}+\mathfrak{n}+\frac{zm_{\boxplus}-(k-1)}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}+2\mathfrak{n}.

Rearranging the terms, we arrive at

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\mathfrak{u}+\mathfrak{n}+\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}z=-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}.

This implies

\displaystyle\left(z-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{y_{t}-1-\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus}}{m_{\boxplus}-1-2\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus}}\right)^{-1}=\left(z+\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}+m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}^{2}}{m_{\boxplus}-1-2\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus}}\right)^{-1}=\left(z+\mathfrak{u}+m_{\boxplus}\mathfrak{n}\right)^{-1}=\frac{-m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}.

Next we show the proof of (83). For brevity, we denote
m_{\boxplus i}:=m_{\boxplus}(z_{i}),i=1,2
, and
\omega_{ti}:=\omega_{t}(z_{i}),i=1,2,t\in[\![k]\!]
in the following derivation. Define

\displaystyle V_{t}(z_{1},z_{2}):=-\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus 1}}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\frac{1+\frac{(\omega_{t1}m_{\boxplus 1}-\omega_{t2}m_{\boxplus 2})^{2}}{(z_{1}-z_{2})(m_{\boxplus 1}-m_{\boxplus 2})}+\frac{\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2}}{\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}}}}{m_{\boxplus 1}-1-2\omega_{t1}m_{\boxplus 1}}

From (
251
), we have

(254)

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus 1}(m_{\boxplus 1}-1-2\omega_{t1}m_{\boxplus 1})}=-\frac{\omega_{t1}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus 1}\omega_{t1}(1-\omega_{t1})}.

Using (
254
), we obtain

\displaystyle V_{t}(z_{1},z_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus 1}}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\frac{(\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2})m_{\boxplus 1}m_{\boxplus 2}}{(m_{\boxplus 1}-1-2\omega_{t1}m_{\boxplus 1})(m_{\boxplus 1}-m_{\boxplus 2})}-\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus 1}}{m_{\boxplus 1}(m_{\boxplus 1}-1-2\omega_{t1}m_{\boxplus 1})}

(255)

\displaystyle+\frac{\omega_{t1}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\frac{(\omega_{t1}m_{\boxplus 1}-\omega_{t2}m_{\boxplus 2})(\omega_{t1}m_{\boxplus 1}-\omega_{t2}m_{\boxplus 2}-m_{\boxplus 1}+m_{\boxplus 2})}{\omega_{t1}(1-\omega_{t1})(z_{1}-z_{2})(m_{\boxplus 1}-m_{\boxplus 2})}.

By some elementary algebra with (
254
) and the fact
\omega_{t1}-m_{\boxplus 1}(\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t1}^{2})=\omega_{t2}-m_{\boxplus 2}(\omega_{t2}-\omega_{t2}^{2})=1-y_{t}
, we can rewrite (
255
) as

\displaystyle V_{t}(z_{1},z_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus 1}}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\frac{m_{\boxplus 2}(\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2})}{(z_{1}-z_{2})(m_{\boxplus 1}-m_{\boxplus 2})}-\frac{m_{\boxplus 2}}{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus 1}m_{\boxplus 1}(m_{\boxplus 1}-m_{\boxplus 2})}+\frac{\omega^{\prime}_{t1}}{\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2}}-\frac{\omega^{\prime}_{t1}}{z_{1}-z_{2}}.

Summing over
t
and then taking derivative w.r.t
z_{2}
, we obtain (83).

Next, we consider the simplification of the expectation. Recall from the main text the expression of
E(z)
,

(256)

\displaystyle E(z)=\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}(z)}{m_{\boxplus}(z)}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}\Big[{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}^{2}\Big]}{m_{\boxplus}(z)-1-2\omega_{t}(z)m_{\boxplus}(z)}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right).

For brevity, we omit
z
from the notations in the sequel.

Further, by Proposition 6.1, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}\Big]=\frac{(1+\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus})(m_{\boxplus}-1-\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus})-(m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}^{2}+\omega_{t})(\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus})^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}-1-2m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}}+O_{\prec}\left({\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}}\right),

(257)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}^{2}\Big]=(\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus})^{\prime}+O_{\prec}\left({\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}}\right).

Therefore, substituting (
254
) with
m_{\boxplus 1}
replaced by
m_{\boxplus}
and (
257
) in (
256
), we have for any fixed
t\in[\![k]\!]
,

\displaystyle E(z)=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\frac{1}{\omega_{t}(1-\omega_{t})}\bigg[\frac{(1+\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus})(m_{\boxplus}-1-\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus})-(m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}^{2}+\omega_{t})(\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus})^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}-1-2m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}}-(\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus})^{\prime}\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\frac{1}{\omega_{t}(1-\omega_{t})}\bigg[1-\omega_{t}^{\prime}-\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\left(m_{\boxplus}^{2}+m_{\boxplus}\frac{(\omega_{t}-1)(\omega_{t}^{\prime}m_{\boxplus}+m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}\omega_{t})+\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{\omega_{t}}\right)\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

Further using

\displaystyle\frac{(\omega_{t}-1)(\omega_{t}^{\prime}m_{\boxplus}+m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}\omega_{t})+\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{\omega_{t}}=-m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}^{\prime},

we have

\displaystyle E(z)=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\frac{1}{\omega_{t}(1-\omega_{t})}\bigg[1-\omega_{t}^{\prime}-\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\left(m_{\boxplus}^{2}-m_{\boxplus}^{2}\omega_{t}^{\prime}\right)\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\frac{1}{\omega_{t}(1-\omega_{t})}\bigg[(1-\omega_{t}^{\prime})(2m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}+1-m_{\boxplus})

\displaystyle\qquad+(1-\omega_{t}^{\prime})(m_{\boxplus}-2m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t})-\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\left(m_{\boxplus}^{2}-m_{\boxplus}^{2}\omega_{t}^{\prime}\right)\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}(1-\omega_{t}^{\prime})+\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\frac{(1-\omega_{t}^{\prime})m_{\boxplus}}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}\omega_{t}(1-\omega_{t})}\bigg[m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}-2\omega_{t}m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}-\omega_{t}^{\prime}m_{\boxplus}\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}(1-\omega_{t}^{\prime})+\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\frac{(1-\omega_{t}^{\prime})m_{\boxplus}}{2m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}\left(\frac{m_{\boxplus}-1-2m_{\boxplus}\omega_{t}}{\omega_{t}(1-\omega_{t})}\right)^{\prime}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}(1-\omega_{t}^{\prime})-\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}\frac{(1-\omega_{t}^{\prime})m_{\boxplus}}{2m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}\left(\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}\right)^{\prime}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}(1-\omega_{t}^{\prime})\left(\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}}-\frac{m^{\prime\prime}_{\mu}}{2m^{\prime}_{\mu}}\right)+\frac{\omega^{\prime\prime}_{t}}{2\omega^{\prime}_{t}}-\frac{\omega^{\prime\prime}_{t}}{2}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega^{\prime\prime}_{t}}{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}+(k-1)\left(\frac{2m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}}{m_{\boxplus}}-\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}\right)\bigg]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right).

where in the third step we used (
254
). The completes the proof of (90).

F.2.
Proof of Corollary
1.14

F.2.1.
Expectation for Schott’s statistics:
f(x)=x^{2}

Notice that we can first rewrite

\displaystyle a_{x^{2}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi i}\oint_{\gamma}z^{2}\frac{\partial\log\left(\frac{\prod_{t=1}^{k}\omega_{t}^{\prime}(z)}{[(-1/m_{\boxplus})^{\prime}]^{(k-1)}}\right)}{\partial z}{\rm d}z

(258)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi i}\oint_{\gamma}z^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\partial\log\left(\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}m_{\boxplus}^{2}}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}\right)}{\partial z}{\rm d}z-\frac{1}{4\pi i}\oint_{\gamma}z^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\partial\log\left(\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}\right)}{\partial z}{\rm d}z.

By (
9
) and (
12
), we can get

(259)

\displaystyle\omega_{t}=\frac{m_{\boxplus}-1-\sqrt{(m_{\boxplus}+1)^{2}-4y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}}{2m_{\boxplus}},

where the square root takes the branch that
\Im\sqrt{w}>0
when
\Im w>0
.
Here
\omega_{t}
and
m_{\boxplus}
are functions of
z
. Taking derivative with respect to
z
, we have,

(260)

\displaystyle\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{2m_{\boxplus}^{2}}+\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-2y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}{2m_{\boxplus}^{2}\sqrt{(1+m_{\boxplus})^{2}-4y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}}

Summing over index
t
for (
259
) and using (
11
), we have

(261)

\displaystyle z=-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}+\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-\sqrt{(m_{\boxplus}+1)^{2}-4y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}}{2m_{\boxplus}}.

Taking derivative with respect to
z
, we have,

(262)

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}=\frac{1}{1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-2y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}{2\sqrt{(1+m_{\boxplus})^{2}-4y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}}\right)}.

Therefore, substituting (
260
) and (
262
) into (
258
), we have

(263)

\displaystyle a_{x^{2}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi i}\oint_{\gamma}z^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\partial\log\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-2y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}{2\sqrt{(1+m_{\boxplus})^{2}-4y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}}\right)}{\partial z}{\rm d}z

(264)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4\pi i}\oint_{\gamma}z^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\partial\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-2y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}{2\sqrt{(1+m_{\boxplus})^{2}-4y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}}\right)\right)}{\partial z}{\rm d}z.

By choosing
\gamma
so that
|z|
is sufficiently large on
\gamma
, we have that
m_{\boxplus}
is sufficiently small. Then we see that the log functions are analytic on
\gamma
. Further, we introduce the shorthand notations

(265)

\displaystyle g_{t}(m_{\boxplus}):=\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-2y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}{\sqrt{(1+m_{\boxplus})^{2}-4y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}},\qquad\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus}):=\sqrt{(m_{\boxplus}+1)^{2}-4y_{t}m_{\boxplus}}.

Then, for (
264
), performing integration by parts, and using (
261
), (
262
) and
{\rm d}z=m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}dm_{\boxplus}
, we have

\displaystyle a_{x^{2}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}z\log\left(\frac{1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}z\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\left(-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{3}}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-\ell_{s}(m_{\boxplus})}{2m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\right)\log\left(\frac{1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\left(1-\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\left(-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{3}}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-\ell_{s}(m_{\boxplus})}{2m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\right)\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\left(1-\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{s}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus}

(266)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:I_{1}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}I_{2,s}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}I_{3,s}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}I_{4,ij}+J_{1}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}J_{2,s}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}J_{3,s}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}J_{4,ij},

where
\gamma_{m}
is the contour of
m
after change of variable, and

\displaystyle I_{1}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\log\left(\frac{1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{k}{2}\right)dm_{\boxplus},

\displaystyle I_{2,s}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-\ell_{s}(m_{\boxplus})}{2m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\log\left(\frac{1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{k}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus},

\displaystyle I_{3,s}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{g_{s}(m_{\boxplus})}{m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\log\left(\frac{1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus},

\displaystyle I_{4,ij}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{(m_{\boxplus}+1-\ell_{i}(m_{\boxplus}))g_{j}(m_{\boxplus})}{2m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\left(\frac{1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus},

\displaystyle J_{1}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{k}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus},

\displaystyle J_{2,s}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-\ell_{s}(m_{\boxplus})}{2m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\left(1-\frac{k}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus},

\displaystyle J_{3,s}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{g_{s}(m_{\boxplus})}{m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus},

\displaystyle J_{4,ij}=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{(m_{\boxplus}+1-\ell_{i}(m_{\boxplus}))g_{j}(m_{\boxplus})}{2m_{\boxplus}^{3}}\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus}.

Note that the contour
\gamma_{m}
of
m_{\boxplus}
will not enclose the pole
0
more than once when
z
goes through
\gamma
. This is guaranteed by the fact that
\Im m_{\boxplus}
has the same sign as
\Im z
.
When
|z|
is sufficiently large,
|m_{\boxplus}|
is sufficiently small, and thus the only pole inside
{\gamma_{m}}
is
m_{\boxplus}=0
. Hence, by the residue theorem, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}y_{t}(1-y_{t}),\quad I_{3,s}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{k}y_{t}(1-y_{t}),

\displaystyle J_{1}=-\left(1-\frac{k}{2}\right)\sum_{t=1}^{k}y_{t}(1-y_{t}),\quad J_{3,s}=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{k}y_{t}(1-y_{t}),

\displaystyle I_{2,s}=I_{4,ij}=J_{2,s}=J_{4,ij}=0,

Plugging the above results into (
266
) yields

(267)

\displaystyle a_{x^{2}}=0.

According to (
19
), what remains is the calculation of
N\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}d\mu_{\boxplus}(x)
. For this term, we can compute it using the relation between the moments and the free cumulants
[
48
]
. Let
m_{k}(\mu)
and
c_{k}(\mu)
be the
k
-th moment and
k
-th free cumulant of
\mu
, respectively. Then we have

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mu_{\boxplus})=m_{1}(\mu_{\boxplus}),\quad c_{2}(\mu_{\boxplus})=-m_{1}^{2}(\mu_{\boxplus})+m_{2}(\mu_{\boxplus}).

Also, we have

\displaystyle c_{j}(\mu_{\boxplus})=c_{j}(\mu_{1})+c_{j}(\mu_{2})+\cdots+c_{j}(\mu_{k}).

Hence, we can obtain

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mu_{\boxplus})=c_{1}(\mu_{1})+c_{1}(\mu_{2})+\cdots+c_{1}(\mu_{k})=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{p_{t}}{N},

\displaystyle c_{2}(\mu_{\boxplus})=c_{2}(\mu_{1})+c_{2}(\mu_{2})+\cdots+c_{2}(\mu_{k})=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{p_{t}}{N}\left(1-\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle N\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{2}d\mu_{\boxplus}(x)=Nm_{2}(\mu_{\boxplus})=\sum_{i\neq j}^{k}\frac{p_{i}p_{j}}{N}+\sum_{t=1}^{k}p_{t},

which together with (
267
) gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}{\rm Tr}H^{2}=\sum_{i\neq j}^{k}\frac{p_{i}p_{j}}{N}+\sum_{t=1}^{k}p_{t}.

F.2.2.
Variance for Schott’s statistics:
f(x)=x^{2}

Recall
\sigma_{f}
from (
21
). Denoted by
\omega_{ti}:=\omega_{t}(z_{i}),i=1,2,t\in[\![k]\!]
and
m_{\boxplus i}:=m_{\boxplus}(z_{i}),i=1,2
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{x^{2}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{1}}\oint_{\gamma_{2}}z_{1}^{2}z_{2}^{2}\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2}}{(\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2})^{2}}-\frac{1}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}-(k-1)\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus 1}m^{\prime}_{\boxplus 2}}{(m_{\boxplus 1}-m_{\boxplus 2})^{2}}\bigg]{\rm d}z_{2}{\rm d}z_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:\sum_{t=1}^{k}K_{1,t}+K_{2}+K_{3}

We first consider
K_{2}
,

(268)

\displaystyle K_{2}=\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{1}}\oint_{\gamma_{2}}\frac{z_{1}^{2}z_{2}^{2}}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}{\rm d}z_{2}{\rm d}z_{1}=0.

For
K_{1,t}
, using (
259
) and (
260
), we have

\displaystyle K_{1,t}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}z_{1}^{2}z_{2}^{2}\frac{\omega^{\prime}_{t1}\omega^{\prime}_{t2}}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{\prime}m_{\boxplus 2}^{\prime}(\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2})^{2}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{z_{1}^{2}z_{2}^{2}\left(1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})\right)\left(1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus 2}\right)}{\left(m_{\boxplus 1}\left(1+\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 2})\right)-m_{\boxplus 2}\left(1+\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})\right)\right)^{2}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1},

where
g_{t}
and
\ell_{t}
are defined in (
265
), and
\gamma_{mi}
is the contour of
m_{\boxplus i}
after change of variable. For the inner integral,

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}z_{1}^{2}\frac{1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus 2})}{\left(m_{\boxplus 2}\left(1+\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})\right)-m_{\boxplus 1}\left(1+\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 2})\right)\right)^{2}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}

\displaystyle=\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{\left(1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus 2})\right)\left(-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus 2}+1-\ell_{s}(m_{\boxplus 2})}{2m_{\boxplus 2}}\right)^{2}}{\left(m_{\boxplus 2}\left(1+\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})\right)-m_{\boxplus 1}\left(1+\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 2})\right)\right)^{2}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}=:\hat{K}_{1,t}(m_{\boxplus 1})

Setting
|z_{2}|
sufficiently large, which makes
m_{\boxplus 2}=0
be the only singular point inside
\gamma_{m2}
, by the residue theorem, we can check via tedious but elementary calculation

\displaystyle\hat{K}_{1,t}(m_{\boxplus 1})=2\pi\mathrm{i}\left(\frac{2y_{t}-1-2y}{2m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}+\frac{1+\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2m_{\boxplus 1}^{3}}\right).

Plugging it into
K_{1,t}
, we have

\displaystyle K_{1,t}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}z_{2}^{2}\left(1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})\right)\hat{K}_{1,t}(m_{\boxplus 1}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}z_{2}^{2}\left(\frac{2y_{t}-1-2y}{2m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}+\frac{1+\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2m_{\boxplus 1}^{3}}\right)\left(1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\left(-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}+1-\ell_{s}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2m_{\boxplus 1}}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2y_{t}-1-2y}{2m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}+\frac{1+\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2m_{\boxplus 1}^{3}}\right)\left(1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

Performing residue calculations again and summing over
t
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}K_{1,t}=

\displaystyle 44\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}-72\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{3}+36\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{4}-8y-48k\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}+80k\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{3}-40k\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{4}

\displaystyle+8ky+48\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}y-32\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{3}y+4k\left(\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}\right)^{2}+20ky^{2}+8ky^{3}+8\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}y^{2}

(269)

\displaystyle-16y^{2}-8y^{3}-8k\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}y^{2}-56k\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}y+32k\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{3}y.

Finally, we calculate
K_{3}
. First we rewrite

\displaystyle K_{3}=\frac{(k-1)}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{z_{1}^{2}z_{2}^{2}}{(m_{\boxplus 2}-m_{\boxplus 1})^{2}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}.

For the inner integral, we have

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{z_{2}^{2}}{(m_{\boxplus 2}-m_{\boxplus 1})^{2}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}=

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{1}{(m_{\boxplus 2}-m_{\boxplus 1})^{2}}\left(-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus 2}+1-\ell_{s}(m_{\boxplus 2})}{2m_{\boxplus 2}}\right)^{2}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}.

Performing residue calculation, we get

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{z_{2}^{2}}{(m_{\boxplus 2}-m_{\boxplus 1})^{2}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}=2\pi\mathrm{i}\left(\frac{2}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{3}}-\frac{2y}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle K_{3}=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-k)}{\pi i}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}z_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{2}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{3}}-\frac{2y}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-k)}{\pi i}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\left(\frac{2}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{3}}-\frac{2y}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}\right)\left(-\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}+\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}+1-\ell_{s}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2m_{\boxplus 1}}\right)^{2}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}.

Again, by residue theorem, we obtain

\displaystyle K_{3}=

\displaystyle(1-k)\Big(80\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{3}-48\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}-40\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{4}+8y

(270)

\displaystyle-48\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}y+32\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{3}y-8\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}y^{2}+4\left(\sum_{s=1}^{k}y_{s}^{2}\right)^{2}+20y^{2}+8y^{3}\Big).

Combining (
268
), (
269
) and (
270
), we get

\displaystyle\sigma_{x^{2}}=4\sum_{i\neq j}y_{i}y_{j}(1-y_{i})(1-y_{j}).

This completes the calculation for Schott’s statistics.

F.3.
Proof of Corollary
1.15

F.3.1.
Expectation for Wilks’ statistics:
f(x)=\log(x)

Recall
a_{f}
from (
20
) and the contour
\gamma^{0}_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}^{0}
defined in (
18
). Let
\gamma^{0}_{mi}
and
\gamma^{0}_{wi}
be the corresponding contour of
m_{\boxplus}
and
\omega_{t}
respectively. We have

\displaystyle N\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log(x)d\mu_{\boxplus}+a_{\log(x)}=-\frac{N}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma^{0}_{1}}\log(z)m_{\boxplus}(z){\rm d}z-\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma^{0}_{1}}\log(z){\rm d}\;{\log\left(\frac{\prod_{t=1}^{k}\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{[(-1/m_{\boxplus})^{\prime}]^{(k-1)}}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{N}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}^{0}}\log(z(m_{\boxplus}))\frac{m_{\boxplus}}{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus}-\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma^{0}_{1}}\log(z(m_{\boxplus})){\rm d}\;{\log\left(\frac{\prod_{t=1}^{k}\omega_{t}^{\prime}}{[(-1/m_{\boxplus})^{\prime}]^{(k-1)}}\right)}=:NL_{1}+L_{2}.

Recall the definition of
g_{t}
and
\ell_{t}
in (
265
). Further using (
261
) and (
11
), we have

\displaystyle L_{1}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}^{0}}\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\frac{m_{\boxplus}}{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus}+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}^{0}}\log\left(-m_{\boxplus}\right)m_{\boxplus}{\rm d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}^{0}}\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\frac{m_{\boxplus}}{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus}+\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma^{0}_{w1}}\log\left(-m_{\boxplus}\right)m_{\boxplus}{\rm d}\omega_{t}

(271)

\displaystyle+\frac{k-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}^{0}}\frac{\log\left(-m_{\boxplus}\right)}{m_{\boxplus}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus}=:L_{1,1}+\sum_{t=1}^{k}L_{1,2t}+L_{1,3}.

We first have
L_{1,3}=0
since the contour
\gamma_{m}
doesn’t enclose
0
, and the integrand is analytic inside the contour. Then we consider
L_{1,1}
. Let

\displaystyle h_{1}(m_{\boxplus}):=

\displaystyle\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m+1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\frac{m_{\boxplus}}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right)\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus}+1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right).

We have by the residue at infinity,

\displaystyle L_{1,1}=\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{1}(m_{\boxplus}),\infty\right)+\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{1}(m_{\boxplus}),0\right)=-\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}h_{1}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\right),0\right)+\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{1}(m_{\boxplus}),0\right).

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}h_{1}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\right),0\right)=(1-y)\log(1-y),\quad\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{1}(m_{\boxplus}),0\right)=0.

Therefore,

(272)

\displaystyle L_{1,1}=-(1-y)\log(1-y).

For
L_{1,2t}
, we have

\displaystyle L_{1,2t}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(-m_{\boxplus}\right)\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(-m_{\boxplus}\right)\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}}-\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}\right)\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(-m_{\boxplus}\right)\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}

For the first integral, we can compute it based on the contour of
\omega_{t}
. By the previous analysis, we know that as
|z|\to 0
,
|\omega_{t}|\to 0
, and as
|z|\to\infty
,
|\omega_{t}|\to\infty
. Therefore, the contour of
\omega_{t}
will enclose two branch points of the
\log
term,
\omega_{t}=1
and
\omega_{t}=1-y_{t}
, but not
0
. Thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}}-\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}\right)\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(\frac{1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}\right)\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}d\omega_{t}-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{t}}\right)\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(\frac{1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}\right)\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}.

Here in last step, the second term in
L_{1,2t}
equals to 0 since it only has one pole
\omega_{t}=1
inside the contour, and the residue at this pole is 0.
By the residue at infinity, the remaining term can be calculated as follows. Let

\displaystyle h_{2}(\omega_{t})=\log\left(\frac{1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}\right)\frac{y_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}},

then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma}h_{2}(\omega_{t}){\rm d}\omega_{t}=\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{t}^{2}}h_{2}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{t}}\right),0\right)=0.

As a result,

\displaystyle L_{1,2t}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(-m_{\boxplus}\right)\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(\frac{1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}\right)\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}\log\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{t}}\right)\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}}{\rm d}\omega_{t}

The second term vanished since the integrand has antiderivative

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\log(\omega_{t})\right)^{2},

which is single valued along the contour. For the first term, let

\displaystyle h_{3}(\omega_{t})=\log\left(\frac{1-y_{t}-\omega_{t}}{1-\omega_{t}}\right)\frac{1-y_{t}}{\omega_{t}},

we have by the residue at infinity,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{w}}h_{3}(\omega_{t}){\rm d}\omega_{t}=

\displaystyle-\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{t}^{2}}h_{3}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{t}}\right),0\right)+\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{3}(\omega_{t}),0\right)=(1-y_{t})\log(1-y_{t}),

which gives

(273)

\displaystyle L_{1,2t}=(1-y_{t})\log(1-y_{t}).

Plugging (
272
) and (
273
) into (
271
), we have

\displaystyle L_{1}=-(1-y)\log(1-y)+\sum_{t=1}^{k}(1-y_{t})\log(1-y_{t}).

Next, we calculate
L_{2}
. We rewrite

\displaystyle L_{2}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma^{0}_{1}}\log(z(m_{\boxplus})){\rm d}\;{\log\left(\prod_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}m_{\boxplus}^{2}}{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}\right)}-\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma^{0}_{1}}\log(z(m_{\boxplus})){\rm d}\;{\log\left(\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma^{0}_{1}}\log(z(m_{\boxplus})){\rm d}\;\log\left(\frac{\omega_{t}^{\prime}m_{\boxplus}^{2}}{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus}}\right)-\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma^{0}_{1}}\log(z(m_{\boxplus})){\rm d}\;{\log\left(\frac{m_{\boxplus}^{\prime}}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}\right)}=:\sum_{t=1}^{k}L_{2,1t}+L_{2,2}.

For
L_{2,1t}
, we have by (
260
),

\displaystyle L_{2,1t}=\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus})}{z(m_{\boxplus})}\log\left(\frac{1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus}.

Let

\displaystyle h_{4}(m_{\boxplus})=\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus})}{z(m_{\boxplus})}\log\left(\frac{1+g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right),

then by the residue at infinity, we have

\displaystyle L_{2,1t}=\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}h_{4}(m_{\boxplus}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus}=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}h_{4}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\right),0\right)-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{4}(m_{\boxplus}),0\right).

By direct calculation, we can get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}h_{4}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\right),0\right)=-\log(1-y_{t}),\quad\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{4}(m_{\boxplus}),0\right)=0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle L_{2,1t}=-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-y_{t}).

For
L_{2,2}
, we have by (
262
),

\displaystyle L_{2,2}=-\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus})}{z(m_{\boxplus})}\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus}.

Let

\displaystyle h_{5}(m_{\boxplus})=\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus})}{z(m_{\boxplus})}\log\left(1-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{1-g_{t}(m_{\boxplus})}{2}\right),

the residue at infinity, we have

\displaystyle L_{2,2}=-\frac{1}{4\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{m}}h_{5}(m_{\boxplus}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus}=-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}h_{5}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\right),0\right)+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{5}(m_{\boxplus}),0\right).

By direct calculation, we can get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}^{2}}h_{5}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus}}\right),0\right)=-\log(1-y),\quad\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{5}(m_{\boxplus}),0\right)=0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle L_{2,2}=\frac{1}{2}\log(1-y).

Combining the above results, we can obtain

\displaystyle L_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\log(1-y)-\frac{1}{2}\log(1-y_{t}).

Then we have the final result of the expectation of the Wilks’ statistics, which is

\displaystyle N\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log(x){\rm d}\mu_{\boxplus}+a_{\log(x)}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left(N-p_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\log(1-y_{t})-\left(N-Ny-\frac{1}{2}\right)\log(1-y).

F.3.2.
Variance for Wilks’ statistics:
f(x)=\log(x)

Using the same notation as the case
f(x)=x^{2}
, recall that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\log(x)}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{1}}\oint_{\gamma_{2}}\log(z_{1})\log(z_{2})\bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2}}{(\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2})^{2}}-\frac{1}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}-(k-1)\frac{m^{\prime}_{\boxplus 1}m^{\prime}_{\boxplus 2}}{(m_{\boxplus 1}-m_{\boxplus 2})^{2}}\bigg]{\rm d}z_{2}{\rm d}z_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle:\sum_{t=1}^{k}K_{1,t}+K_{2}+K_{3}

For our analysis, it is convenient to take
\gamma_{m1}
and
\gamma_{m2}
be the contour of
m_{\boxplus 1}
and
m_{\boxplus 2}
, respectively. For Wilks’ statistics, we only care about the positive eigenvalues of
H
. Therefore, the contour of
z
should enclose those positive eigenvalues but not
0
. In other words, the contours of
m_{\boxplus 1}
and
m_{\boxplus 2}
should not enclose
0
. In addition, by choosing the parameters in
\gamma_{i}
’s suitably, we can have that
\gamma_{m1}
encloses
\gamma_{m2}
. Further, in this section, we view
z
and
\omega_{t}
be functions of
m_{\boxplus}
, and all the derivatives are taken w.r.t
m_{\boxplus}
.

We first consider
K_{3}
,

\displaystyle K_{3}=\frac{k-1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1}))\log(z(m_{\boxplus 2}))}{(m_{\boxplus 1}-m_{\boxplus 2})^{2}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}.

For the inner integral, we have

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{\log(z(m_{\boxplus 2}))}{(m_{\boxplus 1}-m_{\boxplus 2})^{2}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}=\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 2})}{z(m_{\boxplus 2})(m_{\boxplus 2}-m_{\boxplus 1})}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}.

Let

\displaystyle h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2})=\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 2})}{z(m_{\boxplus 2})(m_{\boxplus 2}-m_{\boxplus 1})}

be the integrand of the contour integral. Instead of calculating the residue inside
\gamma_{m2}
, again we turn to compute the residue at infinity, to simplify the calculation. Since
\gamma_{m2}
does not enclose
0
and
\gamma_{m1}
encloses
\gamma_{m2}
, we have

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}=

\displaystyle-2\pi\mathrm{i}\left(\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2}),\infty\right)+\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2}),m_{\boxplus 1}\right)+\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2}),0\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi\mathrm{i}\left(\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}^{2}}h_{6}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}}\right),0\right)-\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2}),m_{\boxplus 1}\right)-\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2}),0\right)\right)

Using (
261
), by direct calculation, we can get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}^{2}}h_{6}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}}\right),0\right)=0,\quad\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2}),m_{\boxplus 1}\right)=\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})},\quad\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2}),0\right)=\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}h_{6}(m_{\boxplus 2}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}=-2\pi\mathrm{i}\left(\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})}+\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\right).

Then we can calculate the outer integral,

\displaystyle K_{3}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k-1)\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1}))\left(\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})}+\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k-1)\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\log\left(-1+\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}+1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2}\right)\left(\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})}+\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

\displaystyle+\frac{(k-1)\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\log\left(m_{\boxplus 1}\right)\left(\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})}+\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})}+\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}=\left(\log\left(-1+\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}+1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2}\right)\right)^{\prime},

then first integral is zero because the integrand has antiderivative

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\log\left(-1+\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}+1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2}\right)\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1})m_{\boxplus 1}))^{2},

which is analytic along the contour. For the second integral, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(k-1)\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\log\left(m_{\boxplus 1}\right)\left(\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})}+\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-k)\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\log\left(-1+\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}+1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

Let

\displaystyle h_{7}(m_{\boxplus 1}):=\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\log\left(-1+\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}+1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2}\right).

Applying the technique of residue at infinity, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(1-k)\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}h_{7}(m_{\boxplus 1}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}=2(k-1)\left(\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}h_{7}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\right),0\right)-\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{7}(m_{\boxplus 1}),0\right)\right)

By direct calculation, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}h_{7}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\right),0\right)=\log(-1+y),\quad\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{7}(m_{\boxplus 1}),0\right)=\log(-1).

Therefore,

\displaystyle K_{3}=2(k-1)\log(1-y).

Next, we calculate
K_{1,t}
,

\displaystyle K_{1,t}=-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{\log(z(m_{\boxplus 2}))\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1}))}{(\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2})^{2}}{\rm d}\omega_{t2}{\rm d}\omega_{t1}

For the inner inegral,

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma_{w2}}\frac{\log(z(m_{\boxplus 2}))}{(\omega_{t1}-\omega_{t2})^{2}}{\rm d}\omega_{t2}=

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma_{m2}}\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 2})}{z(m_{\boxplus 2})(\omega_{t2}-\omega_{t1})}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}.

Let

\displaystyle h_{8}(m_{\boxplus 2})=\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 2})}{z(m_{\boxplus 2})(\omega_{t2}-\omega_{t1})}

be the integrand. We have

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma}h_{8}(m_{\boxplus 2}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 2}=2\pi\mathrm{i}\left(\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}^{2}}h_{8}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}}\right),0\right)-\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{8}(m_{\boxplus 2}),m_{\boxplus 1}\right)-\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{8}(m_{\boxplus 2}),0\right)\right).

Using (
259
) and (
261
), by direct calculation, we can get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}^{2}}h_{8}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 2}}\right),0\right)=\frac{1}{\omega_{t1}},\quad\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{8}(m_{\boxplus 2}),m_{\boxplus 1}\right)=\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})\omega^{\prime}_{t1}},\quad\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{8}(m_{\boxplus 2}),0\right)=0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\oint_{\gamma}g_{3}(m_{\boxplus 1}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}=2\pi\mathrm{i}\left(\frac{1}{\omega_{t1}}-\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})\omega^{\prime}_{t1}}\right).

Then we can calculate the outer integral,

\displaystyle K_{1,t}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{w1}}\frac{\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1}))}{\omega_{t1}}{\rm d}\omega_{t1}+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1}))\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}

The second integral is zero because the integrand has antiderivative

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1}))\right)^{2},

which is single valued along the contour. For the first integral, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{w1}}\frac{\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1}))}{\omega_{t1}}{\rm d}\omega_{t1}=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1})){\rm d}\log(\omega_{t1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})}\log\left(\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}-1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2}\right){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}\frac{\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1}))}{m_{\boxplus 1}}{\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}.

These two integral can be calculated using residue at infinity now. Let

\displaystyle h_{9}(m_{\boxplus 1})=\frac{z^{\prime}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{z(m_{\boxplus 1})}\log\left(\frac{m_{\boxplus 1}-1-\ell_{t}(m_{\boxplus 1})}{2}\right),\quad h_{10}(m_{\boxplus 1})=\frac{\log(z(m_{\boxplus 1}))}{m_{\boxplus 1}}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}h_{9}(m_{\boxplus 1}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}=-2\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}h_{9}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\right),0\right)+2\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{9}(m_{\boxplus 1}),0\right).

By direct calculation, we can get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}^{2}}h_{9}\left(\frac{1}{m_{\boxplus 1}}\right),0\right)=-\log(-1+y_{t}),\quad\mathrm{Res}\left(h_{9}(m_{\boxplus 1}),0\right)=-\log(-1).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}h_{9}(m_{\boxplus 1}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}=2\log(1-y_{t}).

The calculation of integral of
g_{5}(m_{\boxplus 1})
is identical to
g_{2}(m_{\boxplus 1})
, thus we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi}\oint_{\gamma_{m1}}h_{10}(m_{\boxplus 1}){\rm d}m_{\boxplus 1}=-2\log(1+y).

As a result,

\displaystyle K_{1,t}=2\log(1-y_{t})-2\log(1+y).

Combining the results of
K_{1,t}
and
K_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\log(x)}=-2\log(1+y)+2\sum_{t=1}^{k}\log(1-y_{t}).

Appendix G
Proof of Theorem
1.17

The proof of Theorem
1.17
is also based on Theorem
5.3
. We need the following Lemmas to replace the stochastic quantities by the deterministic quantities.

Lemma G.1
.

Let
{p_{\max}}\leq N^{1/2-\epsilon}
for any given (small) constant
\epsilon>0
. If
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any fixed
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(274)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}{G}(z)-m_{y}(z)\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{\max}}{N}\right),

(275)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z)-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right),\quad t\in[\![k]\!],

(276)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z)-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right),\quad t\in[\![k]\!].

The same bounds hold for
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

Proof of Lemma
G.1
.

The proof of (
274
) is identical to the proof in Section
E.2
. All the estimates remain valid if
\|G(z)\|=O_{\prec}(1)
which is guaranteed by Lemma
4.2
. Hence, we omit the details.

Next, we prove (
275
) based on (
274
) and (
218
)-(
220
). Recall (
220
), we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)=

\displaystyle({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z))({\rm tr}G(z)-{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z))+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z)){\rm tr}G(z)+O_{\prec}\left(\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)^{2}\vee\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(y_{t}-{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)\right)m_{y}(z)+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right)

Solving the above equation for
{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)
, we can obtain (
275
). Using (
275
) together with (
219
), we have (
276
). This completes the proof of Lemma
G.1
.
∎

Lemma G.2
.

Let
{p_{\max}}\leq N^{1/2-\epsilon}
for any given (small) constant
\epsilon>0
. If
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any fixed
z_{1}\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}
and
z_{2}\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(277)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}{P}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z_{1})m_{y}(z_{2})}{(1+m_{y}(z_{1}))(1+m_{y}(z_{2}))}\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right),

(278)

\displaystyle\left|{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}(z_{1}){P}_{t}{G}(z_{2})-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z_{1})m_{y}(z_{2})}{(1+m_{y}(z_{1}))(1+m_{y}(z_{2}))}\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right).

The same bounds hold for
z_{1}\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}
and
z_{2}\in(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

Proof of Lemma
G.2
.

The proof is similar to the proof of Proposition
5.1
. We start with (
151
), replacing
{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})
by
{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})
and then absorbing the
O_{\prec}(p_{t}^{2}/N^{2})
terms into the error, we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})+({\rm tr}P_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})){\rm tr}G(z_{2})=O_{\prec}\left(\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N}\right)^{2}\vee\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Using Lemma
G.1
to replace the stochastic quantities into deterministic quantities we obtain (
277
). Then (
278
) follows immediately by the second estimates of (
151
). This completes the proof of Lemma
G.2
.
∎

In addition to the concentration results for the tracial quantities, we also have the following concentration results for the diagonal entries of the random matrices.

Lemma G.3
.

Let
{p_{\max}}\leq N^{1/2-\epsilon}
for any given (small) constant
\epsilon>0
. If
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
, for any fixed
z\in(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}^{0}_{2})^{+}
, we have

(279)

\displaystyle\left|[G(z)]_{ii}-m_{y}(z)\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),

(280)

\displaystyle\left|[{P}_{t}{G}(z)]_{ii}-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),\quad t\in[\![k]\!],

(281)

\displaystyle\left|[{P}_{t}{G}(z){P}_{t}]_{ii}-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),\quad t\in[\![k]\!],

(282)

\displaystyle\left|[{W}_{t}{G}(z){W}^{\prime}_{t}]_{jj}-\frac{m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),\quad t\in[\![k]\!].

The same bounds hold for
z\in(\bar{\gamma}_{1})^{+}\cup(\bar{\gamma}_{2})^{+}
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
.

Proof of Lemma
G.3
.

By Propositions
4.3
and
5.2
, we can obtain

\displaystyle|G_{ii}(z)-{\rm tr}G(z)|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

Replacing
{\rm tr}G(z)
by Lemma
G.1
, together with the condition that
p_{\max}\leq N^{1/2-\epsilon}
for some small
\epsilon>0
, we can obtain (
279
). Similarly, we can prove (
280
), (
281
) and (
282
). This concludes the proof of Lemma
G.3
.
∎

With the aid of these lemmas, we begin the proof of Theorem
1.17
.
Recall that

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}(z)={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z)],\qquad\beta_{t}(z)=\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm tr}G(z)-{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)].

Using Lemma
G.1
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}(z)=\frac{y_{t}}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right),

and similarly,

\displaystyle\beta_{t}(z)=\frac{m_{y}(z)}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{t}(z)}{1+\alpha_{t}(z)+\beta_{t}(z)}=

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{y_{t}}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}}{\frac{1}{1-y_{t}}+\frac{m_{y}(z)}{1-y_{t}}-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{y_{t}}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right),

where we used Taylor expansion and then ignored all the
O_{\prec}(p_{t}^{2}/N^{2})
terms.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left(z_{1}-\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{\alpha_{s}(z_{1})}{1+\alpha_{s}(z_{1})+\beta_{s}(z_{1})}\right)^{-1}=\left(z_{1}-\frac{y}{(1+m_{y}(z_{1}))^{2}}\right)^{-1}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)

Using the quadratic equation of
m_{y}(z_{1})
(c.f. (
249
)), we can get

(283)

\displaystyle\left(z_{1}-\frac{y}{(1+m_{y}(z_{1}))^{2}}\right)^{-1}=-\frac{m^{\prime}_{y}(z_{1})}{m_{y}(z_{1})}.

Also, by Lemma
G.2
, we have,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[\partial_{z_{2}}\left({\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})\right)\right]=\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z_{1})m_{y}(z_{2})}{(1+m_{y}(z_{1}))(1+m_{y}(z_{2}))}\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right).

Therefore,

(284)

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[\partial_{z_{2}}\left({\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})P_{t}G(z_{2})\right)\right]}{1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})}=\frac{\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z_{1})m_{y}(z_{2})}{(1+m_{y}(z_{1}))(1+m_{y}(z_{2}))}\right)}{1+m_{y}(z_{1})}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right).

Similarly, we can obtain

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[\partial_{z_{2}}\left({\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})G(z_{2})\right)\right]}{1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})}=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\frac{{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})}{z_{1}-z_{2}}-\frac{{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{2})}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)\right]}{1+\alpha_{t}(z_{1})+\beta_{t}(z_{1})}

(285)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\frac{\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z_{1})}{1+m_{y}(z_{1})}-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z_{2})}{1+m_{y}(z_{2})}}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)}{1+m_{y}(z_{1})}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N^{2}}\right).

Combining (
283
), (
284
) and (
G
), we can get

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{m^{\prime}_{y}(z_{1})}{m_{y}(z_{1})}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z_{1})m_{y}(z_{2})}{(1+m_{y}(z_{1}))(1+m_{y}(z_{2}))}\right)}{1+m_{y}(z_{1})}-\frac{m^{\prime}_{y}(z_{1})}{m_{y}(z_{1})}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{\partial_{z_{2}}\left(\frac{\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z_{1})}{1+m_{y}(z_{1})}-\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z_{2})}{1+m_{y}(z_{2})}}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\right)}{1+m_{y}(z_{1})}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

By some further calculation, we can simplify the above expression,

(286)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{m^{\prime}_{y}(z_{1})m^{\prime}_{y}(z_{2})}{(m_{y}(z_{1})-m_{y}(z_{2}))^{2}}-\frac{1}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{2}}-\frac{ym^{\prime}_{y}(z_{1})m^{\prime}_{y}(z_{2})}{(1+m_{y}(z_{1}))^{2}(1+m_{y}(z_{2}))^{2}}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

Adding the parts that
|\Im z|\leq N^{-K}
of the contours back to the integral of the main term in the RHS of (
286
) (with negligible error) gives the variance in Theorem
1.17
.

For the expectation, we can calculate it as follows. Let

(287)

\displaystyle E_{t}(z):={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm tr}P_{t}G(z)\right]-\frac{Ny_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)},\quad E(z):={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm tr}G(z)\right]-Nm_{y}(z).

By Lemma
G.1
, we immediately obtain

\displaystyle E_{t}(z)=O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N}}\vee\frac{p_{t}p_{\max}}{N}\right),\quad E(z)=O_{\prec}\left({p_{\max}}\right).

Our task is to give an explicit expression for
E(z)
. In the following estimation, since only one parameter
z
is involved, we use the shorthand notation
G:=G(z)
for brevity.

Following the same procedure as we did in the calculation of expectation in Theorem
1.11
, we start from the cumulant expansion of
{\rm tr}P_{t}G
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm tr}P_{t}G\right]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[\partial_{t,ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\right]+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{3}^{t,j}}{2N^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[\partial^{2}_{t,ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\right]+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{6N^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[\partial^{3}_{t,ji}[W_{t}G]_{ji}\right]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

Here the error term can be bounded similarly to the error estimates of
\mathsf{E}_{a},a=1,2,3
in Lemma
5.4
, we omit the details.
With a slight abuse of notations, we use
J_{t1},J_{t2}
and
J_{t3}
to denote each term, which reads

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm tr}P_{t}G\right]=

\displaystyle:J_{t1}+J_{t2}+J_{t3}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

For
J_{t1}
, similar to (
191
), we have

\displaystyle J_{t1}=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right]{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm Tr}{G}-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right).

For
J_{t2}
, we can apply similar arguments as we did in the calculation of
\mathsf{I}_{t3}
in the proof of Lemma
5.4
, which gives

\displaystyle J_{t2}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

For
J_{t3}
, we only need to consider the terms consisting of four diagonal entries, which reads,

\displaystyle J_{t3}=\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}^{2}\left([{I}-{P}_{t}]_{ii}[({P}_{t}-{I}){G}]_{ii}-[({I}-{P}_{t}){G}]_{ii}^{2}\right)\Big]+O_{\prec}\left({\frac{p_{t}}{N^{2}}}\right).

Here the error term comes from the estimation for the summation of off-diagonal entries, which is identical to the estimation of
\mathsf{I}_{t42}
in the proof of Lemma
5.4
. Combining
J_{t1}
,
J_{t2}
and
J_{t3}
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm Tr}P_{t}G\right]=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right]{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm Tr}{G}-{\rm Tr}{P}_{t}{G}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}{P}_{t}{G}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}{G}^{2}-{\rm tr}Q_{t}G\right]

(288)

\displaystyle+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\Big[[\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}^{2}\left([{I}-{P}_{t}]_{ii}[({P}_{t}-{I}){G}]_{ii}-[({I}-{P}_{t}){G}]_{ii}^{2}\right)\Big]+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

Performing cumulant expansion up to
\kappa_{4}^{t,j}
term on
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}]
and
{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}]
, and then using Lemma
G.1
and Lemma
G.3
to replace the random quantities by deterministic quantities, together with the condition that
{p_{\max}}\leq N^{1/2-\epsilon}
for some small
\epsilon>0
, we have

(289)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm Tr}\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}\right]=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}]}{1-y_{t}}+\frac{y_{t}}{(1-y_{t})^{2}}+\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{N^{2}(1-y_{t})}+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right)},

(290)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\left[{\rm Tr}{Q}_{t}{G}\right]=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}P_{t}GP_{t}]}{1-y_{t}-\frac{1}{N}}-\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}m_{y}(z)}{N^{2}(1-y_{t})^{2}}+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right)},

and

\displaystyle\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}}{{N}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\bigg[[\left({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{-1}]_{jj}^{2}\left([{I}-{P}_{t}]_{ii}[({P}_{t}-{I}){G}]_{ii}-[({I}-{P}_{t}){G}]_{ii}^{2}\right)\bigg]

(291)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{ij}^{(t)}\frac{\kappa_{4}^{t,j}\left(m_{y}(z)+m_{y}^{2}(z)\right)}{N^{2}(1-y_{t})^{2}}+{O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)}.

Plugging (
287
), (
289
), (
290
) and (
G
) into (
G
), after some basic algebra,
\kappa_{4}^{t,j}
terms will be cancelled out, and we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\frac{E_{t}^{2}(z)}{1-y_{t}}-E_{t}(z)\left(1+\frac{m_{y}(z)}{1-y_{t}}+\frac{y_{t}(1-m_{y}(z))}{(1-y_{t})(1+m_{y}(z))}+\frac{E(z)}{N(1-y_{t})}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{Ny_{t}^{2}m_{y}^{2}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}(1-y_{t})}+\frac{y_{t}E(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))(1-y_{t})}-\partial_{z}\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}+\frac{y_{t}m_{y}^{2}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{\frac{7}{6}}}\right).

And we know that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\frac{E_{t}^{2}(z)}{1-y_{t}}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}^{2}{p_{\max}^{2}}}{N^{3}}\right),\qquad\frac{1}{N}\frac{E_{t}(z)E(z)}{1-y_{t}}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}{p_{\max}^{2}}}{N^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle y_{t}E_{t}(z)=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}^{2}{p_{\max}}}{N^{2}}\right),\qquad y_{t}^{2}E(z)=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}^{2}{p_{\max}}}{N^{2}}\right).

Together with the condition that
p_{\max}\leq N^{1/2-\epsilon}
for some small
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle-E_{t}(z)\left(1+m_{y}(z)\right)+\frac{Ny_{t}^{2}m_{y}^{2}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}+\frac{y_{t}E(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))}-\partial_{z}\frac{y_{t}m_{y}(z)}{1+m_{y}(z)}+\frac{y_{t}m_{y}^{2}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{p_{t}}{N^{7/6}}\right).

Dividing the coefficient of
E_{t}
on both sides, and then summing over t, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}E_{t}(z)=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{Ny_{t}^{2}m_{y}^{2}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}+\frac{yE}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}-\partial_{z}\frac{ym_{y}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}+\frac{ym_{y}^{2}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{3}}

Notice that by the resolvent identity, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}E_{t}(z)=zE(z).

Hence,

\displaystyle E(z)=

\displaystyle\left(z-\frac{y}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{Ny_{t}^{2}m_{y}^{2}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}-\partial_{z}\frac{ym_{y}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{2}}+\frac{ym_{y}^{2}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{3}}\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right)

(292)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{Ny_{t}^{2}m_{y}(z)m_{y}^{\prime}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{3}}+\frac{y(m_{y}^{\prime}(z))^{2}}{m_{y}(z)(1+m_{y}(z))^{3}}-\frac{ym_{y}(z)m_{y}^{\prime}(z)}{(1+m_{y}(z))^{3}}+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right).

Adding the parts that
|\Im z|\leq N^{-K}
of the contours back to the integral of the main term in the RHS of (
292
) (with negligible error) gives the expectation in Theorem
1.17
.

Appendix H
Removing the sample mean: Proof of Theorem
1.18

In this section, we discuss the case when sample mean does not equal to
0
. To subtract the sample mean, we first introduce the following projection matrix,

(293)

\displaystyle A:=\mathrm{I}-\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}.

Therefore, the sample matrix becomes
\tilde{X}_{t}:=X_{t}A
. Similarly,
let
