\displaystyle\tilde{P}_{t}:=AX_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A,\quad\tilde{Q}_{t}:=AX_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-2}X_{t}A,\quad\tilde{W}_{t}:=(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A.

We would like to study the LSS of

\displaystyle\tilde{H}:=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\tilde{P}_{t}.

The corresponding Green function is denoted by

\displaystyle\tilde{G}\equiv\tilde{G}(z):=(\tilde{H}-z)^{-1}.

The proof of Theorem
1.18
follows almost the same procedure as the proof of Theorem
1.11
with minor modification. Therefore, we only show the key steps, i.e, (i) how the
N-1
factor shows up, (ii) how we do the cumulant expansion for terms like
[\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}]_{ii}
, which does not have
X_{t}
as its leading factor, (iii) some additional technical treatments.

Notice that for all
t\in[\![k]\!]
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}={\rm tr}X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A,\quad{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}\tilde{P}_{t}={\rm tr}X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A\tilde{G}\tilde{P}_{t},\quad{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}={\rm tr}X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A\tilde{G}A.

Using the recursive moment estimates similar to the proof of Lemma
4.4
, one can show that

(294)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}={\rm tr}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}\left({\rm tr}A-{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),\quad t\in[\![k]\!],

(295)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}\tilde{P}_{t}={\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}A\left({\rm tr}A-{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),\quad t\in[\![k]\!],

(296)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}=\left({\rm tr}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}A\right)\left({\rm tr}\tilde{G}A-{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}A\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),\quad t\in[\![k]\!].

By (
295
) and (
294
) together with the trivial identity
{\rm tr}A=(N-1)/N,{\rm tr}\tilde{P}_{t}=p_{t}/N
, we have for all
t\in[\![k]\!]
,

\displaystyle{\rm tr}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}=\frac{\tilde{y}_{t}}{1-\tilde{y}_{t}}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),\quad{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}A=\frac{1}{(1-\tilde{y}_{t})}\frac{{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{t}GA}{N-1}+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

where
\tilde{y}_{t}:=p_{t}/(N-1)
. Plugging the above two estimates into (
296
), we get

(297)

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}A}{N-1}=\left(\frac{\tilde{y}_{t}}{1-\tilde{y_{t}}}-\frac{1}{(1-\tilde{y}_{t})}\frac{{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}A}{N-1}\right)\left(\frac{{\rm Tr}\tilde{G}A}{N-1}-\frac{{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}A}{N-1}\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).\quad t\in[\![k]\!].

Define the new approximation subordination functions
\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)
as

(298)

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)=z-\frac{\sum_{s\neq t}\frac{{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{s}\tilde{G}A}{N-1}}{\tilde{m}_{N}(z)},\quad t\in[\![k]\!],

where
\tilde{m}_{N}(z):={\rm Tr}\tilde{G}A/(N-1)
. Therefore, by the definition of
\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)
and the identity
\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}A={\rm Tr}\tilde{H}\tilde{G}A={\rm Tr}(\tilde{H}-z)\tilde{G}A+z{\rm Tr}\tilde{G}A=N-1+z{\rm Tr}\tilde{G}A
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)=z-\frac{k-1}{\tilde{m}_{N}(z)}.

Another direct consequence from (
298
) is that we can rewrite
{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}A/(N-1)
as
1+\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)\tilde{m}_{N}(z)
for all
t\in[\![k]\!]
, which together with (
297
) gives

(299)

\displaystyle 1+\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)\tilde{m}_{N}(z)=\left(\frac{\tilde{y}_{t}}{1-\tilde{y_{t}}}-\frac{1+\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)\tilde{m}_{N}(z)}{1-\tilde{y}_{t}}\right)\left(\tilde{m}_{N}(z)-(1+\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)\tilde{m}_{N}(z))\right)+O_{\prec}\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right),\quad t\in[\![k]\!].

We see that the above equation is almost identical to (
43
) in the proof of Proposition
4.3
, but with
y_{t}
,
\omega_{t}^{c}(z)
and
m_{N}(z)
replaced by
\tilde{y}_{t}
,
\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)
and
\tilde{m}_{N}(z)
, respectively. As a result, (
298
) and (
299
) will form a perturbed system of (
35
) with
\mu_{t}\equiv\tilde{\mu}_{t}:=\tilde{y}_{t}\delta_{\{1\}}+(1-\tilde{y}_{t})\delta_{\{0\}},t\in[\![k]\!]
. By the stability analysis as we did in the proof of Proposition
4.3
, we get

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{\omega}_{t}^{c}(z)-\tilde{\omega}_{t}(z)\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),t\in[\![k]\!],

where
\tilde{\omega}_{t}(z)
is the solution of (
35
) with
\mu_{t}\equiv\tilde{\mu}_{t}
. This further implies

\displaystyle\left|\tilde{m}_{N}(z)-\tilde{m}_{\boxplus}(z)\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

where

\tilde{m}_{\boxplus}(z):=m_{\mu_{t}}(\tilde{\omega_{t}}(z)),t\in[\![k]\!]

is the Stieltjes transform of
\tilde{\mu}_{\boxplus}:=\tilde{\mu}_{1}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus\tilde{\mu}_{k}
.

Next, we explain how we do the cumulant expansion for terms like
[\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}]_{ii}
, which does not have
X_{t}
as its leading factor and we cannot use the cyclicity of trace. We take the estimate for
\sum_{t=1}^{k}[\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}]_{ii}
as an example. Since

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}[\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}]_{ii}=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}[X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A\tilde{G}]_{ii}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{t=1}^{k}[\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A\tilde{G}]_{ii}

(300)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}[X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A\tilde{G}]_{ii}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{u=1}^{N}[X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A\tilde{G}]_{ui}

For the first term we can perform cumulant expansion as usual. For the second term, let
M:=\sum_{t=1}^{k}X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t},

we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{N}[X_{t}^{\prime}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{t}A\tilde{G}]_{ji}\Big|\leq\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\sum_{u=1}^{N}|[MA\tilde{G}]_{ui}|^{2}N}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sqrt{[\tilde{G}^{*}AMMA\tilde{G}]_{ii}}\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},

where in the first step we used Cauchy-schwarz, and in the last step we used the fact that
\|M\|,\|\tilde{G}A\|\prec 1
.
Therefore, the second term in (
300
) can be viewed as the error term. Thus, similar to the proof of Lemma
C.2
, we have for any
\alpha_{t}
satisfies
\sup_{t}|\alpha_{t}|<C
,

(301)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\alpha_{t}[\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}]_{ii}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\alpha_{t}({\rm tr}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G})([\tilde{G}A]_{ii}-[\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}]_{ii})+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

We see that from (
301
), we have
[\tilde{G}A]_{ii}
instead of
[\tilde{G}]_{ii}
. This is another technical thing that we have to deal with. Actually,
[\tilde{G}A]_{ii}
and
[\tilde{G}]_{ii}
share the same approximation, up to negligible error. In general, we have the following lemma.

Lemma H.1
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, let
U\in\mathbb{C}^{m\times N}
be a random matrix with
\|U\|\prec 1
. Then we have

\displaystyle|[UA]_{ui}-[U]_{ui}|\prec N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

for any
u\in[\![m]\!]
and
i\in[\![N]\!]
.

Proof.

By the definition of
A
in (
293
), we have

\displaystyle|[UA]_{ui}-[U]_{ui}|=\frac{1}{N}|[U\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}]_{ui}|=\frac{1}{N}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{N}[U]_{ui}\Big|\leq\frac{1}{N}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N}|[U]_{ui}|^{2}N}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sqrt{[UU^{\prime}]_{uu}}\prec\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},

where in the third step we used Cauchy-schwarz, and in the last step we used the condition that
\|U\|\prec 1
.
∎

By Lemma
H.1
together with the fact that
\sum_{t=1}^{k}\alpha_{t}({\rm tr}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G})=O_{\prec}(1)
, (
301
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\alpha_{t}[\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}]_{ii}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\alpha_{t}({\rm tr}(X_{t}AX_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G})([\tilde{G}]_{ii}-[\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}]_{ii})+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right).

This gives
|[\tilde{G}]_{ii}-\tilde{m}_{\boxplus}(z)|\prec N^{-1/2}
, and the other estimates for diagonal entries can be obtain similarly.

The last technical issue we would like to point out is that in the estimation of
\mathsf{I}_{t1}
in (
173
), the term
{\rm tr}Q_{t}G^{2}
is rewritten as
\partial_{z}{\rm tr}Q_{t}G
. Then by Cauchy’s integral formula, we can use the estimate for
{\rm tr}Q_{t}G
to obtain the estimate for
{\rm tr}Q_{t}G^{2}
. However, in the current case, this term becomes
{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}A\tilde{G}
, which prevent us from using the identity
\tilde{G}^{2}=\partial_{z}\tilde{G}
. This issue can be handled by the following lemma.

Lemma H.2
.

For any bounded
\mathcal{W}_{t}\in\mathbb{C}
such that
\sup_{t}|\mathcal{W}_{t}|<C
for some strictly positive constant
C
,
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
such that
\|\tilde{G}(z_{1})\|,\|\tilde{G}(z_{2})\|\prec 1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}(z_{1})A\tilde{G}(z_{2})=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}(z_{1})\tilde{G}(z_{2})+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).

Proof.

By the definition of
A
in (
293
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}(z_{1})A\tilde{G}(z_{2})-\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}(z_{1})\tilde{G}(z_{2})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}(z_{1})\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\tilde{G}(z_{2})

Denoted by
\tilde{M}:=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}\tilde{Q}_{t}
. Similar to (
144
), we have
\|\tilde{M}\|\prec 1
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{t=1}^{k}\mathcal{W}_{t}{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}(z_{1})\mathds{1}\mathds{1}^{\prime}\tilde{G}(z_{2})=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{i,u}^{N}[\tilde{G}(z_{2})\tilde{M}\tilde{G}(z_{1})]_{ui}\leq\frac{1}{N}\|\tilde{G}(z_{2})\tilde{M}\tilde{G}(z_{1})\|\prec\frac{1}{N},

which completes the proof.
∎

The foregoing lemma indicates that the error of dropping the projection
A
after summation over
t
is still negligible. Therefore, we can again use the identity
\tilde{G}^{2}=\partial_{z}\tilde{G}
to transform the estimate for
{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}^{2}
to the estimate for
{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}
. In addition, Lemma
H.2
can be also applied to the estimation of
\mathsf{I}_{t2}
(c.f. (
176
)) , where
{\rm tr}Q_{t}G(z_{1})G(z_{2})
becomes
{\rm tr}\tilde{Q}_{t}\tilde{G}(z_{1})A\tilde{G}(z_{2})
in the current case.

For the estimation of the expectation, we let

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{t}(z):={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}]-(N-1)(1+\tilde{\omega}_{t}(z)\tilde{m}_{\boxplus}(z)),\quad\tilde{E}(z):={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}[{\rm Tr}\tilde{G}A]-(N-1)\tilde{m}_{\boxplus}(z).

Then following the same procedure as the calculation fo the expectation in Theorem
1.11
, we have the approximation for
\tilde{E}(z)
. By the trivial identity
N+z{\rm Tr}\tilde{G}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}=\sum_{t=1}^{k}{\rm Tr}\tilde{P}_{t}\tilde{G}A=N-1+z{\rm Tr}\tilde{G}A
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\big[{\rm Tr}\tilde{G}\big]={\mathbb{E}}^{\chi}\big[{\rm Tr}\tilde{G}A\big]-\frac{1}{z}+O_{\prec}(N^{-D})=(N-1)\tilde{m}_{\boxplus}(z)+\tilde{E}(z)-\frac{1}{z}.

This gives the expectation in Theorem
1.18
.

Appendix I
Relaxing the moment condition: Proof of Theorem
1.20

In this section, we use a Green function comparison argument to relax the moment condition. We only consider the relaxation of Theorem
1.11
, and the others can be done similarly. We start with the following lemma
[
32
]
.

Lemma I.1
.

Fix
m>2
and let
x
be a real random variable, with absolutely continuous law, satisfying

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[x]=0,\quad{\mathbb{E}}[x^{2}]=\sigma^{2},\quad{\mathbb{E}}[|x|^{m}]\leq C_{m},

for some constant
C_{m}>0
. Let
\lambda>2\sigma
. Then there exists a real random variable
y
that satisfies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[y]=0,\quad{\mathbb{E}}[y^{2}]=\sigma^{2},\quad|y|\leq\lambda,\quad\mathbb{P}\left(x\neq y\right)\leq 2C_{m}\lambda^{-m}.

In particular,
{\mathbb{E}}[|y|^{m}]\leq 3C_{m}
. Moreover, if
m>4
and
\sigma=1
, then there exists a real random variable
z
matching the first four moments of
y
, and satisfies
|z|\leq 6C_{m}
.

Now let
H
be the matrix defined in (
4
) under Assumption
1.7
and
1.19
.
By using the foregoing lemma, we can construct the following two versions of
X_{t},t\in[\![k]\!]
. Let
x:=\sqrt{N}X_{t,ij},\lambda:=N^{1/2-\epsilon},C_{m}:=C
, and
\epsilon=\delta/(4(4+\delta))>0
, we can construct a random variable
X_{t,ij}^{(1)}:=N^{-1/2}y
, such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big]=0,\quad{\mathbb{E}}\big[\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big)^{2}\big]={\mathbb{E}}\big[\left(X_{t,ij}\right)^{2}\big],\quad|X_{t,ij}^{(1)}|\leq N^{-\epsilon},\quad\mathbb{P}\left(X_{t,ij}\neq X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\right)\leq 2CN^{-2-\delta/4},

With
X_{t,ij}^{(1)}
we define
H^{(1)}:=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big)^{\prime}\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big)^{\prime}\big)^{-1}X_{t,ij}^{(1)}
. Further using the second part of Lemma
I.1
on
y=\sqrt{N}X_{t,ij}^{(1)}
, we can construct a random variable
X_{t,ij}^{(2)}:=N^{-1/2}z
, and
X_{t,ij}^{(2)}
satisfies Assumption
1.6
. Then we can define
H^{(2)}
analogously. The main idea is due to
\mathbb{P}(X_{t,ij}\neq X_{t,ij}^{(1)})\leq 2CN^{-2-\delta/4}
, we have
\mathbb{P}(H\neq H^{(1)})=O(N^{-\frac{\delta}{4}})
. Hence, we can work with
H^{(1)}
instead of
H
. Then we compare the statistics of
H^{(1)}
with
H^{(2)}
, where the latter satisfies Assumption
1.6
and thus all previous result in this work hold for
H^{(2)}
.

Let

H^{(t,(i)(j))}:=\sum_{s=1}^{k}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))},

where
\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}
is defined entrywise by
\mathcal{X}_{s,uv}^{(t,(i)(j))}:=X_{t,uv}^{(2)}
if
u\leq j,v\leq i
, and
s\leq t
; otherwise,
\mathcal{X}_{s,uv}^{(t,(i)(j))}:=X_{t,uv}^{(1)}
. In particular,
H^{(1,(1)(0))}:=H^{(1)}
,
H^{(k,(N)(p_{k}))}:=H^{(2)}
and
H^{(s,(1)(0))}:=H^{(s-1,(N)(p_{s-1}))}
. The Green function of
H^{(t,(i)(j))}
is denoted by
G^{(t,(i)(j))}\equiv G^{(t,(i)(j))}(z):=(H^{(t,(i)(j))}-z)^{-1}
.

By the rigidity of eigenvalues of sample covariance matrices with bounded support condition
[
59
]
, i.e.,
|X_{t,ij}^{(1)}|,|X_{t,ij}^{(2)}|\leq N^{-\epsilon}
, we have

(302)

\displaystyle c<\lambda_{\min}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big)\leq\lambda_{\max}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big)<C,

for some strictly positive constants
c,C>0
with high probability. Note that the result presented in
[
59
]
only consider the case of
y_{s}\sim 1
. Based on (
302
), we can easily show that (
302
) holds for the case of
0<y_{s}<1
by Cauchy Interlacing Theorem. More specifically, the results of lower rank covariance matrices follow from the one with large rank. Therefore, we still have the following high probability bounds,

(303)

\displaystyle\|G^{(t,(i)(j))}\|,\;\|\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\|,\;\|\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\|\prec 1.

Then, similar to (
97
), we have (with high probability) for suitable contour
\gamma
,

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}f(H^{(t,(i)(j))})=

\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\oint_{\gamma}{\rm Tr}G^{(t,(i)(j))}f(z){\rm d}z=\frac{-1}{2\pi{\rm i}}\oint_{\gamma\setminus\{|\Im z|<N^{-K}\}}{\rm Tr}G^{(t,(i)(j))}f(z){\rm d}z\cdot\Xi^{(t,(i)(j))}+O_{\prec}(N^{-K+1})

\displaystyle=:L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)+O_{\prec}(N^{-K+1}),

here the choice of
\gamma
depends on the test function
f
(c.f. (
18
)). The truncation function
\Xi^{(t,(i)(j))}
is defined as

(304)

\displaystyle\Xi^{(t,(i)(j))}:=\prod_{s=1}^{k}\chi\big({\rm tr}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\big)\chi\big({\rm tr}\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big),

which is used to control
\big\|\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\big\|
and
\big\|\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big\|
crudely but deterministically.

Next we do the comparison on the
L^{(1,(1)(0))}_{N}(f)
(corresponds to
H^{(1)}
) and
L^{(k,(N)(p_{k}))}_{N}(f)
(corresponds to
H^{(2)}
). Let
F=F(x+\mathrm{i}y)
be a complex-valued, smooth, bounded function, with bounded derivatives. Then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L^{(k,(N)(p_{k}))}_{N}(f)\right)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L^{(1,(1)(0))}_{N}(f)\right)\Big]=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big].

Let us focus on the one step difference

(305)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big].

Hereafter, we use the three tuple
(s,u,v)
to locate the entries of
H^{(t,(i)(j))}
, i.e., the
(s,u,v)
entry of
H^{(t,(i)(j))}
is
\mathcal{X}_{s,uv}^{(t,(i)(j))}
. Notice that
H^{(t,(i)(j))}
and
H^{(t,(i)(j-1))}
have only one different entry at the position
(t,i,j)
. With a slight abuse of notation, we use
\partial_{t,ij}
to denote the partial derivative w.r.t the
(t,i,j)
entry. Therefore, we can perform Taylor expansion around
0
at the
(t,i,j)
entry of the two terms in (
305
). We view
F
is a function of
(t,i,j)
entry, and use the notation
F|_{(t,i,j)=x}
to denote the value of
F
with
(t,i,j)
entry being
x
.

\displaystyle F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)

\displaystyle=F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}+X_{t,ij}^{(2)}\cdot\partial_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}

\displaystyle+\frac{\big(X_{t,ij}^{(2)}\big)^{2}}{2}\partial^{2}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}+\frac{\big(X_{t,ij}^{(2)}\big)^{3}}{6}\partial^{3}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}

\displaystyle+\frac{\big(X_{t,ij}^{(2)}\big)^{4}}{24}\partial^{4}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}+\frac{\big(X_{t,ij}^{(2)}\big)^{5}}{120}\partial^{5}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=h_{2}},

where
h_{2}
is a random variable satisfying
|h_{2}|\leq|X_{t,ij}^{(2)}|
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)

\displaystyle=F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}+X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\cdot\partial_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}

\displaystyle+\frac{\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big)^{2}}{2}\partial^{2}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}+\frac{\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big)^{3}}{6}\partial^{3}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}

\displaystyle+\frac{\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big)^{4}}{24}\partial^{4}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}+\frac{\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big)^{5}}{120}\partial^{5}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=h_{1}},

where
h_{1}
is a random variable satisfying
|h_{1}|\leq|X_{t,ij}^{(1)}|
. Since
H^{(t,(i)(j))}
and
H^{(t,(i)(j-1))}
have only one different entry at the position
(t,i,j)
, we have

\partial^{a}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0}=\partial^{a}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=0},\quad a=1,2,3,4.

Together with the fact that the first four moments of
X_{t,ij}^{(1)}
and
X_{t,ij}^{(2)}
are identical (c.f. Lemma
I.1
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big]=\frac{1}{120}\bigg({\mathbb{E}}\left[\big(X_{t,ij}^{(2)}\big)^{5}\partial^{5}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=h_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big)^{5}\partial^{5}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j-1))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=h_{1}}\right]\bigg)=:I^{(2)}+I^{(1)}.

For
I^{(2)}
, we have

\displaystyle|I^{(2)}|\lesssim

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big|X_{t,ij}^{(2)}\big|^{5}\cdot\sup_{|h_{2}|\leq N^{-\epsilon}}\bigg|\partial^{5}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=h_{2}}\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big|X_{t,ij}^{(2)}\big|^{5}\right]{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{|h_{2}|\leq N^{-\epsilon}}\bigg|\partial^{5}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=h_{2}}\bigg|\right]

Let
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{t}^{(t,(i)(j))}:=\mathcal{X}_{t}^{(t,(i)(j))}(h_{2})
be the matrix with
\mathcal{X}_{t,ij}^{(t,(i)(j))}
replaced by
h_{2}
. By a similar argument as Lemma
C.5
, together with the bounds in (
303
), we have the following estimates hold uniformly in
|h_{2}|\leq N^{-\epsilon}
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{t}^{(t,(i)(j))}\|,\;\big\|\big(\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{t}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big(\tilde{\mathcal{X}}_{t}^{(t,(i)(j))}\big)^{\prime}\big)^{-1}\big\|\prec 1.

Therefore, using chain rule with the fact that
F
has bounded derivatives, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{|h_{2}|\leq N^{-\epsilon}}\bigg|\partial^{5}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=h_{2}}\bigg|\prec 1.

Then with the help of the truncation function
\Xi^{(t,(i)(j))}
defined in (
304
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sup_{|h_{2}|\leq N^{-\epsilon}}\bigg|\partial^{5}_{t,ij}F\left(L_{N}^{(t,(i)(j))}(f)\right)\Big|_{(t,i,j)=h_{2}}\bigg|\right]=O_{\prec}(1).

Finally, by the construction of
X_{t,ij}^{(2)}
, we get

\displaystyle|I^{(2)}|\prec N^{-\frac{5}{2}}.

Similarly, for
I^{(1)}
, we have

\displaystyle|I^{(1)}|\prec{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big|X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big|^{5}\right]\leq N^{-\epsilon}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big|X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\big|^{4}\right]=O(N^{-2-\epsilon}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L^{(k,(N)(p_{k}))}_{N}(f)\right)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left(L^{(1,(1)(0))}_{N}(f)\right)\Big]=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}O(N^{-2-\epsilon/2})=O(N^{-\epsilon/2}).

By approximation argument, for
F
in the above class, together with the definition of
L^{(k,(N)(p_{k}))}_{N}(f)
and
L^{(1,(1)(0))}_{N}(f)
, we have

(306)

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left({\rm Tr}f(H^{(1)})\right)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}\big[F\left({\rm Tr}f(H^{(2)})\right)\big]\Big|=O(N^{-\epsilon/2})

The transition from
H^{(1)}
to
H
is immediate, we have

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathbb{E}}\Big[F\left({\rm Tr}f(H^{(1)})\right)\Big]-{\mathbb{E}}\big[F\left({\rm Tr}f(H)\right)\big]\Big|\leq O\big(\mathbb{P}\big(H^{(1)}\neq H\big)\big)=\sum_{t=1}^{k}\sum_{ij}^{(t)}O\big(\mathbb{P}\big(X_{t,ij}^{(1)}\neq X_{t,ij}\big)\big)=O\left(N^{-c/4}\right).

Combining with (
306
), we conclude the proof of Theorem
1.20
.

Appendix J
Discussion on the contours

In this section, we argue that by choosing
\epsilon_{1i}
,
\epsilon_{2i}
,
M_{1i}
and
M_{2i}
,
i=1,2
appropriately, we can always have that
\{m_{\boxplus}(z):z\in\gamma_{1}^{0}\}
and
\{m_{\boxplus}(z):z\in\gamma_{2}^{0}\}
are well separated and the same holds if
(\gamma_{1}^{0},\gamma_{2}^{0})
is replaced by
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})
. We consider the following two cases, contours
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})
with
\hat{y}\in(0,\infty)
and contours
(\gamma_{1}^{0},\gamma_{2}^{0})
with
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
.

The former case is trivial since
|m_{\boxplus}(z)|\sim|z|^{-1}
as
|z|
goes to infinity. Therefore, we can set
M_{11}
and
M_{21}
much greater than
M_{12}
and
M_{22}
such that
|m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})|
and
|m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})|
are of different orders (in
|z|
) if
z_{1}\in\gamma_{1}
and
z_{2}\in\gamma_{2}
.

Next, we consider the case of contours
(\gamma_{1}^{0},\gamma_{2}^{0})
with
\hat{y}\in(0,1)
. To show
\{m_{\boxplus}(z):z\in\gamma_{1}^{0}\}
and
\{m_{\boxplus}(z):z\in\gamma_{2}^{0}\}
are well separated, it suffices to show that for any
z_{1}\in\gamma_{1}^{0}
and
z_{2}\in\gamma_{2}^{0}
, we have
m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})\neq m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})
. We only consider the following cases while the others are trivial.

Case 1: If
z_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21})
and
z_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22})
, by setting
\epsilon_{1a},a=1,2
sufficiently small, we have
|m_{\boxplus}(z_{a})|\sim\epsilon_{1a}^{-1}
(since
\mu_{\boxplus}
has point mass at
0
by Lemma
4.1
). Therefore, we can choose
\epsilon_{11}
much smaller than
\epsilon_{12}
, i.e.
\epsilon_{11}=\epsilon_{12}^{2}
, to ensure that
|m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})|>|m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})|
.

Case 2: If
z_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21})
and
z_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22},M_{12})
, using Lemma
4.1
together with the fact that
\Re z_{2}<0
, we have

|m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})|\leq\int\frac{1}{|\lambda-z_{2}|}{\rm d}\mu_{\boxplus}(\lambda)\leq\frac{1}{|z_{2}|}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{12}}.

Therefore, together with Case 1, we can still obtain
|m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})|>|m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})|
.

Case 3: If
z_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21},M_{11})
and
z_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22},M_{12})
, we compare the imaginary parts of
m_{\boxplus}(z_{a}),a=1,2
. By the definition of the Stieltjes transform, we have

\Im m_{\boxplus}(z_{a})=\int\frac{\Im z_{a}}{|\lambda-\Re z_{a}|^{2}+|\Im z_{a}|^{2}}{\rm d}\mu_{\boxplus}(\lambda),\qquad a=1,2.

Let
\Im z_{a}
be sufficiently small (
\epsilon_{2a}
sufficiently close to
\epsilon_{1a}
) with
\Im z_{2}\geq\frac{2M_{12}^{2}}{\epsilon_{11}^{2}}\Im z_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\Im m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})\geq\frac{2M_{12}^{2}}{\epsilon_{11}^{2}}\int\frac{\Im z_{1}}{|\lambda-\Re z_{2}|^{2}+|\Im z_{2}|^{2}}{\rm d}\mu_{\boxplus}(\lambda)\geq\int\frac{\Im z_{1}}{|z_{1}|^{2}}{\rm d}\mu_{\boxplus}(\lambda)\geq\Im m_{\boxplus}(z_{1}),

where in the last step, we used Lemma
4.1
and the fact that
\Re z_{1}<0
. Therefore, we can conclude that
m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})\neq m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})
in this case.

Case 4: If
z_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21},M_{11})
and
z_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22})\cap\{\Im z\geq\sqrt{\epsilon_{12}^{2}-\epsilon_{22}^{2}}\}
, by Lemma
4.1
, we have there exists a constant
c>0
, such that

\displaystyle\Im m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})\geq c\frac{\Im z_{2}}{|z_{2}|^{2}}=c\frac{\Im z_{2}}{\epsilon_{12}^{2}}=c\frac{\Im z_{2}}{\epsilon_{11}},

where in the last step we used
\epsilon_{11}=\epsilon_{12}^{2}
(Case 1). Therefore, choosing
\Im z_{2}\geq\max\{\frac{2M_{12}^{2}}{\epsilon_{11}^{2}},\frac{1}{c\epsilon_{11}}\}\Im z_{1}
, we arrives at

\displaystyle\Im m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})\geq\frac{\Im z_{1}}{\epsilon_{11}^{2}}=\int\frac{\Im z_{1}}{|z_{1}|^{2}}{\rm d}\mu_{\boxplus}(\lambda)\geq\Im m_{\boxplus}(z_{1}).

This gives
m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})\neq m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})
in this case.

If
z_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{2}(\epsilon_{11},\epsilon_{21},M_{11})
and
z_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}(\epsilon_{12},\epsilon_{22})\cap\{\Im z<\sqrt{\epsilon_{12}^{2}-\epsilon_{22}^{2}}\}
, it is easy to check that
\Re m_{\boxplus}(z_{1})
and
\Re m_{\boxplus}(z_{2})
have different sign, when
\epsilon_{12}
is sufficiently small.

For the other cases, the discussion is similar and simpler, namely, we can always compare either the total sizes of
m_{\boxplus}
’s, or the real parts or imaginary parts of them. Hence, we omit the details.

Appendix K
Discussion on Semicircle Law

In this section, we discuss the case when
y\to\infty
as
N\to\infty
. We first define

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}:=\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{k}(P_{t}-y_{t}I)}{\tilde{y}},\qquad\tilde{y}:=\sqrt{y-\sum_{t}^{k}y_{t}^{2}}.

The corresponding Green function can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(z):=(\mathcal{H}-z)^{-1}.

Further, with
\tilde{z}:=y+z\tilde{y}
, we have

(307)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(z)=\left(\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{k}(P_{t}-y_{t}I)}{\tilde{y}}-z\right)^{-1}=\tilde{y}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{k}P_{t}-\tilde{z}\right)^{-1}=\tilde{y}G(\tilde{z})

By the method of recursive moment estimation, one can show the following estimates

(308)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}{P}_{t}G(\tilde{z}){P}_{t}-(1-y_{t}){\rm tr}{Q}_{t}G(\tilde{z})=O_{\prec}(\frac{1}{\tilde{y}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}})

(309)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}{P}_{t}G(\tilde{z})-({\rm tr}({X}_{t}{X}_{t}^{\prime})^{-1}-{\rm tr}{Q}_{t}G(\tilde{z}))({\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}G(\tilde{z}))=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{y}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right)

Notice that for any fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
, we have
\|G(\tilde{z})\|=\|\mathcal{G}(z)\|/\tilde{y}\sim\tilde{y}^{-1}
. Plugging (
218
) and (
308
) into (
308
), we have

(310)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}{P}_{t}G(\tilde{z})-\frac{y_{t}-{\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z})}{1-y_{t}}({\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})-{\rm tr}{P}_{t}G(\tilde{z}))=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{y}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right).

Multiplying both sides by
(1-y_{t})
, and then summing over
t
, we have

(311)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}HG(\tilde{z})-y{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})-\sum_{t=1}^{k}({\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z}))^{2}+{\rm tr}HG(\tilde{z}){\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})=O_{\prec}\left(\max_{i}\frac{\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{Np_{i}}}\right).

Next, we present an approximation for
\sum_{t=1}^{k}({\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z}))^{2}
. By (
310
), we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z})-y_{t}{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})=({\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z}))^{2}-{\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z}){\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})+O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1-y_{t}}{\tilde{y}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\right)=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1-y_{t}}{\tilde{y}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{t}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{y_{t}}{\tilde{y}^{2}}\right).

Therefore, we can have the following estimates,

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t=1}^{k}({\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z}))^{2}-\sum_{t=1}^{k}y_{t}^{2}{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})\right|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\left|{\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z})-y_{t}{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})\right|\left|{\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z})+y_{t}{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})\right|

\displaystyle\prec

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{k}\frac{y_{t}}{\tilde{y}}\left|{\rm tr}P_{t}G(\tilde{z})-y_{t}{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})\right|=O_{\prec}\left(\tilde{y}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{1}{\tilde{y}}\right).

Plugging the above estimates back into (
311
), we have

\displaystyle{\rm tr}HG(\tilde{z})-y{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})-\sum_{t=1}^{k}y_{t}^{2}{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})+{\rm tr}HG(\tilde{z}){\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})=O_{\prec}\left(\max_{i}\frac{\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{Np_{i}}}\vee\tilde{y}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{1}{\tilde{y}}\right).

Further simplifying the LHS of the above equation using the trivial identity
{\rm tr}HG(\tilde{z})=1+\tilde{z}{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})
together with (
307
), we have

\displaystyle 1+z{\rm tr}\mathcal{G}(z)+({\rm tr}\mathcal{G}(z))^{2}=

\displaystyle-{\rm tr}G(\tilde{z})(1+z{\rm tr}\mathcal{G}(z))+O_{\prec}\left(\max_{i}\frac{\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{Np_{i}}}\vee\tilde{y}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{1}{\tilde{y}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{\prec}\left(\max_{i}\frac{\tilde{y}}{\sqrt{Np_{i}}}\vee\tilde{y}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{p_{\max}}{N^{3}}}\vee\frac{1}{\tilde{y}}\right).

If
k\sim N^{1-\epsilon}
for some
\epsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle 1+z{\rm tr}\mathcal{G}(z)+({\rm tr}\mathcal{G}(z))^{2}=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\epsilon/2}}\vee\frac{1}{\tilde{y}}\right).

Which implies that

\displaystyle|m_{sc}(z)-{\rm tr}\mathcal{G}(z)|=O_{\prec}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\epsilon/2}}\vee\frac{1}{\tilde{y}}\right),

where
m_{sc}(z)
is the Stieltjes transform of the semicircle law. Let
\hat{\mu}_{N}
be the ESD of
\mathcal{H}
. By the continuity theorem of Stieltjes transform, we conclude that
\hat{\mu}_{N}
converges weakly in probability to
\mu_{sc}
, where

\displaystyle\mu_{sc}:=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{(4-x^{2})_{+}}{\rm d}x,

under the assumption that
k\sim N^{1-\epsilon}
for some small
\epsilon>0
,
y\to\infty
as
N\to\infty
, and Assumption
1.6
.
